As with the step-process check above, these `psql` results were read interactively and not redirected to a saved file, so — same caveat as the W0-1 `pg_locks` entry — this claim is not independently re-verifiable from a saved artifact, though it is corroborated by the run's own `updatedAt` timestamp and the absence of any error from the scoped `WHERE`-clause queries.
- **Expected:** Per I3, cancelling a mutex-holding run must release the mutex so a successor job sharing the same `spec.concurrency.mutex` is not starved. Held exactly as expected for this ordinary (no-outage) cancel path.
- **Notes:** This part deliberately did not combine the mutex job with the controller-kill race (kept it cheap per the runbook's own "prefer (i) if it's cheap" framing) — it establishes that the release path itself is correct in isolation, not that it survives a simultaneous controller-kill race. A follow-up that re-runs Race A Part 1's exact cancel-then-kill-within-1s timing against a mutex-holding run (rather than `edge-longrun`, which carries no mutex) would be the natural next step if I3-under-failover specifically (rather than I3-under-ordinary-cancel) is ever in scope; not done here to avoid scope creep beyond what the brief asked for.

## W1-4 — resolved branch-internal asset bug: this campaign branch authored its own mutex payloads with a `spec.mutex` shorthand that has never existed, and they 400'd on first use
- **Classification:** **Not a violation and not an observation — and therefore not counted in the W1 tally below.** It is neither a product defect (no product code or schema is implicated) nor a still-open issue (it was fixed inside this branch, before any scenario consumed the affected asset). Kept as an entry because the lesson is real and reusable: the campaign plan's own worked example contradicted the campaign plan's own "verified API facts" block, and only executing a scenario caught it.
- **Invariant:** N/A — test-asset accuracy, not a runtime invariant.
- **Severity:** n/a (resolved within the branch; see **Resolution** below)
- **Repro (as it stood at `b112b5c`, the commit that introduced the asset):** `curl -X POST localhost:18080/api/v1/jobs ... --data-binary @../edgecase/workloads/mutex-successor.payload.json` against the healthy baseline stack.
- **Observed:** `400` with body `invalid yaml: yaml: unmarshal errors:\n  line 7: field mutex not found in type dsl.Spec`. The task brief's own suggested inline snippet for this scenario (`spec.mutex: edge-mutex`) hits the identical error. Cross-checked against `internal/dsl/types.go:86` (`Mutex string `yaml:"mutex,omitempty"`` lives on the `Concurrency` struct, not `Spec` directly) and `internal/dsl/parse_test.go:198-214` (`TestParse_ConcurrencyMutex`, which nests it as `spec.concurrency.mutex`) — the correct, currently-working shorthand is:
  ```yaml
  spec:
    concurrency:
      mutex: edge-mutex-cancel
  ```
  Using this corrected form, the job applied cleanly (`200`) and the mutex-release probe above (Race A Part 2) proceeded without further issue.
- **Origin — the asset was never valid, and no product schema change is implicated.** An earlier draft of this entry called `mutex-successor.payload.json` a "pre-existing" asset that had gone "stale since at least whatever schema change moved `mutex` under `concurrency`," with git history left uninvestigated. That was wrong on both counts, and the history is cheap to check: `git show b112b5c:test/edgecase/workloads/mutex-successor.payload.json` shows the file was **created by this branch's own Task 2** (`b112b5c`, `test(edgecase): add W1 fault-injection tooling and workloads`) already carrying `spec.mutex`, copied verbatim from the plan's own worked snippets in Task 2 Step 4 (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-29-edge-case-campaign-w1.md`, the `sideeffect` and `mutex-successor` JSON blocks, now at `:166` and `:176` and now carrying the corrected form under a `:169` corrected-after-execution note). Those snippets, as originally written, contradicted the *same plan's* line 19, which states the correct `spec.concurrency.mutex` form under "Verified API facts used throughout (do not re-derive)". So the payload never applied cleanly at any point in its life, and nothing in the product moved: `Mutex` has lived on the `Concurrency` struct (`internal/dsl/types.go:86`) throughout.
- **Resolution:** fixed in commit `b3158ce` (`test(edgecase): fix mutex payloads to spec.concurrency.mutex, ...`), which re-nested `mutex` under `concurrency` in **both** affected payloads — `test/edgecase/workloads/mutex-successor.payload.json` and `test/edgecase/workloads/sideeffect.payload.json`. Verified at HEAD: both files now read `spec.concurrency.mutex: edge-mutex`. The bug was caught before any scenario consumed the broken form — W1-4 Race A Part 2 used a corrected inline job, and W1-5 applied `sideeffect`/`mutex-successor` only after `b3158ce` (both `200`, `w1-5/jobs-apply.txt`). **No action remains.** The plan's two bad snippets were corrected in the same pass as this entry (now `plan:166` and `:176`, with a `:169` note recording the original form), so the source of the copy is closed too.
- **Notes:** The transferable lesson is not "a checked-in asset was stale" (it never was stale — it was born wrong); it is that a plan can state a fact correctly in its facts block and then contradict it in its own worked example a hundred and fifty lines later, and neither review nor `sh -n`-style syntax checks catch that — only executing the asset does. Pair this with carry-forward item 2 below (the revocation-premise correction), which is the same failure mode in the same document. Adjacent, still-live context: this codebase has a known "stale-doc traps" pattern tracked outside this campaign (`needs:` field removed, `hashFile` renamed from `checksum`), which is presumably where the incorrect `spec.mutex` shorthand was picked up from in the first place.

## W1-4 — observation, Race B: cancel attempted during a live outage fails loudly (`502`, nginx-originated, no false success), and a retried cancel after recovery completes normally within one poll interval
- **Invariant:** I1 (run accounting), I5 (bounded recovery)
- **Severity:** minor (observation — confirms designed/expected behavior, no defect)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-4-cancel-vs-failover.md`, Race B. Triggered a fresh `edge-longrun` run `34588d4c-e694-4238-b299-4620ebb638c7` (claimed by `agent1`), confirmed `Running`. Killed all three controllers hard (`06:31:28.650Z`-`06:31:31.400Z`), confirmed `readyz` `502`, then attempted the cancel through the LB while fully down.
- **Observed:**
  - **Cancel-while-down:** `POST /runs/<id>/cancel` at `06:31:38.433Z` returned exactly `HTTP 502` (`curl` exit code `0` — nginx itself answered, it did not time out or refuse the TCP connection) with an nginx-generated `502 Bad Gateway` HTML body (`nginx/1.27.5`), in ~72ms. No false `2xx`, no ambiguous response.
  - **Restart and retry:** controllers restarted `06:31:47.261Z`-`06:31:48.729Z`; `readyz` returned `200` at `06:32:00.766Z` (~12-13.5s restart-to-`readyz`-`200`, at the fast end of the ~12-38s spread seen across restarts of this same compose file — see the carried-forward note in the W1 checkpoint: that span is **not** controller readiness, which the captured controller logs put at ~1-2s, and its cause is unexplained and rig-side). The **identical** cancel `POST` retried at `06:32:08.726Z` returned `204`.
  - **Normal cancellation:** the run's `GET` immediately showed `status:"Cancelled"` with `updatedAt:"2026-07-29T06:32:08.763778Z"` (essentially the retry POST's own timestamp). `agent1`'s cancel-poller had been failing continuously with `http 502` from `06:31:33.409Z` through `06:31:53.409Z` during the outage, then (after a gap covering the restart) logged `{"time":"2026-07-29T06:32:13.413629652Z",...,"msg":"received cancellation signal from master; interrupting run",...}` — detection landed **~4.65s** after the retried cancel committed, inside one `CancelPollInterval` (5s), matching ordinary steady-state cancel latency (no outage was overlapping the actual commit this time, unlike Race A).
- **Expected:** Per I1/I5, a cancel attempted against a fully-down control plane must fail visibly (not silently succeed, not silently corrupt state) and must be safely retriable once the control plane recovers, with the retry behaving like an ordinary cancel. Both held exactly: the down-path attempt produced an unambiguous `502` with no run-side side effect, and the retried cancel after recovery committed and was detected within normal bounds.
- **Notes:** No violation. The faster-than-usual (~13s vs ~26-38s elsewhere in this campaign) restart-to-`readyz`-`200` time after this particular restart is noted but not chased further — nothing in the observed logs pointed to a distinct mechanism, and I1/I5 held regardless of the exact recovery duration. **Do not read that span as controller readiness:** W1-3's and W1-4 Race A's captured controller logs put process boot plus leader election at ~1-2s, so the varying part lives in the LB/observation path, not in the controller — see the carry-forward note at the end of this file.

## W1-5 — a transient 4xx from an intermediary makes the agent permanently abandon step/finish reporting, so a run whose step actually succeeded is permanently recorded `Failed`
- **Invariant:** I7 (state display consistency — run status vs reality) violated; I1 (run accounting — every API-accepted run reaches exactly one terminal state) held throughout, including here — the run does settle on exactly one terminal state, it is simply the wrong one, which is an I7 concern, not an I1 one.
- **Severity:** major
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-5-oneway-partition-zombie.md`, "Addendum — 4xx-permanent abandonment with no reap". Inline job `edge-short` (native, single step `quick`: `sleep 25; echo done-short`), triggered as run `4be51a4f-c1b8-4111-920c-a8029cec71cb` at `12:09:18.34Z`, `claimed_at 12:09:18.721946Z` by `agent1`, then `../edgecase/tools/inject.sh nginx-block agent1` completing at `12:09:19.77Z` — i.e. the partition is in place ~1s into a ~28s step, and healed at `12:10:12.81Z` (`claimed_at`+54s). The run is nonetheless never reaped, and the reason is simpler than "the heal undid it": **the partition was only 53.0s long** (`12:09:19.77Z` block complete -> `12:10:12.81Z` unblock complete, `w1-5/addendum-inject.txt` / `w1-5/addendum-timeline.txt`), against a `staleAfter` of **90s**. `agent1`'s `last_seen_at` therefore never got 90s stale at any point, so `ListStuckRunIDs`'s `a.last_seen_at < NOW() - staleAfter` conjunct (`internal/store/postgres.go:1224`) **never matched on any reaper tick at all** — not "never matched *again*" after the heal. The 60s claim grace (`claimed_at`+60s = `12:10:18.72Z`) did expire, but the grace clause is a conjunct, not a trigger: expiring it only makes a run *eligible* for the staleness test it never came close to failing. Raw evidence in the scratchpad: `w1-5/addendum-inject.txt`, `w1-5/addendum-timeline.txt`, `w1-5/addendum-evidence.txt`, `w1-5/agent1-full.log`, `w1-5/derived-counts.txt`.
- **Observed:** The step ran to completion locally (nothing about `sleep 25; echo done-short` depends on controller reachability), and at exactly step-end the agent abandoned **both** of its reports outright rather than retrying:
  ```
  agent1-1 | {"time":"2026-07-29T12:09:46.758124585Z","level":"ERROR","msg":"permanent error, giving up retry","status":403,"error":"response omitted"}
  agent1-1 | {"time":"2026-07-29T12:09:46.758941554Z","level":"ERROR","msg":"permanent error, giving up retry","status":403,"error":"response omitted"}
  ```
  (`12:09:46.758Z` = `claimed_at` + 28.0s = `sleep 25` + step startup; two lines = the step report and the run `FinishRun`.) Mechanism, code-confirmed: `retryUntilSuccess` (`internal/agent/retry.go:33-36`) classifies **every** `HTTPError` with `StatusCode < 500` as permanent —
  ```go
  if errors.As(err, &httpErr) && httpErr.StatusCode < 500 {
      slog.Error("permanent error, giving up retry", "status", httpErr.StatusCode, "error", httpErr.Body)
      return
  }
  ```
  — so the otherwise-unbounded retry loop that W1-1/W1-2 measured absorbing 5xx outages for tens of seconds (~81s in W1-1's all-controller-restart entry, `FINDINGS.md:164`; ~114s of Postgres downtime in W1-2's hard-kill entry, `FINDINGS.md:204`) gives up on the **first** 4xx and never tries again, including after the partition heals. `grep -n "permanent error" w1-5/agent1-full.log` shows exactly four such lines across this whole scenario (`12:05:30.358`/`.359` for the main probe's run, `12:09:46.758`/`.759` for this one) and nothing after either heal.
  The consequences, all read back after the heal (`w1-5/addendum-evidence.txt`):
  - **Run status is wrong and permanent.** `runs` row: `4be51a4f-... | edge-short | Failed | agent1 | claimed_at 2026-07-29 12:09:18.721946+00 | updated_at 2026-07-29 12:10:31.733876+00`. No stuck-run reaper line mentions this run id (`grep -nE "stuck-run reaper" w1-5/controllers.log` matches only `12:04:48.855796904Z ... runId":"94268ad8-..."`, the main probe's run) — what actually failed it is the **heartbeat reconcile** (`internal/controller/api_agent.go:99-124`): `claimed_at` + `heartbeatReconcileGrace` (60s) = `12:10:18.72Z`, and `agent1`'s heartbeat cadence (15s; the failing series captured in `w1-5/derived-counts.txt` ends `12:10:01.717674087Z`, the last attempt made while still blocked) extrapolated from that series (+15s to the first post-heal success, +15s again to land past the grace boundary) puts its first post-grace heartbeat at `12:10:31.7Z`, matching `updated_at 12:10:31.733876Z` to ~30ms. The healed agent no longer listed the run as active (its goroutine had exited at `12:09:46`), so the controller correctly concluded "orphaned" — from the control plane's point of view there was never any evidence the step succeeded.
  - **The step's output is gone.** `GET /api/v1/runs/4be51a4f-.../logs?after=0` returns `[]` and `/logs/stats` returns `{"count":0,"maxSeq":0,"minSeq":0}` — the `done-short` line was never delivered and no `[N log line(s) dropped: controller unreachable]` marker exists.
  - **Caveat on the success claim, stated precisely:** the step's exit code was never delivered (that is the defect), so "it succeeded" is an inference from (a) the step being deterministic and unable to fail, and (b) the abandoned reports being timestamped at exactly `claimed_at`+28s, which is where a completed `sleep 25` lands. That the true outcome is *unobservable from the control plane* is itself part of the finding.
- **Expected:** Per I7, a run's recorded status must not contradict reality; per I1 a run must reach exactly one terminal state, and the documented failure semantics for an agent that is merely unreachable are the buffer-and-retry-until-success behavior W1-1 and W1-2 measured for 5xx outages (`retryUntilSuccess`, unbounded, doubling to a 30s cap). Treating a 4xx as permanent is defensible for a **controller-originated** 4xx — a `403` from `agentRunGuard` genuinely means "this run is claimed by another agent" (`internal/controller/agent_guard.go:121-128`) and retrying it forever would be pointless. The gap is that the agent cannot tell a controller-originated 4xx from one minted by anything in the network path, and treats both as unrecoverable.
- **Notes — injection-artifact caveat, read this before triaging:** the `403` here came from **nginx's IP deny**, which is how this campaign injects a one-way partition (the agent image has no `iptables` and no `NET_ADMIN`, so a packet-level partition is not injectable). A *real* packet-level partition would surface connect timeouts / transport errors, which are **not** `HTTPError`s and would therefore have been retried normally — so this scenario's specific trigger is an artifact of the mechanism. What is **not** an artifact is the code path: `retry.go:33-36` keys on status code alone, with no notion of who produced it, so any intermediary that transiently answers 4xx puts a run into this state. Realistic production triggers with the same shape: an ingress/WAF/service-mesh ACL briefly denying (`403`), a rate limiter shedding agent reports (`429`), or a `404` window while an ingress route is reconfigured — none of which are the controller saying "give up". Rated **major** rather than minor because the resulting status is *wrong* (not merely late or undiagnosable), *permanent* (no mechanism ever revisits it — the reconcile that settled it is itself acting on the missing report), and *actively misleading*: an operator or downstream automation seeing `Failed` would re-run a job whose side effects have already landed, which is exactly the duplicate-side-effect outcome the stuck-run reaper's own fail-never-requeue design exists to prevent (`internal/controller/stuckrun_reaper.go:72-73`). Not critical: no corruption, no security impact, and the run does still settle on exactly one terminal state. Fix directions, in increasing cost: (a) exclude `408`/`429` and, more importantly, distinguish controller-originated 4xx from intermediary-originated ones (e.g. require a controller-emitted marker header/JSON error body before honoring a 4xx as permanent, and treat a bare/HTML 4xx as retryable); (b) bound rather than abandon — retry 4xx with the same backoff up to a long ceiling; (c) at minimum, make the abandonment loud on the *controller* side too, since today the only trace is a single ERROR line in the agent's own log.
- **See also — W1-6 forward reference:** the addendum probe above has pre-executed the generic half of W1-6 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:79`, "Agent credential revocation while its run executes: 4xx-permanent report abandonment vs run reap"). Both hit the identical `if` at `retry.go:34`, but the two 4xx sources are not the same class: W1-6 Part B's 401 is a **legitimately** permanent controller-originated rejection (the agent's credential really is revoked; retrying forever would be pointless), whereas this entry's 403 is an **illegitimate** intermediary-originated 403 (nginx's IP deny, not the controller saying "give up") — the same abandonment code path, triggered for opposite reasons. When W1-6 is run, triage its Part B result against this distinction rather than re-deriving the `retry.go:34` mechanism from scratch.

## W1-5 — a reaped/orphan-failed run's in-flight `step_reports` row stays `Running` forever, contradicting the run's own terminal status
- **STATUS: FIXED ON MAIN — do not re-triage.** PR #113 (`2c018fe`, "fix(controller): terminalize in-flight steps when reaping an orphaned run") landed independently while this wave was executing and adds `store.MarkRunStepsInterrupted` (`internal/store/postgres.go:810-819`), called from `failOrphanedRun` (`internal/controller/stuckrun_reaper.go:81`) **before** `MarkRunFinished`, turning a reaped run's `Running` step reports into `Failed` with `ended_at` stamped. The W1 stack was built from a branch based on `d2f1030`, which predates that commit, so the behavior recorded below is real for the code under test but no longer holds at HEAD. The corroborating W1-6 observations at the `step_reports` bullets below are covered by the same fix. Entry retained as the campaign's record of the defect and of its independent discovery in production ("a long-running job whose macOS agent went offline mid-step").
- **Invariant:** I7 (state display consistency)
- **Severity:** minor
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-5-oneway-partition-zombie.md`. Observed on **both** runs this scenario failed, by two different mechanisms (stuck-run reaper and heartbeat reconcile), so it is not specific to either path. Raw evidence: `w1-5/db-final.txt`, `w1-5/addendum-evidence.txt`.
- **Observed:** After both runs had settled terminally, `SELECT run_id, step_index, step_name, status, exit_code, started_at, ended_at FROM step_reports` returned:
  ```
                  run_id                | step_index | step_name |  status   | exit_code |          started_at           |  ended_at
  --------------------------------------+------------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-------------------------------+-----------
   94268ad8-50f7-4c27-b49b-454abaf49283 |          0 | append    | Running   |           | 2026-07-29 12:03:29.711167+00 |
   eb130ec5-2c15-4f05-91ec-e2834cf383c9 |          0 | probe     | Succeeded |         0 | 2026-07-29 12:05:09.935462+00 | 2026-07-29 12:05:09.945831+00
   4be51a4f-c1b8-4111-920c-a8029cec71cb |          0 | quick     | Running   |           | 2026-07-29 12:09:18.730681+00 |
  ```
  while the corresponding `runs` rows read `Failed` (`94268ad8` at `12:04:48.850844+00`, `4be51a4f` at `12:10:31.733876+00`). Both step rows still carry `status='Running'`, `exit_code=NULL`, `ended_at=NULL`, indefinitely — nothing in the system ever revisits them. Two independent code paths guarantee this outcome, so it is not a race:
  - `FinishRun` (`internal/store/postgres.go:747-782`), the single funnel used by `MarkRunFinished` and therefore by both `failOrphanedRun` callers, updates `runs`, then `DELETE FROM mutex_holders WHERE run_id = $1`, then `UPDATE named_lock_slots ... WHERE run_id = $1`, and **nothing else** — `step_reports` is never touched.
  - Even a late step report cannot repair it: `handleAgentReportStep` (`internal/controller/api_agent.go:513-525`) `GetRun`s first and, for a `Succeeded`/`Failed`/`Cancelled` run, returns `200 {"alreadyFinalized":true}` **without** calling `UpsertStepReport` — deliberately, so a stale report cannot write step state inconsistent with a terminal run. The side effect is that the pre-existing `Running` row, which is *equally* inconsistent, is the one that survives.
- **Expected:** Per I7, run status and the rows that describe it must not contradict each other or reality. A `Failed` run whose only step is displayed as still `Running`, with no end time, contradicts both: nothing is executing, and the run is over.
- **Notes:** Rated **minor**, not major, on this file's own rubric: the authoritative run status is correct, no lock is leaked (`mutex_holders`/`named_lock_slots` were both `(0 rows)` — see the I2/I3 entry below), nothing schedules or re-runs off the stale row, and no data is lost. It is a display/audit-row inconsistency under an already-correct terminal status — real and permanent, hence recorded as a violation rather than an observation, but low impact. Worth noting it is *systemic* rather than partition-specific: every orphan-fail path (stuck-run reaper, heartbeat reconcile, agent restart-with-same-ID reconcile, `call:` cascade cancel) goes through `failOrphanedRun` -> `MarkRunFinished` -> `FinishRun`, so any reaped run in any deployment leaves this row behind. Cheap fix: in the same `FinishRun` transaction, `UPDATE step_reports SET status = <run status>, ended_at = NOW() WHERE run_id = $1 AND status = 'Running'` — it is already a transaction, and the reaper already pays the per-run cost precisely so lock release can be done per-run.

## W1-5 — observation: the stuck-run reaper releases the mutex ~40s before the unfenced agent stops executing; a successor acquires it immediately, and the partitioned run produces exactly its 120 side effects, once each
- **Invariant:** I2 (at-most-once side effects), I3 (no lock leaks), I6 (zombie containment — *measure, don't judge*)
- **Severity:** minor (observation — confirms designed/expected behavior, no defect)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-5-oneway-partition-zombie.md`, main probe. Stack: `test/ha/docker-compose.ha.yaml` + `test/edgecase/compose/oneway.override.yaml`. Baseline gate confirmed first (`readyz` 200, exactly one leader `controller1`, both `agent1`/`agent2` registered with fresh `lastSeenAt` — `w1-5/baseline-gate.txt`). Applied `edge-sideeffect` and `edge-mutex-successor` (both `200`, `w1-5/jobs-apply.txt`), cleared `test/edgecase/sideeffect-data/sideeffect.log`, triggered run `94268ad8-50f7-4c27-b49b-454abaf49283`, and blocked the claiming agent within ~1.0s of the claim (`claimed_at 12:03:29.704791Z` -> nginx reload complete `12:03:30.703Z`; ~1.6s if measured from run *creation* at `12:03:29.119899Z` instead — anchoring to the claim, since that's when the agent could first act on the run). Raw evidence: `w1-5/inject.txt`, `w1-5/timeline-to-reap.txt`, `w1-5/successor.txt`, `w1-5/mutex-and-zombie-tail.txt`, `w1-5/i2-accounting.txt`, `w1-5/sideeffect.log`, `w1-5/agent1.log`, `w1-5/controllers.log`, `w1-5/db-final.txt`, `w1-5/post-heal-state.txt`, `w1-5/derived-counts.txt`.
- **Observed** (all times UTC, 2026-07-29):
  - **Injection.** Run created `12:03:29.119899Z`, `claimed_at 12:03:29.704791Z` by `agent1`, step `append` started `12:03:29.711167Z`. `inject.sh nginx-block agent1` issued `12:03:29.83Z`, nginx reload complete `12:03:30.70Z` (**T0**), denying `172.20.0.7`. First failing heartbeat `12:03:31.551822899Z` (`http 403`), first failing cancel poll `12:03:34.713281187Z` — the block was effective within ~1s.
  - **Reap (I5-adjacent sanity check).** `controller1`: `{"time":"2026-07-29T12:04:48.855796904Z","level":"WARN","msg":"stuck-run reaper: failed orphaned run (agent lost)","runId":"94268ad8-..."}` followed by `"stuck-run reaper: failed orphaned runs","count":1`; `runs.updated_at` for the `Failed` transition is `12:04:48.850844Z`. That is **78.1s after T0**. The agent's heartbeat-failure series is a clean 15s cadence (`12:03:31.55, 12:03:46.56, 12:04:01.56, 12:04:16.58, 12:04:31.58, 12:04:46.59, ...`), so its last *successful* heartbeat was `12:03:16.55Z` — inferred from the cadence, since a successful heartbeat logs nothing, and independently corroborated by the reaper's own predicate (`last_seen_at < NOW()-90s` at reap time requires `last_seen_at < 12:03:18.86Z`). Reap therefore landed **92.3s after the last live heartbeat**, i.e. `staleAfter` (90s) plus part of one 30s reaper tick — inside the `docs/high-availability.md` contract of `staleAfter` + reaper interval.
  - **Zombie window (I6, the measurement this scenario exists for).** The step kept executing and kept appending to the shared volume for **40.2s** after the run was authoritatively `Failed`: at reap time the log held 79 lines; the first line stamped after the reap is `run,80,12:04:49` and the last line written is `run,120,12:05:29` — **41 side-effect lines (iterations 80-120) produced by a run the control plane had already failed, whose mutex it had already handed to someone else.** The end of the window is not the system reasserting control: it is simply the 120-iteration loop running out. Nothing could have stopped it — there is no fencing token, the step shell has no lease check, and the agent's only inbound channel (the cancel poller) was failing `403` on every 5s attempt throughout (`12:03:34.71` ... `12:05:29.76`, 24 consecutive failures in `w1-5/agent1.log`, all for this run; `w1-5/agent1-full.log` shows 29 total across the whole scenario — those 24 plus 5 more from the addendum's `edge-short` run below).
  - **I3 — mutex released while the holder still ran.** Successor `edge-mutex-successor` triggered at `12:05:09.187Z` (20.4s after the reap; the gap is the tooling round-trip between two shell invocations, not any system delay): created `12:05:09.217251Z`, observed `Queued` at `12:05:09.356Z`, `claimed_at 12:05:09.930021Z` by **`agent2`** (the unblocked agent), `Succeeded` at `12:05:09.949063Z` — **732ms from creation to Succeeded**. It did this while `sideeffect.log` was demonstrably still growing (100 lines at `12:05:09.356Z`, 102 at `12:05:11.632Z`), i.e. the mutex was genuinely free while the previous holder was still executing. Direct table check at `12:05:30.456Z`, and again at the end of the scenario: `SELECT * FROM mutex_holders;` -> `(0 rows)` and `SELECT * FROM named_lock_slots;` -> `(0 rows)`, both redirected to `w1-5/mutex-and-zombie-tail.txt` / `w1-5/db-final.txt` rather than read interactively.
  - **I2 — no re-execution, no duplicates.** `GET /api/v1/runs?jobName=edge-sideeffect` returns **exactly one** run (`94268ad8-...`, `Failed`) — the reaper fails and never re-queues, and no second run appeared. `sideeffect.log` holds exactly **120 lines**, `run,1,12:03:29` through `run,120,12:05:29`; `awk -F, '{print $2}' | sort -n | uniq -d` produces **empty output** (no duplicated iteration number) and `| uniq | wc -l` gives **120** distinct iterations. Correlation caveat: `UNIFIED_RUN_ID` is not injected into native steps, so field 1 of every line is the literal string `run` (`awk -F, '{print $1}' | sort -u` -> `run`); attribution rests on there being exactly one `edge-sideeffect` run in the entire scenario, which the run listing above establishes.
  - **Post-heal behavior.** Unblocked at `12:06:10.94Z`. `agent1` logged **zero** `ERROR`/`WARN` lines between `12:06:11Z` and `12:09:18Z` (`w1-5/agent1-full.log` — not `w1-5/agent1.log`, whose capture ends `12:06:10.405884812Z`, ~0.5s **before** the unblock completed at `12:06:10.939Z`, so it cannot itself support a post-unblock zero-errors claim); `GET /api/v1/agents` at `12:07:25Z` showed `agent1` with `lastSeenAt 12:07:16.634484Z` (fresh), and `agent1` went on to claim new work — it is the agent that claimed the addendum's `edge-short` run at `12:09:18Z`. So the healed agent re-registers and resumes claiming cleanly. What it did **not** do is replay anything: its step report and finish call for the dead run had already been abandoned permanently at `12:05:30.358Z`/`.359Z` (`"permanent error, giving up retry","status":403`) — see the major entry above. Consequently the CAS/seal responses the runbook set out to observe (`200 alreadyFinalized` on a late finish/step report, `204`-append of a late log tail onto a `Failed` run) **were never exercised**: no buffered write was ever retried. `GET /runs/94268ad8-.../logs/stats` -> `{"count":0,"maxSeq":0,"minSeq":0}`, which is also simply correct here — the workload writes to a file with `>>` and emits nothing on stdout, so this run had no log lines to lose in the first place.
  - **Log-volume side observation.** While partitioned, `agent1` emitted **1301 `ERROR`-level `"msg":"claim"` lines** between `12:03:31.685183207Z` and `12:06:10.405884812Z` (~159s) — about **8.2 lines/s** — out of 1341 total log lines, because 16 detached claim slots each retry every ~2s and each logs its `403` at `ERROR`. A partitioned agent is therefore a sustained ERROR-log firehose whose volume scales with claim-slot count, and the two lines that actually matter (`"permanent error, giving up retry"`) are buried in it at the same severity.
- **Expected:** Per I3, a reaped run's mutex must be released so a successor is not starved — `FinishRun` does the `DELETE FROM mutex_holders` in the same transaction as the status flip (`internal/store/postgres.go:768-770`), and the successor's 732ms Queued-to-Succeeded confirms it end to end. Per I2, the reaper must fail and never re-queue (`stuckrun_reaper.go:72-73` states re-running partially-executed steps could duplicate side effects) — confirmed: one run, 120 iterations, each exactly once. Per I6 the campaign spec is explicit that zombie behavior is measured, not judged ("not pass/fail: the architecture has no hard fencing; the operator judges acceptability") — so the 40.2s / 41-side-effect window is reported as a number, not a defect.
- **Notes:** The number that matters for the operator's judgement is not 40.2s in the abstract — it is that the zombie window is *bounded only by the step's own remaining runtime*, with no ceiling from the system. Here a 120s step blocked ~1.0s after claim yielded ~40s of post-failure execution; the same reap timing against a 30-minute step would yield ~28 minutes of it, and any side effect the step performs in that window (this workload appends to a shared volume; a real one might deploy, migrate, or publish) happens *after* the control plane has declared the run failed and released its mutex to a successor that may be doing the same thing concurrently. The mutex is released correctly per I3 and yet, in physical terms, two runs can be inside the same critical section simultaneously — the successor acquiring at `12:05:09.93Z` while the zombie kept writing until `12:05:29Z` is a live, measured instance of exactly that. That is the honest shape of "no hard fencing," and it is the input the operator needs when deciding whether fencing (lease tokens checked at side-effect time, or a shorter `staleAfter` traded against false reaps) is worth building. **Injection-mechanism caveat:** the partition is an nginx per-agent IP deny, not a packet-level partition — the agent image has no `iptables` and no `NET_ADMIN`. Because unified-cd's agent protocol is strictly agent-polls-controller (claim, heartbeat, log push, step/finish report, cancel poll are all agent-initiated; the controller never dials the agent), an ingress deny is equivalent to a full one-way partition in terms of *what the agent can accomplish*. It is **not** equivalent in terms of *what the agent observes*: it sees prompt `403`s rather than a TCP black-hole of connect timeouts and half-open sockets. Everything above about reap timing, mutex release, side-effect accounting and zombie duration is unaffected by that difference (the reaper only reads `last_seen_at`, which stops advancing either way). What *is* affected is agent-side error classification — and that difference is the whole subject of the major entry above.

## W1-6 — observation: credential revocation takes effect on the agent's very next request (~1.5-5s), not after the ~45-minute cached-token window the campaign plan assumed; the run is then reaped in ~94-107s while the unfenced step keeps executing for another ~162s
- **Invariant:** I6 (zombie containment — *measure, don't judge*), I5 (bounded recovery — reap bound as an adjacent sanity check)
- **Severity:** minor (observation — no invariant violated; behavior is *stronger* than the campaign plan predicted)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-6-credential-revocation.md`, Part A. Plain `test/ha` stack, **no overlay** — the fault is injected through the product's own admin API. Baseline gate confirmed first at `12:42:31Z` (`readyz` 200; exactly one leader, `controller1: 1` vs `controller2: 0`, `controller3: 0`; both `agent1` and `agent2` registered with `lastSeenAt 12:42:21.5Z`) — `w1-6/baseline-gate.txt`. Applied `edge-longrun` (`12:45:17.82Z`, 200), triggered run `00a71dc8-8d6f-47fe-8ed8-32a5ff279185` (`created 12:45:17.889965Z`, `claimed_at 12:45:18.755223Z` by **`agent2`**, step `tick` `started_at 12:45:18.76669Z`), then `docker compose -f docker-compose.ha.yaml run --rm agent-enroll go run -buildvcs=false ./cmd/unified-cli agent identity revoke-credentials agent2`. Raw evidence in the scratchpad: `w1-6/baseline-gate.txt`, `w1-6/partA-trigger.txt`, `w1-6/revoke.txt`, `w1-6/partA-timeline.txt`, `w1-6/db-partA.txt`, `w1-6/agent2-full.log`, `w1-6/controllers.log`, `w1-6/db-final.txt`, `w1-6/audit.txt`, `w1-6/zombie-ps.txt`, `w1-6/derived-counts.txt`.
- **Observed** (all times UTC, 2026-07-29):
  - **The campaign plan's premise is disproved, and the disproof is the main result of Part A.** As originally written — since corrected — the plan asserted in its "Verified API facts used throughout (do not re-derive)" block that "access-token TTL 1h with lazy refresh 15 min before expiry → revocation does NOT bite a live agent quickly", and Task 8 Part A instructed the executor to observe the agent "keep executing AND keep reporting successfully on its cached token (record: heartbeats still accepted, logs still flowing — this is the ~45-min blind window in action)", with the Recording line calling "the 45-min blind window … the fleet's revocation SLA". Those three sites are now `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-29-edge-case-campaign-w1.md:19` (the facts block), `:298` (Task 8 Interfaces) and `:301` (Part A), plus the Recording bullet a few lines below `:301`; all four have been rewritten to record what was measured, under a `:296` banner that preserves the original wording and flags the task as corrected-after-execution — so the quotations above are of the *pre-correction* text and will no longer be found verbatim at those lines. No such window exists. Both halves of the TTL arithmetic are individually correct — `agentAccessTTL = time.Hour` (`internal/controller/api_agent_enrollment.go:21`), `tokenRefreshLeadTime = 15 * time.Minute` plus up to `maxCredentialJitter = 5 * time.Minute` (`internal/agent/credentials.go:27-28`), and `Token()` really does return the cached token while `now + lead + jitter` precedes `accessExpires` (`credentials.go:140`), so the *client* holds one token for ~40-45 min — but the **controller never trusts the token's self-asserted expiry**: `agentAuth` re-reads the credential row from Postgres on *every* agent API request and rejects on `credential.Status != "active" || credential.RevokedAt != nil` (`internal/controller/agent_auth.go:62-77`), with no server-side credential cache (`s.credentialTouches` rate-limits only the `last_used_at` write). The cached token therefore buys the agent nothing once the row is revoked. **The "45-minute blind window" figure was never measured in this campaign and must not be cited as if it had been** — the 40-45 min number above is arithmetic from the two constants, i.e. it is how long the *client* would keep presenting a token it believes is valid, which is a different quantity from how long that token is *accepted*.
  - **Measured revocation latency: 4.95s, and it is entirely the agent's own poll cadence.** `RevokeAgentIdentityCredentials` (`internal/store/postgres_agent_auth.go:467-489`) is a single blanket `UPDATE agent_credentials SET revoked_at = COALESCE(revoked_at, NOW()) WHERE identity_id = $1`, so both rows flipped at the same instant (`w1-6/db-partA.txt`):
    ```
     agent_id | status |  kind   |          revoked_at
     agent2   | active | access  | 2026-07-29 12:45:53.838417+00
     agent2   | active | refresh | 2026-07-29 12:45:53.838417+00
    ```
    matching the audit row `agent.credentials.revoke | agent2 | 204` at `12:45:53.841025+00` (`w1-6/audit.txt`). The first rejection observed on the agent side is its 5s cancel poll:
    ```
    {"time":"2026-07-29T12:45:58.784397076Z","level":"WARN","msg":"cancel poller: get run failed","runID":"00a71dc8-...","error":"http 401: response omitted"}
    ```
    — `revoked_at + 4.95s`. The first *heartbeat* failure follows at `12:46:06.563971Z` (`revoked_at + 12.7s`), consistent with the 15s heartbeat cadence. Neither figure is a server-side grace period: they are the intervals until the agent next happened to call. Part C's `disable` (below) produced **1.51s** for the same reason, and the true server-side latency is zero — the next request after the `UPDATE` commits is rejected.
  - **`lastSeenAt` freezes immediately, which is the direct proof.** `GET /api/v1/agents` returned `agent2 lastSeenAt 2026-07-29T12:45:51.831889Z` — 2.0s *before* `revoked_at` — and that value never advanced again across all 21 polls of the 5-minute window (`w1-6/partA-timeline.txt`, `12:46:10.570Z` through `12:50:54.365Z`), while `agent1`'s advanced normally on every poll. `last_seen_at` can only be written by a successfully authenticated heartbeat, so a frozen value is unambiguous.
  - **Log delivery stops at the same instant.** `GET /runs/<id>/logs/stats` read `{"count":34,"maxSeq":34,"minSeq":1}` at the first poll after revocation and was byte-identical at every subsequent poll and in the final read at `13:01:41Z` (`w1-6/partA-timeline.txt`, `w1-6/db-final.txt`). The full log body (`GET /logs?after=0`, 3450 bytes) contains exactly 34 lines, `tick 1`..`tick 34`, last delivered around `12:45:52Z`. The step went on to emit all 300, so **266 lines were lost**, and `grep -c "dropped"` over the body returns **0** — no `[N log line(s) dropped: controller unreachable]` marker. This is not a new mechanism: `LogPusher.flushLocked` (`internal/agent/runner.go:358-425`; its resend-everything loop over `p.pending` is `:358-366`) and `flushPendingLocked` (`:308-316`) requeue failed batches into `p.pending` with no status-code classification at all, so the 266 lines sat in memory being re-offered (and re-401'd) every 2s until the step ended and the bounded exit-time `Flush` (`:335-353`) gave up on them, and `droppedLines` — the sole trigger for the marker — was never incremented. That is exactly the abandoned-in-`pending`, no-marker shape already filed as **major (I4)** in the W1-2 entry above ("Postgres outage overlapping a step's natural completion…"); recorded here only as an independent reproduction under a different trigger (permanent auth rejection rather than a transient DB outage), not as a new finding.
  - **Reap (I5-adjacent sanity check).** `controller1`:
    ```
    {"time":"2026-07-29T12:47:40.701244716Z","level":"WARN","msg":"stuck-run reaper: failed orphaned run (agent lost)","runId":"00a71dc8-8d6f-47fe-8ed8-32a5ff279185"}
    {"time":"2026-07-29T12:47:40.701267873Z","level":"INFO","msg":"stuck-run reaper: failed orphaned runs","count":1}
    ```
    with `runs.updated_at 2026-07-29 12:47:40.697272+00`. That is **106.9s after `revoked_at`** and **108.9s after the last successful heartbeat** (`12:45:51.832Z`) — inside the `staleAfter` 90s + reaper interval 30s contract in `docs/high-availability.md`. **Nothing else could have settled this run:** the heartbeat reconcile (`internal/controller/api_agent.go:99-124`) requires a *successfully authenticated* heartbeat, which a de-credentialled agent cannot make, so — unlike W1-5's addendum, where the healed agent's own heartbeat is what triggered the reconcile — the stuck-run reaper is the only remaining actor. It fired, and **no run was left `Running`** (I1 held; `w1-6/db-final.txt` shows both scenario runs terminal).
  - **Zombie window (I6, the measurement this part exists for): 162.5s.** The step kept running to its natural end and only revealed itself at step-end, when both of its reports were abandoned at once:
    ```
    {"time":"2026-07-29T12:50:23.194862007Z","level":"ERROR","msg":"permanent error, giving up retry","status":401,"error":"response omitted"}
    {"time":"2026-07-29T12:50:23.196283306Z","level":"ERROR","msg":"permanent error, giving up retry","status":401,"error":"response omitted"}
    ```
    (`w1-6/agent2-full.log`; two lines = the step report and the run `FinishRun`.) `12:50:23.195Z − started_at 12:45:18.767Z = 304.4s`, which is where a 300-iteration `echo; sleep 1` loop lands — the loop ran to completion. **Zombie duration = 12:50:23.195Z − reap 12:47:40.697Z = 162.5s**, during which the step produced its remaining `tick` lines (none of which reached the control plane) on a run the control plane had already recorded `Failed`. As in W1-5, the window ends because the loop runs out, not because anything reasserts control: there is no fencing token, no lease check in the step, and the agent's only inbound channel — the cancel poller — was itself 401ing on every 5s attempt (53 consecutive failures across the run, `w1-6/derived-counts.txt`). **Caveat on the corroborating check:** `docker compose exec -T agent2 ps -ef` at `12:51:23.98Z` showed only the agent binary and no shell/`seq` process (`w1-6/zombie-ps.txt`) — that is *consistent with* the loop having ended at `12:50:23`, but it was taken ~60s after the fact and so does not by itself establish the window; the 162.5s figure rests on the step-end report timestamps above.
  - **Exit-code caveat, stated precisely (same shape as W1-5's addendum):** the step's exit code was never delivered — that is the point — so "it ran to completion" is an inference from (a) the loop being deterministic and unable to fail, and (b) the abandoned reports landing at exactly `started_at + 304.4s`. Unlike W1-5's addendum this does **not** make the recorded status *wrong*: the run genuinely was orphaned (its agent had been deliberately de-credentialled and could never report again), so `Failed` is the correct verdict here, not a status inversion.
- **Expected:** Per I6 the campaign spec is explicit that zombie behavior is measured, not judged ("not pass/fail: the architecture has no hard fencing; the operator judges acceptability"), so the 162.5s window is reported as a number. Per I5 the reap landed inside the documented 90s + 30s bound. Per I1 the run reached exactly one terminal state. Nothing in `docs/` promises a revocation latency in either direction — `docs/cli.md:897` and `docs/troubleshooting.md:1080-1091` describe the verbs and the recovery procedure but state no timing — so the fast-revocation result is recorded as an observation that *corrects a campaign-internal assumption*, not as a contract being met or missed.
- **Notes:** Two things the operator should take from this. **(1) The fleet's revocation SLA is good, and better than the plan documented** — a compromised agent credential stops being accepted the instant the `UPDATE` commits, with no cache to wait out; the only residual exposure is an in-flight HTTP request already past the auth middleware. The plan document has since been corrected at all four sites that carried the disproved premise — `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-29-edge-case-campaign-w1.md:19` (the "Verified API facts used throughout (do not re-derive)" block), `:298` (Task 8 Interfaces), `:301` (Part A) and Task 8's Recording bullet — under a `:296` banner marking the task as corrected-after-execution and preserving the original wording, so the false premise does not propagate into later waves and the fact that a scenario was designed around it is not erased. **(2) Revocation does not stop *execution*, only *reporting*.** The 162.5s zombie window here is the same unfenced-executor shape W1-5 measured at 40.2s, and the same caveat applies: it is bounded only by the step's own remaining runtime, with no ceiling from the system. Revoking an agent's credentials is therefore *not* a containment action for a compromised or misbehaving agent — it severs the control plane's view of that agent while leaving whatever the step is doing (deploying, migrating, publishing) running to completion on the host. An operator responding to a compromise needs to stop the agent process/host as well; the credential verb alone is a *reporting* kill switch, not an *execution* one. That is a docs point worth adding to `docs/troubleshooting.md`'s credential section, which currently frames `revoke-credentials` purely as a recovery step for a lost or replayed refresh credential.

## W1-6 — observation, Parts B & C: a de-credentialled agent exits cleanly on restart (exit 1, no crash loop) and cannot deregister; `revoke-credentials` and `disable` are indistinguishable to a live agent (both `401`) and differ only by status code on the token-refresh path (`403` vs `401`)
- **Invariant:** I1 (run accounting), I5 (bounded recovery — diagnosability), I7 (state display consistency)
- **Severity:** minor (observation — confirms designed behavior; the one genuine defect surfaced by these parts is filed separately below)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-6-credential-revocation.md`, Parts B and C. Part B restarts the revoked `agent2` from Part A. Part C repeats the probe against a fresh agent and run: run `0f40b6c4-223f-4d40-b9c1-ff2c75f41bc0` (`created 12:54:37.602185Z`, `claimed_at 12:54:38.631553Z` by **`agent1`**, step `started_at 12:54:38.638066Z`), then `... unified-cli agent identity disable agent1`. Raw evidence: `w1-6/partB-restart.txt`, `w1-6/partB-agent2-startup.log`, `w1-6/partC-trigger.txt`, `w1-6/disable.txt`, `w1-6/partC-live.txt`, `w1-6/partC-timeline.txt`, `w1-6/partC-restart.txt`, `w1-6/partC-evidence.txt`, `w1-6/agent1-full.log`, `w1-6/audit.txt`, `w1-6/metrics.txt`, `w1-6/db-final.txt`.
- **Observed** (all times UTC, 2026-07-29):
  - **`disable` behaves identically to `revoke` against a live agent.** `SetAgentIdentityEnabled(..., false)` (`internal/store/postgres_agent_auth.go:437-465`) sets `agent_identities.status='disabled'` **and** runs the same blanket credential revoke, in one transaction — confirmed live, `disabled_at` and both credentials' `revoked_at` all read `2026-07-29 12:55:07.144433+00` (`w1-6/partC-live.txt`). `agent1`'s `lastSeenAt` froze at `12:55:06.860418Z` (0.28s before), and its first rejection was the cancel poller at `12:55:08.652648Z` = **`disabled_at + 1.51s`**, `http 401` — the *same* status code Part A's revoke produced, not `403`. This is by design: `GetAgentCredentialForAuth` returns `ErrAgentIdentityDisabled` for a disabled identity and `ErrAgentCredentialNotFound` for a revoked credential (`postgres_agent_auth.go:286-291`), and `agentAuth` collapses **both** to a bare `401 unauthorized` (`internal/controller/agent_auth.go:64-70`). So on the agent API path the two verbs are indistinguishable to the agent, to the agent's log, and to the `unifiedcd_agent_auth_events_total` metric (all such failures land in `provider="access",reason="invalid"` — `w1-6/metrics.txt`).
  - **The `403 agent identity disabled` difference is real but only on the token-refresh path, i.e. only visible at restart.** `RotateAgentRefresh` checks identity status *before* the credential (`postgres_agent_auth.go:349-352`), so `respondAgentCredentialError` emits `403 "agent identity disabled"` for disable and `401 unauthorized` for a merely-revoked credential (`internal/controller/api_agent_enrollment.go:496-512`). Both restarts confirmed it, and the audit table records the split cleanly (`w1-6/audit.txt`):
    ```
     2026-07-29 12:53:25.546015+00 | agent | agent.enrollment.exchange | 5f5f4538-... | 401   <- agent2, revoked
     2026-07-29 12:53:25.553496+00 | agent | agent.refresh             | 5aaaed1e-... | 401
     2026-07-29 12:58:43.487818+00 | agent | agent.enrollment.exchange | 22c51d57-... | 401   <- agent1, disabled
     2026-07-29 12:58:43.505942+00 | agent | agent.refresh             | 116d9d08-... | 403
    ```
    and the `unifiedcd_agent_auth_events_total` counters on `controller2` distinguish them too: `{provider="refresh",reason="disabled",result="failure"} 1` alongside `{provider="refresh",reason="invalid",result="failure"} 1` (`w1-6/metrics.txt`). **On the agent's own side the difference is a bare status code with no text** — `{"level":"ERROR","msg":"resolve agent identity","error":"credential request returned http 401"}` (`12:53:25.555573Z`, agent2) versus `...http 403` (`12:58:43.508405Z`, agent1). The `"agent identity disabled"` body string never appears in the agent log; an operator must know the 401/403 mapping to read it. `docs/troubleshooting.md:1080-1091` does document exactly this mapping ("the agent receives `unauthorized`, `agent identity disabled`, or `enrollment unavailable` while starting or refreshing"), scoped to "starting or refreshing" — which is precisely the path where `403` does appear — so this is **not** a docs contradiction, just a thin agent-side error surface.
  - **Clean exit, not a crash loop.** Both restarts produced an identical shape: the container starts, fails to obtain a token, and exits(1) once. `docker inspect` (`w1-6/partB-restart.txt`, `w1-6/partC-restart.txt`):
    ```
    agent2: restartCount=0 status=exited exit=1 started=12:53:25.239525763Z finished=12:53:25.558152525Z   (0.32s)
    agent1: restartCount=0 status=exited exit=1 started=12:58:43.204062055Z finished=12:58:43.512546461Z   (0.31s)
    ```
    Each emitted exactly two lines: a `WARN "enrollment token rejected (expired or already consumed); continuing with the existing credential"` (the one-time enrollment token really had been consumed at first boot, so this is correct, if easy to misread as the cause) followed by the `ERROR "resolve agent identity"` above. `docker-compose.ha.yaml` sets no `restart:` policy, so the container stays `Exited (1)` — no loop, no log churn, no retry storm at startup. This is the code path predicted from `cmd/unified-cd-agent/main.go:167-173`, except that the failure lands in `EnsureIdentity` rather than in `Agent.Run`'s `Register`, because a present `enrollment.token` file makes the credential manager attempt the enroll-then-refresh sequence during identity resolution.
  - **Graceful shutdown cannot deregister.** On `agent2`'s SIGTERM the drain path completed instantly (its run had already ended at `12:50:23`) and the final deregistration 401'd: `{"time":"2026-07-29T12:53:24.886862281Z","level":"WARN","msg":"deregister failed","agentId":"agent2","error":"http 401: response omitted"}` (`w1-6/agent2-full.log`). A de-credentialled agent therefore cannot remove its own `agents` row; it disappears only when the background sweep `DeleteStaleAgents(ctx, 5*time.Minute)` (`cmd/controller/main.go:390`, `internal/store/postgres.go:1203`) collects it. Observed exactly that: `agent2` was still listed with its frozen `lastSeenAt 12:45:51.831889Z` at `12:50:54Z` and was gone from the `agents` table by `12:52:04Z` (`w1-6/partA-timeline.txt`, `w1-6/db-partA.txt`), i.e. ~5 min after its last heartbeat. Final state at `13:01:41Z`: `SELECT id, last_seen_at FROM agents;` -> `(0 rows)` and `GET /api/v1/agents` -> `[]` (`w1-6/db-final.txt`, `w1-6/identity-final.txt`).
  - **Who fails the run — the stuck-run reaper, both times, and the restart is irrelevant to it.** Part C's run was reaped at `controller1` `12:56:40.947643323Z` with `runs.updated_at 12:56:40.942247+00` = **`disabled_at + 93.8s`** (and last-heartbeat + 94.1s), versus Part A's +106.9s — both inside the 90s + 30s bound, the spread being where the revocation lands within the 30s reaper tick. **No run was left `Running`; I1 held.** Note the ordering: in both parts the reap happened *before* the restart (5m43s before in Part B, 2m00s before in Part C), so the brief's question "the restart abandoned the run — so who fails the run, and when?" is answered by the revocation itself rather than by the restart. That ordering is not an artifact of how long this probe waited: the reaper's clock starts at the last successful heartbeat, which stops at revocation, so a run becomes eligible ~90-120s after revocation no matter when (or whether) the agent is restarted, and the restarted agent's `ReconcileRuns` (`internal/agent/agent.go:204-208`) can never run because the process exits before reaching it.
  - **Part C's zombie window is a lower bound only.** `agent1`'s step was ~58s short of its 300 ticks (arithmetic from `started_at 12:54:38.638Z`) when the Part C restart terminated it at `12:58:41.549Z`, so the measured `12:56:40.942Z` -> `12:58:41.549Z` = **120.6s** of post-`Failed` execution was cut off by the probe, not self-terminated — Part A's 162.5s is the self-terminating figure. `agent1`'s old process logged the shutdown signal and then nothing further before being replaced (no `deregister failed`, no drain-completion line, `w1-6/partC-evidence.txt`), so what its drain path actually did is **not observable from the captured logs** and no claim is made about it.
  - **Both runs' `step_reports` rows are stale.** `SELECT ... FROM step_reports` after both settled (`w1-6/db-final.txt`):
    ```
     00a71dc8-... | 0 | tick | Running |  | 2026-07-29 12:45:18.76669+00  |
     0f40b6c4-... | 0 | tick | Running |  | 2026-07-29 12:54:38.638066+00 |
    ```
    while both `runs` rows read `Failed`. This is a third and fourth reproduction of the already-filed **minor I7** entry above ("W1-5 — a reaped/orphan-failed run's in-flight `step_reports` row stays `Running` forever"); recorded as corroboration that it is systemic across orphan-fail triggers, **not** re-filed. `mutex_holders` was `(0 rows)` — neither run used a mutex, so this is a null result for I3, not a pass.
  - **Run 2 also lost its log tail — but by a *different* and much less surprising mechanism than run 1's, and it is NOT a reproduction of the W1-2 major.** `GET /runs/0f40b6c4-.../logs/stats` reads `{"count":28,"maxSeq":62,"minSeq":35}` at every poll from the first one taken through the final read (`w1-6/partC-timeline.txt`, `w1-6/db-final.txt`) — 28 lines delivered, frozen from `disabled_at` onward exactly as run 1's froze at 34. By the time `agent1`'s process was terminated (`12:58:41.549Z` shutdown signal), the step had been running `12:58:41.549Z - started_at 12:54:38.638Z = 242.9s`, i.e. it had emitted roughly **242** of its 300 `tick` lines — so **~214 of those ~242 emitted lines were never delivered**, and `grep -rc "dropped"` across every captured log/evidence file for this scenario returns **0**. **But the step-end `Flush` path was never reached here.** Run 1's step ran to its natural end (`12:50:23`), so `finishLogs` really did stop the auto-flush ticker and burn the bounded 5s `Flush` budget against an unreachable controller — that is the W1-2 major's exact mechanism, and it left the two `"permanent error, giving up retry"` lines that prove the step-end teardown ran. Run 2's process was **SIGTERMed mid-step** by the Part C restart, ~58s short of its 300 ticks, and `w1-6/partC-evidence.txt` records the consequence directly: `agent1`'s permanent-error section is **empty** — zero such lines in the entire log — because the step never finished and the teardown never ran. Its buffered lines died with the killed process. That is ordinary process-kill data loss, not the bounded-retry-window defect: no marker is expected when the process carrying `p.pending` simply ceases to exist. **Correction to an earlier version of this bullet, which called run 2's loss "the same mechanism as run 1's":** it is not. The `LogPusher` machinery involved is shared (`flushLocked`/`flushPendingLocked`, `internal/agent/runner.go:358-366`, `:308-316`, requeue failed batches into `p.pending` with no status-code classification), but the *loss event* differs, and only run 1 corroborates the W1-2 major. Recorded as corroboration for run 1 only; neither is re-filed.
- **Expected:** Per I1, both runs reached exactly one terminal state within a bounded time, via the documented reaper path. Per I5, the agent's failure mode on restart is bounded and terminal rather than a loop. Per I7, no status contradicted another beyond the already-filed stale `step_reports` row. The `401` vs `403` split matches `docs/troubleshooting.md:1080-1091`, which scopes `agent identity disabled` to "starting or refreshing". All as designed.
- **Notes:** The practical operator takeaway is that **`revoke-credentials` and `disable` are the same action from the agent's perspective and differ only in what a *re-enrollment* attempt is told**: revoke leaves the identity re-enrollable (a fresh enrollment token will work), disable does not until someone runs `enable`. Nothing observable to a running agent, its logs, or the access-path metric distinguishes them, so an operator diagnosing "why did my fleet stop" cannot tell which verb was used without reading the audit log. That is a reasonable design (auth fails closed with a generic 401 rather than leaking identity state to an unauthenticated caller) and is recorded as an observation, not a defect. **Overlap note — read with the W1-5 major above:** both step-end abandonments and both restarts here drove the identical `if` at `internal/agent/retry.go:33-36` (every `HTTPError` with `StatusCode < 500` is permanent; `internal/agent/client.go:107-108` mints one for any status `>= 400`) that W1-5 filed as **major**. That entry is **not** re-filed here, and the reason is the distinction its own forward reference set up: W1-5's `403` was minted by an *intermediary* (nginx's IP deny) and abandoning on it was wrong, whereas W1-6's `401`/`403` are *controller-originated* and genuinely permanent — the agent's credential really is revoked and no amount of retrying would help. **Judged on this scenario's evidence, abandoning is the correct behavior here**: retrying forever would only add to the rejected-auth load measured in the entry below, and the run is settled correctly by the reaper regardless. W1-6 therefore *narrows* rather than widens the W1-5 finding — it confirms the classification rule has a legitimate use case, which is why the fix the W1-5 entry recommends should be its options (a), "distinguish controller-originated 4xx from intermediary-originated 4xx", **and** (c), "make the abandonment loud on the controller side too" — and **not** its option (b), "retry 4xx with the same backoff up to a long ceiling", which would degrade this scenario without helping W1-5's. W1-6's own evidence *strengthens* (c) rather than dropping it: the only trace of either abandonment here was the same two `"permanent error, giving up retry"` ERROR lines, sitting inside an 8.24/8.07-lines/s same-severity claim-rejection firehose (see the rejection-load observation below) — on the agent side that is already nearly invisible, and the controller side today has no trace of the abandonment at all, which is exactly the silence (c) exists to fix.

## W1-6 — `agent_identities.last_authenticated_at` is exposed as an API field and a schema column but is never written by any code path, so `lastAuthenticatedAt` is permanently absent from every identity read
- **Invariant:** Documented/exported API contract — primary: `lastAuthenticatedAt` is a published JSON field on an operator-facing endpoint, and operator-facing wire format is a contract independent of whether it is separately named by a campaign invariant. I7 (state display consistency) — secondary: I7's own spec text (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:54`, "run status, approval status, and audit rows never contradict each other or reality") enumerates run status, approval status, and audit rows; identity metadata is not literally in that list, so I7 is cited as the closest-fit invariant, not as a direct hit.
- **Severity:** minor
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-6-credential-revocation.md`, Part C final reads. `GET /api/v1/agent-identities/agent1` and `/agent2` after ~19 minutes of normal operation and ~690 successful agent authentications. Raw evidence: `w1-6/identity-final.txt`, `w1-6/db-partA.txt`, `w1-6/metrics.txt`.
- **Borderline-call note:** this finding survives on the contract limb, not the invariant limb, and the contract itself is thin — `lastAuthenticatedAt` is undocumented in `docs/` (only `docs/authentication.md:140-141` mentions the endpoint at all, and only to name it, not its fields). A stricter reading would call a field that has never once been populated **dead code** rather than a live contradiction of reality. Recorded as a violation on the API/schema contract, with that stricter alternative reading flagged rather than silently resolved.
- **Observed:** Both identity reads omit the field entirely (`w1-6/identity-final.txt`, `13:00:44Z`):
  ```
  agent1: {"id":"5c50f4df-...","agentId":"agent1","status":"disabled","enrollmentMethod":"enrollment","authorizedLabels":["kind:linux"],"createdAt":"2026-07-29T12:42:21.503476Z","disabledAt":"2026-07-29T12:55:07.144433Z"}
  agent2: {"id":"3792b116-...","agentId":"agent2","status":"active","enrollmentMethod":"enrollment","authorizedLabels":["kind:linux"],"createdAt":"2026-07-29T12:42:21.442296Z"}
  ```
  and the column is `NULL` for both rows in Postgres (`SELECT id, agent_id, status, disabled_at, last_authenticated_at FROM agent_identities;`, `w1-6/db-partA.txt`) despite `unifiedcd_agent_auth_events_total{provider="access",reason="ok",result="success"}` summing to **689** across the three controllers over the stack's lifetime (`w1-6/metrics.txt`: 230 + 233 + 226). The field is not merely stale — it is never written at all:
  - declared in the API contract as `LastAuthenticatedAt *time.Time` with JSON tag `lastAuthenticatedAt,omitempty` (`internal/api/agent_auth.go:80`) and populated into every identity response by `identityMeta` (`internal/controller/api_agent_enrollment.go:173`);
  - a real column since `internal/store/migrations/013_agent_identity_auth.up.sql:11`;
  - `SELECT`ed / `RETURNING`ed in five places (`internal/store/postgres_agent_auth.go:508`, `:517`, `:526`, `:547`, `:558`);
  - and the **only three** `UPDATE agent_identities` statements in the codebase are `postgres_agent_auth.go:201` (`authorized_labels`), `:256` (`authorized_labels, authorized_capabilities`) and `:447` (`status, disabled_at`) — none touches `last_authenticated_at`, and neither `INSERT` supplies it. The per-request freshness write that *does* exist targets a different table and column: `TouchAgentCredential` -> `UPDATE agent_credentials SET last_used_at = NOW()` (`postgres_agent_auth.go:295-304`), which is populated as expected (`agent1 access last_used_at 2026-07-29 12:47:21.95982+00`, `w1-6/db-partA.txt`).
- **Expected:** A field named `lastAuthenticatedAt`, declared in the exported API contract, backed by a dedicated schema column, and returned by the only per-identity read endpoint, is itself a promise — independent of any specific campaign invariant — that an operator can ask "when did this identity last authenticate?" and get a real answer. The answer is structurally always "never", including for identities that authenticated hundreds of times seconds earlier. `omitempty` makes the failure silent: the key is simply absent, which is indistinguishable from an identity that has genuinely never authenticated. Per I7 (secondary, see above), this also falls under "never contradict... reality" in spirit, even though identity metadata is not one of the three items I7's spec text names.
- **Notes:** Rated **minor**, not major, on this file's own rubric: it is a diagnosability/audit-surface gap under otherwise-correct behavior — nothing schedules, authorizes, or reaps off this field, no run or credential state is affected, and the information is recoverable from `agent_credentials.last_used_at`, from `agents.last_seen_at`, and from the `unifiedcd_agent_auth_events_total` metric. It is filed as a **violation** rather than an observation because it is a published API field that permanently contradicts reality, not a risk that merely could bite; note the claim rests on an exported API/JSON contract plus a schema column, not on any internal doc comment. The cost matters most in exactly the situation this scenario probes: after `revoke-credentials`, the identity read is the operator's only per-identity view, and it shows `status: "active"` with no `disabledAt` and no `lastAuthenticatedAt` — nothing whatsoever indicating that every credential for that identity was revoked minutes earlier. (That `status` stays `active` after `revoke-credentials` is itself correct and deliberate — revoke leaves the identity re-enrollable, unlike `disable` — so it is recorded here as context, not as a second defect.) Cheapest fix: set `last_authenticated_at = NOW()` alongside the existing `TouchAgentCredential` write, which is already rate-limited by `s.credentialTouches.shouldTouch` and so adds no new per-request cost; or drop the field from the API and the schema if it is not wanted. A second, larger option worth the operator's consideration: surface credential state on the identity read (e.g. an active-credential count or a `credentialsRevokedAt`), so that `revoke-credentials` — which `docs/troubleshooting.md:1084-1088` prescribes as the response to a *lost or replayed refresh credential*, i.e. a security incident — leaves a visible trace on the object an operator would actually inspect.

## W1-6 — observation: a de-credentialled agent becomes a permanent, un-backed-off rejection generator — ~8.2 ERROR log lines/s on the agent, and a controller-side rejected-auth load that *rises* from an ~8.3/s steady floor to a derived ~67/s peak (20,447 total, averaging ~30.6/s across the outage), until a human stops the process
- **Invariant:** I5 (bounded recovery — the agent never recovers and never backs off)
- **Severity:** minor (observation — no invariant violated and no documented promise broken; recorded for the numbers and the fleet-scale extrapolation)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-6-credential-revocation.md`, Parts A-C, measured over the two de-credentialled windows: `agent2` from `revoked_at 12:45:53.838Z` to its restart-exit `12:53:25.558Z` (451.7s) and `agent1` from `disabled_at 12:55:07.144Z` to its restart-exit `12:58:43.513Z` (216.4s). Raw evidence: `w1-6/derived-counts.txt`, `w1-6/metrics.txt`, `w1-6/agent1-full.log`, `w1-6/agent2-full.log`, `w1-6/audit.txt`, `w1-6/db-final.txt`.
- **Observed:**
  - **Agent-side log firehose.** `agent2` emitted **3483** `ERROR`-level `"msg":"claim"` lines between `12:46:21.858015814Z` and `12:53:24.666509128Z` (422.8s) — **8.24 lines/s** — out of 3486 ERROR lines total; `agent1` emitted **1600** such lines between `12:55:22.695235118Z` and `12:58:41.074910422Z` (198.4s) — **8.07 lines/s** — out of 1601 (`w1-6/derived-counts.txt`; the two rates are derived by dividing those logged counts by those logged first-to-last spans). The mechanism is the same one W1-5 recorded at 8.2 lines/s under partition: 16 detached claim slots each retrying every ~2s, each logging its rejection at `ERROR`. The two lines that actually matter — the `"permanent error, giving up retry"` pair marking the abandoned step/finish report — sit inside that stream at the same severity. Everything else in both logs is nearly silent by comparison: 3 and 4 `INFO` lines respectively, plus the 15s-cadence heartbeat failures (30 and 14) and 5s-cadence cancel-poll failures (53 and 43) at `WARN`.
  - **Controller-side rejected-auth load, corrected: this is not a flat rate, it is a rising curve, and claim slots are not its dominant term.** `unifiedcd_agent_auth_events_total{provider="access",reason="invalid",result="failure"}` read **6812 / 6809 / 6826** on `controller1` / `controller2` / `controller3` at `13:02:41Z` (`w1-6/metrics.txt`) = **20,447** rejections (measured), against **689** successes over the whole ~20-minute stack lifetime. Dividing that total by the two windows' combined span (668.1s) gives **~30.6 rejected requests/s** (20447 / 668.1, derived) — that number is correct as an *average*, but it is not a rate anything in the system actually ran at: the true shape is dominated by `LogPusher.flushLocked` (`internal/agent/runner.go:358-366`) re-sending **every** still-pending batch as its own `AppendLogBulk` call on each 2s auto-flush tick (`logPusherAutoFlushEvery`, `runner.go:211`), so request count at tick *k* of a sustained outage is *k*, not 1 — cumulative volume grows as a triangular number, quadratic in outage duration, not linear. Reconciliation model (labelled a **model**, not a second measurement, since it is built from a mix of measured and derived inputs — see the breakdown entry directly below for the full arithmetic and the command-level verification): agent2's step kept emitting output for **269.4s** past `revoked_at` before ending naturally, agent1's for **214.4s** past `disabled_at` before being terminated by the Part C restart — at ~134 and ~107 completed 2s ticks respectively, the triangular sums are ≈9,045 and ≈5,778 log-push requests; add the 5,083 measured claim-ERROR lines, ~140 measured heartbeat/cancel-poll failures, and ≈400 (derived) from agent2's 3 exit-time `Flush` retries (`runner.go:335-353`) each re-offering its ~134 still-pending batches (agent1's shutdown was cut short by the container restart before its own exit-time retries could run, so it is assumed to contribute none — the SIGTERM-to-restart gap was ~1.65s against `Flush`'s 1s retry sleep, so one pass could in fact have run; this is the model's one unhedged input, and it moves the total by at most ~1 tick's worth of batches) — **total ≈20,448, against the measured 20,447** (off by 1). That close a fit is strong corroboration the mechanism is right, treated as such and no more.
    - **Peak (derived, not measured directly): ~67 req/s**, at the single 2s tick immediately before agent2's step ended (134 requests / 2s = 67/s) — entirely from log-push retries, not from claims.
    - **Steady state once a step's output stops and nothing is left to re-flush (derived): ~8.3 req/s** — 16 detached claim slots retried every ~2s (≈8.0/s) plus the 15s-cadence heartbeat (≈0.07/s) plus the 5s-cadence cancel-poll (≈0.2/s). This is the number that actually persists indefinitely (see the corrected fleet projection below); the previous version of this entry's ~30/s figure overstated that floor by roughly 4x by treating the whole-outage average as if it were the ongoing rate, while simultaneously understating the transient peak by roughly 2x.
    - The quadratic amplification itself — not the specific 20,447 figure — is the generalizable result and is filed as its own observation entry immediately below, cross-referenced against the F9 and W1-2 spill-adjacent findings.
  - **Each rejection is a database round trip.** `agentAuth` calls `s.store.GetAgentCredentialForAuth(r.Context(), parsed.ID)` (`internal/controller/agent_auth.go:62`) *before* it can know the credential is revoked, and there is no negative cache and no short-circuit — so the bulk of the load above is also that many Postgres `SELECT`s of pure waste, at whatever the instantaneous rate is (rising from ~8.3/s to a ~67/s peak, not a flat ~30/s). This linkage is read from the code path, **not** separately measured (no per-query metric was captured).
  - **The agent never backs off and never re-authenticates.** Its claim loop retries on the same ~2s cadence for the entire window with no escalation, and — unlike the report path, where `retryUntilSuccess` abandons on a 4xx (`internal/agent/retry.go:33-37`) — nothing in the claim/heartbeat/poll loops treats a `401` as terminal. There is also no re-authentication attempt: `Client.do`'s 401 handler invalidates the token source and replays, but only for `GET`s and only if the source implements the unexported `invalidatingTokenSource` (`internal/agent/client.go:37-42`, `:96-105`), and `*CredentialManager` does not implement `Invalidate()` (`grep -rn "Invalidate()" internal/agent` matches only `client.go` and `client_test.go`'s fake), so that branch is dead for the real agent. Confirmed live: neither agent log contains any refresh attempt between revocation and restart, and the only credential-path calls in the entire audit table are the two restart pairs (`w1-6/audit.txt`). The state is therefore stable-but-useless until a human intervenes.
- **Expected:** Nothing documents a backoff obligation for the claim/heartbeat loops, and failing closed on every request is the correct security posture, so no contract is broken. I5's "bounded recovery" concerns returning to steady state after a fault, and this fault is *intentional and permanent* — there is no recovery to be bounded, which is why this is recorded as an observation rather than an I5 violation.
- **Notes:** The number the operator needs is the fleet-scale one, and it is an **extrapolation from the single-agent measurement above, not measured** — and it now has two components rather than one flat rate. The part that persists **indefinitely**, per stuck agent, until a human intervenes: roughly `N x 8.3` rejected authenticated requests/s (and equally `N x 8.3` wasted Postgres queries/s) plus `N x 8.2` ERROR log lines/s — at the ~100-agent scale named in the campaign spec's scope section that is ~830/s of steady-state waste, not the ~3,000/s previously projected from the flat ~30/s misreading. The part that is **transient but can spike far higher**: for as long as any of those N agents has a step still actively producing output during the outage, that agent's log-push contribution alone can reach the ~67/s peak measured here, and — per the amplification entry below — keeps climbing for as long as the step keeps producing output, growing quadratically with how long the step's tail sits unflushed. Two cheap mitigations, in increasing cost, both aimed at the steady floor: (a) apply the existing exponential backoff to sustained `401`s in the agent's claim/heartbeat loops and log at a decaying rate (a revoked agent has nothing useful to do; polling every 2s serves no purpose); (b) add a short negative cache for rejected credential IDs in `agentAuth` so repeated presentations of a known-revoked token do not each cost a query. Neither changes the security posture, since the authoritative check still runs on every cache miss and on every successful auth. The transient peak needs a different fix — see the amplification entry below. Also worth carrying into W6 planning: this is the same log-firehose shape W1-5 measured under partition, but there it was self-limiting (the partition heals, the agent recovers); under revocation the steady floor is unbounded in time — so if a scale/abuse scenario wants a cheap, sustained source of controller auth load, revoking a live agent is it, and pairing that with a chatty still-running step is worse still.

## W1-6 — observation: `LogPusher`'s failed-flush retry re-sends the *entire* accumulated backlog on every 2s tick, so a chatty step under a long outage drives controller request count as O(t²) in outage duration, not O(t)
- **Invariant:** I5 (bounded recovery — resource cost under an ongoing fault) — adjacent, not yet shown to violate a documented bound.
- **Severity:** minor (observation — the mechanism is code-confirmed and the model reconciles almost exactly against measurement, but nothing in `docs/` bounds retry request volume during an outage, so this does not contradict an invariant or a documented contract; recorded for the shape, which is new information, not for a broken promise)
- **Repro:** Same as the entry directly above — `test/edgecase/scenarios/w1-6-credential-revocation.md`, Parts A-C. Raw evidence: `w1-6/derived-counts.txt`, `w1-6/metrics.txt`, `w1-6/agent1-full.log`, `w1-6/agent2-full.log`. Source: `internal/agent/runner.go:207-366`.
- **Observed:**
  - **Mechanism, code-confirmed.** `flushCompleteLinesLocked` (`runner.go:291-305`) fires every `logPusherAutoFlushEvery = 2 * time.Second` (`runner.go:211`) and calls `flushLocked` (`runner.go:358-366`), which does not send only the newest data — it iterates `p.pending` and re-issues a full `AppendLogBulk` HTTP call for **every** batch still sitting there, appending the current buffer as one more batch on top. Nothing in this scenario ever recovers (the credential is permanently revoked), so no batch is ever removed from `p.pending` by success; each tick both resends every prior batch *and* adds a new one. At tick *k* since the outage began, that single tick therefore costs *k* requests, and the running total after *k* ticks is the triangular number *k(k+1)/2* — quadratic in elapsed outage time, not linear. `p.pending`'s only cap is `maxPendingBytes: 1 << 20` (1MiB, `runner.go:256`), a **byte** budget, not a batch-count budget; this scenario's batches are a few hundred bytes each (`tick N` lines), so hundreds of them fit in 1MiB and the drop-oldest/marker eviction path (`runner.go:431` onward) never engages — confirmed live, `grep -rc "dropped"` across every captured log/evidence file for this whole scenario returns **0**.
  - **Reconciliation against the measured total (model, not a second measurement — inputs individually marked below).** Outage-to-step-end/termination spans: agent2 `12:50:23.195Z - revoked_at 12:45:53.838417Z` = **269.4s** (measured, both endpoints from `w1-6/agent2-full.log` and `w1-6/db-partA.txt`); agent1 `12:58:41.549108Z - disabled_at 12:55:07.144433Z` = **214.4s** (measured, from `w1-6/partC-evidence.txt` and `w1-6/partC-live.txt`). At the 2s tick cadence that is **134** and **107** complete ticks (derived: `floor(span/2)`), giving triangular sums **9,045** and **5,778** log-push requests (derived arithmetic).
