  - **The restart policy is the rig's, and it is stated here rather than glossed** (`w3-2/partD-policy.txt`, read *before* the experiment). `test/ha/docker-compose.ha.yaml` sets `restart: "no"` on the one-shot `agent-enroll` only; the controllers inherit Compose's default, and `docker inspect` confirms `{"Name":"no","MaximumRetryCount":0}` on all three. **So on this rig the controller does not crash-*loop*; it exits once and stays exited.** Under `restart: always` or a Kubernetes Deployment it would crashloop and self-heal when the store returned. **The product behaviour under test is `os.Exit(1)` with no in-process retry; the recovery story is delegated entirely to the supervisor, and neither outcome is bounded by anything unified-cd controls.**
  - **THE ASYMMETRY THAT MAKES THIS MORE THAN A RESTART-POLICY NOTE — the same condition is tolerated indefinitely by a process that is already up.** In Part A2 the object store was unreachable for **7 m 30.8 s** while all three controllers kept running: `/readyz` returned **200** on every replica, the LB's `/healthz` returned 200, and a run triggered mid-outage went `Pending → Succeeded` in 34.9 s (`w3-2/partA2-blast.txt`, `03:31:02Z – 03:31:39Z`). So an object-store outage is a **non-event** for a running controller and **fatal** to a starting one. The docs make the opposite pairing explicit for the database: `docs/high-availability.md:289` — "`/readyz` pings the DB, so replicas with a DB connectivity issue are automatically rotated out". Object-store connectivity is checked **once, at startup, fatally**, and never again.
  - **Blast radius while one of three was dead** (`w3-2/partD-blast.txt`): the LB served `/readyz` 200 and a run triggered against it was accepted — **`7b1d023e`, `Pending` at `03:33:31.220982Z`, which is where that capture ends; its completion is in `w3-2/teardown.txt`** (30 `logs` rows / `line_count` 30, object archived `03:34:23`), and the two are cited separately rather than the trigger being read as the success. nginx logged `upstream timed out … while connecting to upstream … 172.20.0.4:8080` for the dead replica but **zero** `no live upstreams` and **zero** 502s, because `proxy_next_upstream` covers `timeout` (`test/ha/nginx.conf:24`). A single dead replica is invisible to callers — which is exactly why the severity argument cannot lean on "the cluster went down", and why this is filed minor.
- **Expected:** **the documented contract is silent on the case that fires, and it is silent next to a paragraph that documents the identical behaviour for a different dependency.** Full survey captured with hit counts printed, per the W3-3 method rule (`w3-2/docs-greps.txt`: **231 / 54 / 210 / 27** hits for the four greps, untruncated; `w3-2/docs-startup-contract.txt` for the targeted one). Reading the whole surrounding section, `docs/high-availability.md:293-333` "External Dependency Redundancy", rather than a line:
  - **`:309-310`, the S3 subsection:** "The controller starts without S3, but log archival is disabled (`no object store configured — log archival disabled`). S3 is required for HA operation." **This is not contradicted, and the reason is the parenthetical**: it quotes the *unconfigured* branch's warning (`cmd/controller/main.go:321`), so the sentence is about `UNIFIED_S3_*` being **absent** — which is true, and which W3-4 measured on the pre-Garage rig. It says nothing about a configured-but-unreachable store. **But the second sentence is NOT scoped away and this entry originally read straight past it: "S3 is required for HA operation" is unconditional, it is in the HA guide, and the rig under test is an HA deployment — so the governing doc says S3 is *required* here.** `docs/configuration.md:68` says the opposite for the same variable (`UNIFIED_S3_ENDPOINT`, Required: **No**, "Without S3, log archival and artifacts are disabled"), which is a **contradiction between two published statements** rather than a scope difference: one is a global reference table and the other is a deployment-mode requirement, and nothing in either says which wins. **Consequence for this entry, applied throughout: the "optional subsystem kills the whole process" framing is withdrawn** (see `:1716`), because under `:310` a fail-closed start is the same design blessed for Vault three paragraphs later. The finding narrows to what `:310` cannot excuse — that the behaviour is never stated, that the two references disagree, and that the *running* replica does not fail closed at all.
  - **`:322-325`, the Vault subsection, three paragraphs later in the same section:** "**The controller fails closed.** It will not start if Vault is unreachable, so a Vault outage during a rollout crash-loops pods until Vault returns. This is deliberate: a controller that started without a key manager would fail every secret operation anyway." **The document therefore knows how to say this, says it for Vault, and does not say it for S3** — while, for S3, saying something that reads as reassurance in a section whose whole subject is what happens when the dependency is unavailable. `grep -rn -iE "starts without|will not start|fails closed|crash-?loop|does not start|cannot start" docs/*.md` returns **6** lines and only `:322-323` covers a start-time dependency failure.
  - **The rollout consequence is real but is recorded as context, not as a contradicted promise.** `docs/high-availability.md:271-279` describes the drain sequence and concludes "This enables one-at-a-time rolling deploys with no downtime." That sentence is about the *shutdown* half and is accurate as written, so it is **not** cited as broken; what it does establish is that one-at-a-time restarts are the documented operating procedure, and during an object-store outage that procedure removes every replica permanently, one at a time, with no warning in the S3 section that it would.
  - **Checked for a prior ruling by this branch before filing**, per the Task-3 lesson: `grep -rn "high-availability.md:3" test/edgecase/` returns citations of `:341`, `:352-356`, `:371-399`, `:382-384`, `:393-395`, `:397-399`, `:511` and `:536` and **no** prior reading of `:305-325` by any wave (`w3-2/docs-startup-contract.txt`).
  - So: **silent on the case that fires, and the finding rests on I5** — but "not contradicted" is now too generous to the docs and is narrowed. The doc gaps are **two**: the Vault paragraph has no S3 counterpart, and `docs/high-availability.md:310` ("S3 is required for HA operation") and `docs/configuration.md:68` (Required: **No**) say opposite things about the same variable with nothing to arbitrate them. Neither gap is filed as the contradicted-contract limb — this entry stays on I5 — but the second one is why the "optional subsystem" framing is withdrawn at `:1716`, `:1729` and `:1734`(a).
- **Notes:** **cross-reference, not a re-filing — and read this before merging it into W0-1.** `FINDINGS.md:43` (W0-1, I5, minor) is the same shape against Postgres: a start-time dependency check with no retry, exit(1), and a cascade. Three things are new here and are why this is a separate entry rather than a second instance appended to that one. **(a) The dependency's published status is self-contradictory, which is the corrected form of what this clause used to say.** ~~"The dependency is documented as optional … an operator reasonably reads an optional subsystem's outage as degrading that subsystem."~~ Postgres is mandatory and obviously so, and that contrast still holds — but S3's status is not "optional", it is **stated both ways**: `docs/configuration.md:68` marks `UNIFIED_S3_ENDPOINT` **Required: No** while `docs/high-availability.md:310` says "S3 is required for HA operation". An operator who reads the configuration reference budgets a degraded subsystem; an operator who reads the HA guide budgets a hard dependency; **neither is told that a configured-but-unreachable store is fatal at start and free at runtime**, which is the actual behaviour and the thing no sentence in either file describes. The separate-entry justification is unaffected — if anything it is stronger, because the contradiction is a second, independent doc defect W0-1's entry has no counterpart for. **(b) The blast radius is the whole process, not the subsystem.** The object store gates exactly two goroutines (`cmd/controller/main.go:399-402`, the archiver and the cache cleaner) plus three HTTP handlers, all of which already have a documented no-store behaviour (503, `internal/controller/api_artifacts.go:21-24`) — so the code demonstrably knows how to run without object storage, and chooses to die instead when the same store is configured but unreachable. **(c) The temporal asymmetry** in the Observed section: tolerated for 7.5 minutes by a running process, fatal in 4 seconds to a starting one, with `/readyz` 200 throughout the former. **Cheapest fixes, and they are not equivalent:** (i) retry `BucketExists` with a bounded backoff before exiting, which matches what W0-1 asked for on the DB path and would cover the boot-race case but not a long outage; (ii) treat an unreachable-but-configured store the way an unconfigured one is already treated — log an error, leave the object routes answering 503, and re-check periodically — which is the only option that makes a rolling deploy safe during an S3 outage; (iii) at minimum, add the missing S3 counterpart to `docs/high-availability.md:309`, because today the section states the reassuring half and omits the operative one — **and in the same edit reconcile `:310`'s "S3 is required for HA operation" with `docs/configuration.md:68`'s Required: No, which currently contradict each other for the same variable.** If the intended answer is that S3 is required in HA and optional single-node, both places should say so; if the intended answer is (ii) above, `:310` is the sentence that changes. **Not the same as the companion observation below**, which is about what happens to a *running* archiver during the same outage.

## W3-2 — `docs/operations.md:53` promises that the sweep-failure backoff state "is held by the current leader only and resets on failover (each problem candidate is retried once, then re-excluded)" and that broken runs "can no longer starve archival … for everything newer"; **both clauses are false, measured live** — the state resets every *tick*, so two runs drew 20 attempts across all three replicas in 14.7 minutes where a single ladder gives 4, and a failing pass holds the cluster-wide archiver lock for its entire duration (≥ 17.46 s continuous, ~44 s per failing `Put`) during which no replica can archive a healthy run either; separately, the outage is invisible everywhere except one `slog.Error` line
- **Invariant:** **documented contract, contradicted on both of its clauses** — `docs/operations.md:53`, §"Sweep failure backoff", quoted verbatim under **Expected** before it is cited, per `:1509`. The contract is the primary limb because no campaign invariant reaches what breaks here: what is falsified is a *published promise about the mitigation*, not run accounting, execution count or archive integrity. **RECLASSIFIED AT BRANCH REVIEW, and the misfiling is recorded rather than quietly repaired, because it is the exact failure mode this file's own method rules exist to prevent.** This entry was filed as an **observation** whose Expected ruled "the docs are silent, not contradicted" — while `FINDINGS.md:1498` had handed this scenario the paragraph **by name** ("W3-2 should frame the finding on **that contract plus observed re-selection** … and should quote `:53` verbatim before citing it"), and while `:547`, `:575`, `:1490` and `:2078` all already treated `docs/operations.md:53` as the contract limb for exactly this defect shape. **Why the entry missed it, stated so the rule is transferable:** its four docs surveys (`w3-2/docs-greps.txt`) asked about the *object store* — endpoint/startup/archival-latency — and **none of them asked about backoff or sweeps**, so `operations.md:53` never entered the survey; and the already-ruled check greped only `"high-availability.md:3"`, which cannot surface a citation of a different file. **The rule: an entry handed a named contract by a checkpoint must grep for that citation specifically, and a docs survey scoped to the dependency will not find a paragraph filed under the sweep that uses it.** Every measurement below is unchanged; only the ruling on them is. **No campaign invariant is violated, and each is ruled rather than left implicit. I4 (log/artifact integrity)** — `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:51`, verbatim: "**Log/artifact integrity** — a Succeeded run's log line count matches what the workload emitted; no duplicates, no reordering; archives stay readable" — **held on every clause it reaches, and that is half the deliverable**: archival is strictly post-terminal, so an archival outage cannot touch a run, and the measurement says so rather than the code comment. **I1 held** (`:48`): 6 runs in phase 1, all `Succeeded`, none affected. **I5** (`:52`) is the invariant this entry is *about*, and it is **not** violated, for a reason worth stating: the system does return to steady state once the store returns (59.1 s and 89.1 s, measured), and **there is no documented bound for archival latency to measure against** — `docs/high-availability.md` carries bounds for leader re-election and the reapers and none for the archiver. What is recorded instead is the *shape* of the recovery and the cost of the retrying, both of which are larger than the code's own comments imply. **NOT I3** — the archiver's Postgres advisory lock is not a "mutex/semaphore/concurrency slot" in I3's sense (`:50`, verified via `mutex_holders`/`named_lock_slots`), and its holder is a live goroutine that never reaches a terminal state.
- **Severity:** **minor** — argued against this file's bands (`FINDINGS.md:6-8`), not defaulted, and the argument names the boundary at which it should be re-argued. **Not critical** (`:6`, "data loss / silent corruption / security"): nothing was lost or corrupted on any surface — all six phase-1 runs ended archived with `line_count` **30 = 30** `logs` rows and every object present (`w3-2/teardown.txt`), and I4 held end to end. **Not major** (`:7`, "incorrect visible behavior, unbounded recovery"): every run's outcome, status, step list and log content was correct throughout; the damage is confined to a subsystem with **no user-visible surface at all** (this entry's own census: no run field, no API, no audit row records archival state), and recovery *was* bounded once the store returned — 59.1 s and 89.1 s, i.e. by the accumulated backoff, not by anything unbounded. The 5× retry amplification is wasted request volume, and the ~15-minute cluster-wide starvation for a full 20-candidate batch is **derived** (20 × 44 s), not measured. **Minor** (`:8`, "diagnosability, docs gap") is where it lands: what is falsified is the *documented model of the mitigation*, and every operational consequence of the falsification is load and latency on a subsystem whose latency the docs bound nowhere. **This is the re-argument `:547` demanded and it lands on the same band, deliberately.** W2-1 filed the identical shape against the identical paragraph at **minor** while explicitly labelling it "code-read, not demonstrated live" and instructing that "a wave that observes the re-selection live (W3-2 will have the object store wired anyway) should re-argue the severity with the contract limb in hand". Live observation is what promotes this from observation to **violation** — the limb changed from inferred to measured — but it does not convert a subsystem-scoped cost into incorrect visible behaviour, so the band does not move. **The boundary, stated so a later wave does not have to rediscover it: with `--log-trim-days` set the archive becomes the read path for trimmed runs (`docs/operations.md:49`), and a starving archiver then delays the point at which a run becomes trimmable rather than merely delaying a copy. That configuration was not under test here. A wave that measures a full poison batch starving healthy archival on a trim-enabled cluster should re-argue this as major.**
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-2-s3-outage.md` Part A, phase 1 (plain `test/ha` + Garage, no interposer — deliberately, because the interposer's `proxy_read_timeout 300s` would bound the hang with *nginx's* configuration rather than unified-cd's). Executed 2026-07-31 `03:02:15Z – 03:32:26Z`. Evidence: `w3-2/partA-runs.txt`, `w3-2/partA-backoff.txt`, `w3-2/partA-controllers.txt`, `w3-2/partA-lockhold-1s.txt`, `w3-2/partA-lockholder.txt`, `w3-2/partA-recovery.txt`, `w3-2/partA2-stall.txt`, `w3-2/partA2-blast.txt`, `w3-2/gate-g4-archive-baseline.txt`, `w3-2/consolidated.txt`.
- **Observed:**
  - **The positive control first, because every negative below is meaningless without it.** Before any injection, `edge-tick` run `26e1f0fe` was archived **28.4 s** after going terminal — terminal at `02:57:25.023131634Z` (its `POST …/finish` 204 on controller1, `w3-2/phase1-all-logs.txt`), `archived_at` `02:57:53.470541`. **An earlier version of this entry said 59 s, which is the interval from the *trigger*** (`02:56:54.792222Z`, 58.7 s) and is the wrong comparand for an archival-latency baseline. The correction *strengthens* the contrast this entry draws: healthy archival lands on the **first** 30 s tick after the run goes terminal, while recovery from the outage below took **two to three** (59.1 s and 89.1 s). The row: `3038` bytes / `line_count` 30 / `max_seq` 30, the object `runs/26e1f0fe-…/logs.ndjson` in Garage at 3.0 KiB, and `{"msg":"archived Run logs","runId":"26e1f0fe…","bytes":3038}` on **controller1** (`w3-2/gate-g4-archive-baseline.txt`). `edge-tick` emits exactly **30** log lines; that constant is the I4 comparand throughout.
  - **The run is untouched, measured on four surfaces rather than inferred from "archival is post-terminal".** With Garage `kill-hard`ed at `03:02:15.838Z`, runs `6fa56fd8` and `8f2ffebb` both reached `Succeeded` at `03:02:47.42Z` and stayed exactly that through 8 minutes of failing archival: `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}` → `"status":"Succeeded"`; `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}/steps` → `"status":"Succeeded","exitCode":0`; `SELECT count(*) FROM logs` → **30** each; `GET /runs/{id}/logs/stats` → `{"count":30,…}` each; and `updated_at` unchanged at `03:02:47.423083+00` / `03:02:47.423661+00` at the end of the window (`w3-2/partA-runs.txt`, `w3-2/partA-backoff.txt`). Both stayed in the archiver's candidate set for the whole outage, verified by running `ListRunsNeedingArchival`'s predicate by hand.
  - **The only trace is the one `slog.Error`, and the census is the evidence.** Every controller log line naming either run id over the whole outage, grouped by `msg`: **50** `http request` (the API reads this scenario itself made) and **20** `failed to archive Run logs`. Zero `WARN`, zero `log archiver error`, zero `log archiver lock`. `audit_logs` holds **9** rows for the session and exactly **2** name either run — both `run.trigger` at creation; there is no archival action in the audit model at all. The agents' logs name the runs twice, both the ordinary `"running"` line. **Nothing on the run, nothing in the DB and nothing in any API records that its logs are unarchived** (`w3-2/partA-backoff.txt`).
  - **THE MEASUREMENT THE PLAN ASKED FOR — the retry rate scales with replica count, and the corrected fact is confirmed and then sharpened.** `bo := newFailureBackoff(time.Minute, time.Hour, 10_000)` is at `internal/controller/archiver.go:28`, **before** the loop at `:29`, so it is one instance per **process** (`internal/controller/failure_backoff.go:9-15`: "Leader-local by design: a failover or restart clears it, costing one retry per poison before it is re-excluded"). Over `03:03:37Z – 03:18:21Z` the two runs drew **20** `failed to archive Run logs` lines, attributed per line from the container prefix (`w3-2/partA-backoff.txt`):

    | replica | `6fa56fd8` | `8f2ffebb` | total |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | controller1 | 4 | 4 | 8 |
    | controller2 | 2 | 2 | 4 |
    | controller3 | 4 | 4 | 8 |

    So **each run was attempted 10 times in 14.7 minutes**. A single-process ladder from the same base (1, 2, 4, 8, 16 minutes — `failure_backoff.go:37-64`) would have given **4** over the same window (*derived*: attempts at t = 0, 1, 3, 7 minutes; the fifth falls at t = 15 min, which is outside the 14.73-minute window by 16 s — an earlier version said 5 and did not apply the window boundary). The extra factor is not "one retry per replica" as a one-off: **leadership rotates on every tick**, because `runArchiveAsLeader` takes the advisory lock and `defer release()`s it within the same pass (`archiver.go:39-52`), so the ten passes alternated `c1, c3, c1, c3, c1, c3, c2, c1, c3, c2` and each replica ran its own independent ladder. **THIS IS THE FALSIFICATION OF `docs/operations.md:53`'s FIRST CLAUSE, and it is stated as a falsification rather than left as a shape.** The paragraph says "The backoff state is held by the current leader **only** and resets **on failover** (each problem candidate is retried **once**, then re-excluded)". There is no durable leader for the state to be held by: `runArchiveAsLeader` takes and releases the advisory lock inside a single pass, so the holder changes between passes with no failure, no failover and no fault of any kind. The state therefore resets **every tick**, not on failover; each problem candidate was retried **ten** times, not once; and the parenthetical's cost model — one wasted retry per failover — understates the measured cost by an order of magnitude on a three-replica cluster. **The direction of the error is what makes it a contract violation rather than a doc imprecision: an operator reading `:53` budgets one extra attempt per failover and gets `N` ladders running permanently in parallel.** Per replica the ladder is exactly as documented — controller1 attempted `6fa56fd8` at `03:03:37`, `03:06:37`, `03:09:35`, `03:14:37`, matching its derived `retryAt` of +1, +2, +4 minutes — and **it is the rotation, not the backoff, that set the aggregate rate for the first three rounds**, because the **measured** rotation period is comparable to the early 60 s and 120 s windows: pass-to-pass spacing is **89–92 s** across the run (pass starts `03:03:37.44`, `03:05:06.89`, `03:06:37.83`, `03:08:06.68`, `03:09:35.76`, `03:11:07.26`, `03:13:07.68`, `03:14:37.25`, `03:16:07.84`, `03:17:37.81`), with a single **120 s** gap at the controller2 handoff. An earlier version said "~105 s", which is not a spacing that appears in the data. **Every replica pays a first attempt**: controller2 had never been leader before `03:13:07` and its exclusion set was empty when it got there.
  - **A failing pass holds the cluster-wide archiver lock for its entire duration**, which is the part with operational teeth. `logArchiverLockKey = 0x6C6F6761` = **1819240289** (`pg_locks.objid`, with `classid=0`, `objsubid=1`). **55 of 70** samples granted, all to the same pid, with the gaps being backoff windows (`w3-2/partA-lockhold-1s.txt`). **The sampler's own cadence is disclosed here rather than only at the checkpoint, because an earlier version of this bullet said "sampled at 1 s … 55 of 70" and a reader would take the window to be 70 s.** The poll asks for 1 s and the capture's header says `70s`, but the 70 rows run `03:16:54.068Z → 03:18:43.653Z` — a **109.6 s** span at a mean cadence of **1.59 s** (*derived* from the capture's own timestamps). The **55/70 granted fraction is unaffected**; the window it covers is ~110 s, not 70 s. Holders attributed to replicas by joining `pg_stat_activity.client_addr` against the container IPs (`w3-2/partA-lockholder.txt`: `232@172.20.0.6` = controller2, granted on **12 consecutive samples**, `03:19:54.502Z` → `03:20:11.963Z`). **That hold is `≥ 17.46 s` and the `≥` is load-bearing, not hedging: the capture's own header is "1s poll until 12 hits", so the sampler terminated on reaching its twelfth hit and the lock was still held when it stopped.** 17.46 s is a **censored lower bound**, and an earlier correction that restated it as a flat "17.46 s" — on the ground that "≥ 18 s" was not supported — fixed the wrong half: the `≥` was right and only the digit was wrong. **Each failing `Put` cost ~44 s** under `kill-hard` — the spacing between the two runs' failure lines within one pass is **42.8–45.2 s** every time (an earlier version said 44–47 s; the true range is lower at both ends, mean 44.2 s) — so a two-run pass held the lock ~89 s. **The batch limit is 20** (`archiver.go:55`): a full poison batch would hold the cluster-wide lock for **~15 minutes** — *derived* from 20 × 44 s, **not measured** — during which no replica can archive a healthy run either. `time.NewTicker`'s dropped ticks mean this does not queue up; it just starves. **THIS IS THE FALSIFICATION OF `docs/operations.md:53`'s SECOND CLAUSE, and it is the clause with the operational teeth.** The paragraph's stated *purpose* for the backoff is that a failing candidate is kept out of "the head of every oldest-first batch — a handful of broken runs **can no longer starve archival**, deletion, or resolution **for everything newer**". The measurement is the opposite on the archival limb: exclusion from the *candidate set* does not buy exclusion from the *lock*, because the cost of a poison run is paid **inside** the pass that selects it and the lock is cluster-wide and held for the whole of it. So a healthy run created after the broken ones is not archived while any replica is grinding through them — measured at ~89 s of continuous lock hold per two-run pass, with the lock granted on 55 of 70 samples across a ~110 s window, and *derived* at ~15 minutes for a full 20-candidate batch. **The two clauses fail together and reinforce each other:** because the state resets every tick, every replica re-selects the same poison runs and re-pays the same lock hold, so the mitigation the sentence describes does not merely fail to help — the mechanism that defeats it (per-tick rotation) multiplies the starvation it was written to prevent by the replica count. **What the sentence would need to be true is one of the two fixes in Notes** — a context deadline on the archiver's `Put`, or releasing the lock between candidates — neither of which is a backoff change.
  - **The hang shape costs more and is bounded by a third-party default, not by unified-cd.** `pause garage` (SIGSTOP, so the kernel completes the TCP handshake and nothing ever answers) held from `03:24:09.1Z` to `03:31:39.9Z` — **a window this runbook opened and closed itself, 7 m 30.8 s**. In it, run `9a778d91` drew **4** attempts, at `03:25:57.8`, `03:27:26.9`, `03:28:56.9` and `03:30:27.5` (controller1, controller3, controller1, controller3), and the lock was granted in **86 of 120** samples, in **~65 s bursts** by four different pids across two replicas (`w3-2/partA2-stall.txt`). **Two provenance labels this bullet owed and did not carry, both of the same kind as `:1753`'s.** (i) The sampler asks for 2 s and its header says `2s x 120 = 4 min`; the 120 rows actually run `03:24:49.9Z → 03:29:53.4Z` — **308.7 s at a ~2.59 s cadence**, so the window is **~5 min, not 4** (*derived* from the capture's own stamps; the 86/120 fraction is unaffected). (ii) **"~65 s" is *derived* from the runs of granted samples, not read off any field — and the last burst is a lower bound, because the sampler ended at its 120th sample with the lock still granted** (`03:29:53.381Z archiverlock=83@172.20.0.5/32`), the same censoring the 12-hit sampler produced at `:1753`. **There is no timeout anywhere in `internal/controller/archiver.go`** — `RunLogArchiver` receives the process context (`:19`) and `obj.Put` inherits it (`:96`) — so the ~65 s is minio-go's own `ResponseHeaderTimeout: time.Minute` (`minio-go/v7@v7.2.0/transport.go:52`, code-read). **The bound on a hung object store is a dependency's default that unified-cd neither sets nor documents.**
  - **Nothing else degrades, which is a large part of why this is an observation.** During the 7.5-minute stall: `/readyz` **200** on all three replicas, LB `/healthz` 200, and a run triggered at `03:31:04.4Z` reached `Succeeded` at `03:31:39.3Z` (`w3-2/partA2-blast.txt`). The stall is archival-only.
  - **Recovery is governed by the accumulated backoff, not by the 30 s interval — quote the right number.** Garage restored at `03:21:54.42Z`; the two runs were archived at `03:22:53.53Z` (**59.1 s**) and `03:23:23.53Z` (**89.1 s**), both by controller1, whose derived `retryAt` values were `03:22:37` and `03:23:20` — i.e. its next tick after each window expired (`w3-2/partA-recovery.txt`). Note the counter-intuitive corollary, visible in the same data: **controller2's exclusions had already expired** (only 2 failures each, `retryAt` `03:19:37` / `03:20:21`), so the replica that had participated *least* was the readiest to recover — it simply did not win a tick. After the `pause` arm the in-flight `Put` completed **inside the unpause itself**: archived at `03:31:39.72Z` against an unpause command that returned at `03:31:39.88Z`. All six phase-1 runs ended archived, `line_count` **30 = 30** `logs` rows for every one, every object present (`w3-2/teardown.txt`). **I4 held end to end.**
- **Expected: `docs/operations.md:53`, §"Sweep failure backoff", quoted in full before it is cited, per the method rule at `:1509`** — and it is quoted in full because both halves of the sentence pair matter and quoting either alone would misrepresent it: "**Sweep failure backoff.** The log archiver, run-retention sweeper, and git resolver retry a persistently failing candidate with exponential backoff (1 min doubling to 1 h) instead of letting it occupy the head of every oldest-first batch — a handful of broken runs can no longer starve archival, deletion, or resolution for everything newer. The backoff state is held by the current leader only and resets on failover (each problem candidate is retried once, then re-excluded)." **This is a published paragraph in `docs/`, it names the log archiver first and by name, and this scenario is the log archiver under exactly the condition it describes — a persistently failing candidate.** It carries two independent assertions and **both are contradicted by measurement above.** **(1) Scope and reset — "held by the current leader only and resets on failover … retried once".** Measured (`:1752`): leadership is a per-tick advisory lock that `runArchiveAsLeader` releases inside the same pass, so ten passes ran on `c1, c3, c1, c3, c1, c3, c2, c1, c3, c2`, each replica kept its own independent ladder, and **each of the two runs was attempted 10 times in 14.7 minutes against the 4 a single ladder gives**. The state resets **every tick**, not on failover, and the candidate is retried **N ladders' worth of times**, not once. **(2) Purpose — "a handful of broken runs can no longer starve archival … for everything newer".** Measured (`:1753`): the cost of a poison candidate is paid **inside** the pass that selects it, under a **cluster-wide** advisory lock held for the whole pass — 55 of 70 samples granted over a ~110 s window, one hold of **≥ 17.46 s** continuous, ~44 s per failing `Put`, ~89 s per two-run pass, *derived* ~15 minutes for the batch limit of 20 — during which **no replica can archive a healthy run either**. Excluding a candidate from the *set* does not exclude it from the *lock*. **What is NOT claimed:** the exponential ladder itself is real and correct — per replica the intervals matched +1, +2, +4 minutes exactly (`:1752`) — so the defect is the paragraph's two claims about that ladder's scope and effect, not its existence; and `:53`'s run-retention-sweeper and git-resolver limbs are **not** measured here (the sweeper is the same construct at `run_retention.go:35-38` and the resolver is `:2078`'s open item, both code-read only). **On the limbs where the docs really are silent, they are recorded as silent and nothing is stretched to reach them:** the full survey (`w3-2/docs-greps.txt`, hit counts **231 / 54 / 210 / 27** printed in the capture, untruncated) turns up **no bound on archival latency** — which is exactly why the I5 limb above is ruled *not* violated — **no statement about how an archival failure is surfaced**, and **nothing about what an object-store outage costs a running controller**; `docs/operations.md:14` names the object store as holding "Log archives, artifacts, cache entries, git-template cache" and stops there. **The `slog.Error`-only invisibility half of this entry therefore stands on the silence and remains an observation in substance; the backoff and lock-hold half stands on `:53` and is the violation.** They are kept in one entry because they are one measurement of one outage and splitting them would double-count it — the tally counts this entry **once**, as a violation.
- **Notes:** the operational point is that **the failure is loud enough to grep for and invisible to everything else** — an operator alerting on `msg="failed to archive Run logs"` catches it immediately; an operator looking at runs, steps, `/readyz`, the API or the audit log never will, and there is no query that answers "which runs are unarchived and for how long" other than reconstructing `ListRunsNeedingArchival`'s predicate by hand. Three cheap improvements, in order of value: (i) a metric or a periodic summary line for the size of the candidate set, so a backlog is observable without log scraping; (ii) a context deadline on the archiver's `Put`/`Delete`, so the cluster-wide lock cannot be held for a minute per poison run by a dependency's default — with 20 candidates that is a quarter of an hour of starvation for healthy runs; (iii) release the lock between candidates, or abandon the rest of a batch after the first S3 connection failure, since the remaining 19 attempts are known-futile and cost ~44 s each. **Cross-references, not re-filings.** This is the controller→object-store hop and overlaps none of the four already-filed agent→controller log-delivery shapes: **W1-2** (loss at the 5 s step-end `Flush` budget), **W1-6** (quadratic request amplification), **F9** (loss at the 1 MiB `p.pending` cap) and **W3-4** (permanent duplicate surplus). Nothing is lost or duplicated here at all — the logs stay in `logs` and are simply never trimmed, which is the behaviour `archiver.go` intends. **THE `docs/operations.md:53` LEDGER, RECONCILED IN ONE PLACE SO THE BRANCH STOPS SAYING TWO THINGS ABOUT ONE PARAGRAPH.** That single sentence pair covers **three** sweeps and the branch now holds a different standing for each. **Log archiver — THIS ENTRY, measured live, contract contradicted on both clauses, filed as a violation.** This closes the instruction at `:1498` and discharges the deferral at `:547` ("a wave that observes the re-selection live … should re-argue the severity with the contract limb in hand"); `:547`'s own claim remains correctly labelled **code-read**, because W2-1 could not observe it on an unwired archiver, and it is that entry's standing, not its limb, that this one changes. **Run-retention sweeper — still code-read only.** `internal/controller/run_retention.go:35-38` is the identical construct with the identical comment; nothing in W3 injected a failing *deletion* candidate, and W3-6 exercised the sweeper's success path only. It is not filed as a second violation and must not be double-counted against `:53` — one paragraph, one contradicted sentence pair, one entry, with two further limbs open. **Git resolver — still open and still unmeasured**, carried by `:2078` item 2, which is correct that the resolver's instance needs the git-over-HTTP fixture W3 did not build. **What `:2078` must no longer say is that the archiver's instance is unfiled: it is filed here.** `:1438` is untouched by all of this and stays right — the *scheduler* is deliberately not one of `:53`'s three sweeps, so W2-9 correctly scoped the paragraph away rather than citing it.

## W3-2 — observation: the archiver's object/record compensation works exactly as its comment claims, but the compensating delete is best-effort against the same store that just failed, and when it fails the orphan it leaves is unreachable by every code path in the tree; separately and independently actionable, the archiver's `Put` body is a non-rewindable `bytes.Buffer`, so minio-go's retry is dead on this path and every hung upload reports a `ContentLength` mismatch that names no outage (one-line fix)
- **Invariant:** none violated. **I4 (log/artifact integrity)** — `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:51`, verbatim: "**Log/artifact integrity** — a Succeeded run's log line count matches what the workload emitted; no duplicates, no reordering; **archives stay readable**" — **held, and the "archives stay readable" clause was the one under attack.** The state that would break it is a `run_log_archives` row pointing at an object that is not there, and the code's claim is that no such state is constructible because the object is written first and the record second. Both produced interleavings confirm it: when the record failed, **no row was created**, so no reader was ever pointed at anything missing. The reverse state — an object with no row — is an **orphan**, not an unreadable archive, and is judged as such below. **I1 held.** **NOT I2**: the archiver is idempotent by construction (`CreateLogArchive` is `ON CONFLICT (run_id) DO UPDATE`, `internal/store/postgres.go:1519-1527`) and this was verified live, so a re-archival overwrites rather than duplicates; per the W2 correction nothing here is a zero-vs-once shape either.
- **Severity:** minor (observation)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-2-s3-outage.md` Part B, phase 2 (`test/ha` + `compose/s3proxy.override.yaml` + `compose/bigbody.override.yaml`). Executed 2026-07-31 `03:41:09Z – 03:47:29Z`. Evidence: `w3-2/partB-arm1.txt` (the failed lever), `w3-2/partB-arm1b.txt`, `w3-2/partB-arm2-setup.txt`, `w3-2/partB-arm2.txt`, `w3-2/partB-orphan.txt`, `w3-2/partB-integrity.txt`, `w3-2/partB-pause.sh` (the driver).
- **Observed:**
  - **METHOD, labelled rather than glossed, because two things about it are synthetic.** (i) The `runs` row was deleted with **raw SQL** timed into the archiver's `Put`, not through `DELETE /api/v1/runs/{id}`. In production only `deleteRunEverywhere` removes a `runs` row, so the *interleaving* is realistic, but the *instrument* is not the product path — and the API route was avoided deliberately, because its own object deletes would have collided with arm 2's `DELETE` block and mixed two mechanisms in one measurement. (ii) The window was opened by **pausing Garage behind the interposer** so the `Put` hung, then unpausing once the row was gone. **The `s3-latency` lever the runbook planned for did not work, and its failure is recorded rather than hidden**: at `s3-latency 30` the black hole `192.0.2.1:3900` answered `connect() failed (111: Connection refused)` instead of hanging, `proxy_next_upstream timeout` does not cover a refusal, and the `Put` got a **502 after 21.037 s** rather than a delay (`w3-2/partB-arm1.txt`). Task 3's measurement of the same verb at `s3-latency 3` is not withdrawn — at 3 s the connect deadline expires before any ICMP unreachable arrives — but the verb is **not dependable at large values**, and `test/edgecase/README.md` now says so.
  - **The in-flight signal was the advisory lock, not a guess.** The driver polls `pg_locks` for `objid` 1819240289 at 1 s and fires the `DELETE` after three consecutive granted samples, i.e. only while `archiveRunLogs` is demonstrably inside its `Put`. Both arms hit on the first attempt with that lever, against a cap of 6 (arm 1 total attempts: **2**, one with the failed `s3-latency` lever and one with `pause`; arm 2: **1**).
  - **Arm 1 — compensation succeeds, and no trace is left anywhere.** Run `56904219`, `Succeeded` at `03:44:01Z`; row deleted at `03:44:27.87Z` while the `Put` was in flight; Garage unpaused at `03:44:29.0Z`. The interposer's per-request bracket shows the whole sequence for that key (`w3-2/partB-arm1b.txt`):

        03:44:29 200 arm=none method=PUT    uri=/unified-cd-logs/runs/56904219-…/logs.ndjson ustatus=200 rt=4.862
        03:44:29 204 arm=none method=DELETE uri=/unified-cd-logs/runs/56904219-…/logs.ndjson ustatus=204 rt=0.001

    and controller3 logged exactly the failure the code comment predicts:

        {"level":"ERROR","msg":"failed to archive Run logs","runId":"56904219-…","error":"failed to create archive record: ERROR: insert or update on table \"run_log_archives\" violates foreign key constraint \"run_log_archives_run_id_fkey\" (SQLSTATE 23503)"}

    Afterwards: **0** `run_log_archives` rows for it and **no object** under `runs/56904219-…/`. The object was written and then removed by the compensating `obj.Delete` at `internal/controller/archiver.go:111`. This is race "(a)" from `internal/controller/run_retention.go:115-119`, produced live.
  - **Arm 2 — the compensation itself fails, and the orphan stays.** Same procedure with `inject.sh s3-block DELETE unified-cd-logs/ 403` additionally armed (`PUT` on the same prefix probed as **not** blocked first, `w3-2/partB-arm2-setup.txt`). Run `59404ade`, `Succeeded` at `03:45:37Z`, row deleted `03:45:59.27Z`, unpaused `03:46:00.4Z`. Bracket:

        03:46:00 200 arm=block[DELETE /unified-cd-logs/ -> 403] method=PUT    uri=/unified-cd-logs/runs/59404ade-…/logs.ndjson ustatus=200 rt=6.267
        03:46:00 403 arm=block[DELETE /unified-cd-logs/ -> 403] method=DELETE uri=/unified-cd-logs/runs/59404ade-…/logs.ndjson ustatus=-   rt=0.000

    controller3 logged the `WARN` the code provides for exactly this, immediately before the same FK `ERROR`:

        {"level":"WARN","msg":"failed to clean up orphaned log archive object after CreateLogArchive failure","runId":"59404ade-…","key":"runs/59404ade-…/logs.ndjson","error":"delete object \"runs/59404ade-…/logs.ndjson\": Access Denied."}

    **The object survives**: `runs/59404ade-091e-430a-af66-c9e1277b5511/logs.ndjson`, 3.0 KiB, still listed by `mc` after the arm was cleared (`w3-2/partB-orphan.txt`).
  - **The orphan is unreachable, on three surfaces plus an argument.** `runs` rows for it: **0**. `run_log_archives` rows: **0**. `logs` rows: **0** (the FK cascade took them). `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}` → **404**. **A fourth surface disagrees with the other three and is recorded rather than dropped:** `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}/logs` returns **200** for the deleted run (`w3-2/partB-orphan.txt`) — an empty body, because the `logs` rows went with the cascade, but a 200 where the run route 404s. It is not a fifth route to the orphan object (the handler reads `logs`, not the bucket) and so does not weaken the unreachability claim; what it does mean is that the surface count for "this run is gone" is three of four, and a caller polling the logs endpoint sees success rather than absence. And nothing will ever collect the object: `ListExpiredRuns` is driven off `runs` rows (`internal/store/postgres.go:1494-1502`) and the row is gone; `deleteRunEverywhere`'s post-`DeleteRun` re-sweep (`internal/controller/run_retention.go:167-170`) — the step that exists precisely to catch an object written during a deletion window — can only fire for a run it is *currently* deleting; and there is no orphan-object reconciler anywhere in the tree. **That last limb is code-read and is labelled as such**: what was produced live is the orphan, not a demonstration that the re-sweep would have caught it.
  - **The positive integrity result, recorded as a pass rather than an absence** (`w3-2/partB-integrity.txt`). Every `run_log_archives` row in the session had its object present at the recorded `size_bytes` (432/432 and 292/292), and one archive was read back end to end through `mc cat` — three well-formed ndjson records with matching `seq`, `timestamp` and `line`, i.e. **readable**, not merely present. Idempotency was verified live: the record for `a0ee8ebc` was deleted while its object stayed, and the next tick re-created it **~29 s** later with identical `size_bytes` / `line_count` / `max_seq` and a fresh `archived_at`, confirming the `ON CONFLICT` path.
  - **A third, separate defect surfaced twice on this path and deserves its own paragraph: the archiver's `Put` body cannot be retried.** `archiveRunLogs` encodes into a `bytes.Buffer` and passes `&buf` to `obj.Put` (`archiver.go:86-96`); `*bytes.Buffer` is not an `io.Seeker`, so when minio-go re-sends the request after its 60 s `ResponseHeaderTimeout` the body is already drained and Go's transport reports `readfrom tcp …: http: ContentLength=<n> with Body length 0`. **The digits differ by capture and are given per capture, because an earlier version quoted one file's number against the other's citation** — which this scenario's own recording rule forbids: `w3-2/partA2-stall.txt` reads **`ContentLength=3070`** on all four `pause`-arm attempts, and **`ContentLength=3047`** appears only in `w3-2/partB-arm1.txt`, the failed `s3-latency` lever. The phenomenon is identical in both and the difference is just the two runs' log sizes. Two consequences. **The client library's retry is dead on this path** — the second attempt can only ever fail, so the effective retry count inside one `Put` is **1** no matter how minio-go is configured. **THAT "1" IS AN INFERENCE AND IS LABELLED AS ONE, BECAUSE ITS NEIGHBOUR AT `:1754` IS LABELLED CODE-READ AND THIS ONE WAS NOT.** It is composed from two captured/read inputs — the code fact that `&buf` is a `*bytes.Buffer` and therefore not an `io.Seeker` (`archiver.go:86-96`, code-read), and the observed symptom that every hung attempt ends in `ContentLength=<n> with Body length 0` (captured) — and **no capture in this wave instruments minio-go's retry counter directly**; nothing prints "attempts=N". The inference is strong and the fix does not depend on it, but a reader must not cite the 1 as a measurement. And **the operator-visible error names no outage**: `kill-hard` produced the clear `dial tcp: lookup garage … no such host`, but every *hang* produced a `ContentLength` mismatch that reads like a unified-cd serialization bug. The one-line fix is to pass `bytes.NewReader(buf.Bytes())`, which is an `io.Seeker` and lets minio-go rewind.
- **Expected:** as-designed, and unusually well-designed — `run_retention.go:111-130` names both races, says which one it closes and why, and `archiver.go:107-110` explains the compensating delete in terms of the exact FK failure this scenario reproduced. **The docs are silent, not contradicted:** the full survey (`w3-2/docs-greps.txt`, hit counts printed) contains no promise about orphaned objects, storage bounds, or what happens when a compensating delete fails; `docs/operations.md:14` only names what the bucket holds.
- **Notes:** the structural point is that **the compensation is best-effort against the store that just failed**, and in the general case the two failures are correlated — the trigger here was a DB fault, but in the `Put`-succeeds / `Delete`-fails shape the object store is the common dependency, and a store refusing writes is a plausible store to refuse deletes. The `WARN` at `archiver.go:112-114` is the only record that a specific key leaked, and it is a log line: once it rotates, the key is unrecoverable except by diffing the bucket against `run_log_archives`, which nothing ships. **FOR W3-6 (Task 5), WHICH OWNS THE ORPHAN FINDING — cross-reference this, do not re-file it.** W3-6's subject is the artifact-side asymmetry (step 3 of `deleteRunEverywhere` runs once, before `DeleteRun`, with no post-delete re-sweep). What W3-2 contributes is a **second, independent production path for the same class**, on the log-archive side, where the re-sweep exists but the object escapes it anyway because the run row is already gone. The two should be one finding about "no orphan-object reconciler exists" with two producers, not two findings.

## W3-2 — observation: a blocked artifact upload fails the step and the run exactly as it should, and then no surface anywhere names the cause — zero controller `WARN`/`ERROR`, zero lines in the run's own log, an agent error of `upload artifact http 500` from a client that never reads the body, and a failed step reporting `"exitCode":0`
- **Invariant:** none violated. **I4 (log/artifact integrity)** — `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:51` — **held, and the check was the point**: the violation would have been a `Succeeded` run with a missing artifact, and what happened is a `Failed` run with a correctly-absent artifact. **I1 held** (`:48`): the run reached exactly one terminal state. **NOT I2** (`:49`): the upload is executed once and never retried (`internal/agent/orchestrator.go:815`, not wrapped in `retryUntilSuccess`), which is a zero-or-once shape, and per the W2 correction that does not violate at-most-once. Filed, like W3-3's and W3-4's companion entries, against the **absence** of an **I7** hook (`:54`, "run status, approval status, and audit rows never contradict each other or reality"): nothing states an untruth — the run really failed and the step really is the one that failed — but the union of every surface cannot say **why**.
- **Severity:** minor (observation)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-2-s3-outage.md` Part C, phase 2 (`test/ha` + `s3proxy` + **`bigbody`**, the last of which is required or the 64 MiB body dies at the LB with 413 before a controller sees it). Arm: `inject.sh s3-block PUT unified-cd-logs/artifacts/ 403` — **403, not 503, chosen deliberately**, since minio-go retries 503 internally up to ten times and this arm measures a one-shot policy, not a retry policy. Executed 2026-07-31 `03:38:57Z – 03:40:26Z`, runs `a0ee8ebc` (control) and `5e49c972` (armed). Evidence: `w3-2/partC-control.txt`, `w3-2/partC-arm.txt`, `w3-2/partC-fail.txt`.
- **Observed:**
  - **The control, so the failure has something to be a delta from.** Unarmed, `edge-artifact-large` (64 MiB of `/dev/urandom`) `Succeeded`: step `upload_blob` **0.813 s**, controller `PUT /api/v1/runs/…/artifacts/big-blob` → **204** in **807 ms**, `GET /runs/{id}/artifacts` → `[{"name":"big-blob"}]`, and the object present at **64 MiB**. The interposer shows a 64 MiB Put is **3** S3 requests (`POST ?uploads=`, `PUT` with `reqlen=67203925`, `POST ?uploadId=`), all `arm=none ustatus=200` (`w3-2/partC-control.txt`).
  - **The arm was confirmed per request and per prefix, not per wall clock.** The verb's own blocked probe returned **403** and its control probe **200**; two extra probes confirmed the fault is prefix-scoped — `PUT /unified-cd-logs/runs/…` (log archival) and `PUT /unified-cd-cache/caches/…` (the **agent's** own S3 client) both **200** (`w3-2/partC-arm.txt`).
  - **The failure is correct on every state surface.** Run `5e49c972` → `"status":"Failed"`. Steps → `make_blob` `Succeeded`, **`upload_blob` `Failed`**, `after_upload` `Skipped`. `GET /runs/{id}/artifacts` → `[]`. Created `03:39:44.39Z`, terminal `03:39:45.29Z`.
  - **Exactly one upload attempt, bracketed from the controller's own access log.** One `PUT /api/v1/runs/5e49c972-…/artifacts/big-blob`, **status 500**, `duration_ms` 160 — `grep -c` over all three controllers for the window: **1**. That confirms `orchestrator.go:815` / `:401-404` live: no retry at the step level, none inside `Client.UploadArtifact` (`internal/agent/client.go:349-371`), and none at the caller.
  - **The failure is clean at the object layer too.** The interposer bracket for the run's key is three lines: `POST ?uploads=` **200**, `PUT` **403** with `arm=block[PUT /unified-cd-logs/artifacts/ -> 403]` stamped on the request, then `DELETE` **204** — minio-go aborted its own multipart upload. Result: **no** object under `artifacts/5e49c972-…/`, and `mc ls --incomplete` over the whole bucket is empty (`w3-2/partC-fail.txt`).
  - **AND NOW THE OBSERVATION. Nothing anywhere says why.** Four surfaces, each checked:
    - **The controller logged no error at all.** `handleArtifactUpload` does `http.Error(w, err.Error(), http.StatusInternalServerError)` (`internal/controller/api_artifacts.go:79-81`) and **no `slog` call whatsoever**. Grep over all three controllers for the window: **zero** `"level":"WARN"` and **zero** `"level":"ERROR"` lines. The only controller-side trace of the whole event is one INFO access line carrying `"status":500`.
    - **The run's own log contains nothing about it.** `SELECT step_index, stream, ts, line FROM logs WHERE run_id='5e49c972-…'` returns **2** rows, both `stdout` from `make_blob`. `executeUploadArtifact` reports the failure through the agent's `slog`, not through the run's log stream, so **there is no `stepIndex: 1` line of any kind** — not even the content-free one W3-3's secret-fetch path at least produced.
    - **The agent's error names a status and nothing else**, and the mechanism is *different* from the wave's `"response omitted"` fact, which matters for the fix. `agent2` logged `{"level":"ERROR","msg":"upload-artifact failed","step":"upload_blob","error":"upload artifact http 500"}` and then `{"level":"ERROR","msg":"upload artifact failed","step":"upload_blob","error":"upload-artifact \"big-blob\": upload artifact http 500"}`. `Client.UploadArtifact` does **not** go through `Client.do`: it hand-builds the request and returns a bare `fmt.Errorf("upload artifact http %d", resp.StatusCode)` at `internal/agent/client.go:369` (**the campaign plan cites `:367-368`; at HEAD the statement is at `:369`, inside the `if` at `:368`**). So the body is not *redacted* by the `"response omitted"` policy (`client.go:107-108`) — **it is never read at all.** The controller's 500 body did carry the real message (`put object "artifacts/…": Access Denied.`, **derived** from the interposer's 403 plus `api_artifacts.go:80`'s `err.Error()`; **the body was not captured on the wire and could not be**, since the only client that reaches this route discards it). Two independent mechanisms produce the same content-free result on two different paths, and a fix for one does not fix the other.
    - **The step report says `Failed` with `"exitCode":0`.** An `uploadArtifact` step has no process, so `ReportStep` sends no exit code (`orchestrator.go:817-820`) and the zero value is serialized. An operator reading `GET /runs/{id}/steps` sees a failed step that exited successfully.
    - **The WebUI was NOT opened** for this scenario; every surface above was read via the HTTP API, the interposer/controller logs and the database.
  - **The contrast the plan flagged is half measured.** A **configured-but-down** store gives **500** (`api_artifacts.go:79-81`) — measured here. An **unconfigured** store gives **503** "object store not configured" (`:21-24`) — **CODE-READ ONLY**, and it has not been measured anywhere in this campaign: producing it needs a controller with `UNIFIED_S3_*` unset, which neither of this scenario's stacks has, and W3-4, which did run on such a rig, never uploaded an artifact. Recorded so nobody reads the pairing as jointly verified. The agent-side texts would differ only in the digits — `upload artifact http 500` versus `upload artifact http 503`.
- **Expected:** as-designed on the failure semantics: a one-shot upload that fails the step and the run is a reasonable policy for a step whose whole purpose is the upload, and `handleArtifactUpload` returning the store's error in the response body is the right thing to do for a caller that reads it. **The docs are silent, not contradicted** — the full survey (`w3-2/docs-greps.txt`, **231 / 54 / 210 / 27** hits printed with the output, untruncated) contains no promise about artifact-upload retry and none about how an upload failure is reported; `docs/configuration.md:452` covers only the sidecar case ("Without it, cache steps are no-ops (Succeeded) and artifact steps fail").
- **Notes:** the diagnosability delta against **W3-3's** secret-fetch entry is why this is filed rather than folded in, and it runs the wrong way: W3-3's path at least produced a controller-side `WARN` naming the cause and one `stderr` line in the run's log. **Here there is neither** — no controller log line, no run log line — on a failure that actually terminates the run. Three cheap fixes, independent of each other: (i) a `slog.Warn` in `handleArtifactUpload`'s `Put` error branch naming the run, the key and the store error, which is the single missing line that would make this attributable; (ii) have `Client.UploadArtifact` read a bounded prefix of the error body — this route is controller-only, so the `"response omitted"` hygiene argument that justifies `client.do`'s policy does not apply with the same force — or at minimum emit the failure into the run's own log stream the way `orchestrator.go:175-184` does for secret fetch; (iii) omit `exitCode` from a step report that has no process rather than serializing 0. **Cross-references, not re-filings.** The `"response omitted"` fact belongs to **W3-3** (`FINDINGS.md:1593`) and is *not* the mechanism here — that distinction is this entry's sharpest point. **W3-6 (Task 5)** owns the artifact-orphan question; this entry establishes the clean-failure baseline it will need (a blocked `PUT` leaves no object and no incomplete multipart upload).

## W3-6 — `deleteRunEverywhere` re-sweeps the log-archive key after `DeleteRun` to catch a write that lands during its own deletion window, and has no equivalent re-sweep of `artifacts/<runID>/`: an artifact uploaded in that window survives the deletion of its run, still downloadable in full, with no reconciler anywhere in the tree — while `docs/configuration.md:84` and `docs/operations.md:47` promise unconditionally that a deleted run's artifacts are deleted
- **Invariant:** **documented contract**, contradicted. `docs/configuration.md:84` (`UNIFIED_RUN_RETENTION_DAYS`): "When a run expires, its database records **and** its object-store data (archived logs, artifacts) are deleted." `docs/operations.md:47` (§"Run retention"): "a sweep that deletes terminal runs older than N days, **including their archived logs and artifacts**." Neither sentence carries a carve-out, and both are implemented by the one function this entry breaks. On the invariant table: **I4 (log/artifact integrity)** — `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:51`, verbatim: "**Log/artifact integrity** — a Succeeded run's log line count matches what the workload emitted; no duplicates, no reordering; archives stay readable" — is **NOT** contradicted, and saying so is part of the finding: W3-2 already ruled on this branch that "the reverse state — an object with no record — does **NOT** break this clause. It is an orphan, not an unreadable archive" (`test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-2-s3-outage.md` §Invariants), and that ruling is inherited rather than re-argued. The object left behind here is perfectly readable; the defect is that it exists. **I1 held** (`:48`): all six runs of the session reached exactly one terminal state, `Succeeded`, before any of them was deleted (`w3-6/census.txt`: nine `run.trigger`/`run.delete` audit rows over six distinct ids, zero non-terminal runs). **The census alone cannot carry all six, and the citation is completed rather than left to be inferred:** it is taken after Parts A and C, so it can only evidence the three runs that survived; the three that were deleted are each recorded `Succeeded` at the moment they were used — `w3-6/partA-race.txt:1` (`d764f49f`), `w3-6/partC-calibrate.txt:2` (`332624bb`) and `w3-6/partC-asymmetry.txt:1` (`2ac4c212`). **NOT I2** (`:49`): nothing executes twice. **NOT I3**: no workload lock is involved, and the retention sweeper's advisory lock is not taken by the manual DELETE route at all. **NOT I5** (`:52`): nothing was injected and nothing had to recover — do not stretch "bounded recovery" over "the object is never collected". **NOT I7**: the surfaces question is filed separately below as an observation.
- **Severity:** minor
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-6-retention-vs-upload.md`, Parts A and C. Stack: `test/ha` + `compose/bigbody.override.yaml` (**required, both halves**: `client_max_body_size 0` or the upload dies at the LB with 413, and `proxy_request_buffering off` or nginx spools the whole body before opening the upstream request and the window does not exist at all). **No S3 interposer** — see the runbook's §Instrument for why `pause garage` cannot be used by a scenario that is timing a `DELETE` which itself calls `obj.Delete` and `obj.List`. Executed 2026-07-31, **measurement window** `04:36:29Z – 04:49:24Z` — from stack-up to Part C's `DELETE` returning; **this is not the session end.** The **session** ran to `04:57:27Z`, i.e. through teardown, which is the figure `:1835` scopes its negative against (`w3-6/teardown.txt` is stamped `04:57:16.881Z` and runs to `04:57:27.724Z`). Evidence: `w3-6/gate.txt`, `w3-6/gate-g6-synthagent.txt`, `w3-6/partA-width.txt`, `w3-6/partA-race.txt`, `w3-6/partA-bracket.txt`, `w3-6/partC-calibrate.txt`, `w3-6/partC-asymmetry.txt`, `w3-6/partB-surfaces.txt`, `w3-6/partB-reachability.txt`, `w3-6/census.txt`, `w3-6/docs-greps.txt`, `w3-6/docs-context.txt`; drivers `w3-6/synth.sh`, `w3-6/partA-race.sh`, `w3-6/partC-asymmetry.sh`.
- **Observed:**
  - **THE INSTRUMENT, DECLARED FIRST, BECAUSE IT IS THE LEAST OBVIOUS THING IN THE ENTRY — AND THE REACHABILITY ARGUMENT IS STRONGER THAN AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS ENTRY CLAIMED.** `DELETE /api/v1/runs/{id}` refuses a non-terminal run with **409** (`internal/controller/api_runs.go:427-432`), and a run driven by a real agent is `Running` for the whole of its `uploadArtifact` step *while nothing interferes*. An earlier draft concluded from that pair that "the two windows never overlap" and that `edge-artifact-large` executed normally "can never be raced". **That is false as a code fact, and the correction cuts in this finding's favour.** `handleCancelRun` marks the row terminal **synchronously** — `MarkRunFinished(r.Context(), id, api.RunCancelled)` at `internal/controller/api_runs.go:374` — so the run is deletable the instant the cancel returns, while the executing agent learns only by **polling**: `var CancelPollInterval = 5 * time.Second` (`internal/agent/orchestrator.go:37`), poller at `:122-147`, terminal branch at `:137-146`, which is also the branch that fires for a stuck-run reap or a failover-driven terminalisation (`reapedByMaster`). **An ordinary agent-driven run is therefore terminal-while-still-uploading for up to 5 s, and `DELETE /api/v1/runs/{id}` is legal for all of it** — through documented operator routes, with a real agent and no instrument whatever. What is genuinely unknown is whether the in-flight `Put` completes before the poller's `cancelRun()` tears down `runCtx`; **nobody tried, so that window is untested, not unreachable.** The synthetic agent was used to get a window that is as wide as the payload and repeatable on demand — not because it is the only way in. What makes the race constructible on that instrument is that `handleArtifactUpload` accepts uploads for **terminal-but-existing** runs on purpose (`agentRunGuard(..., rejectTerminal=false)`, `internal/controller/api_artifacts.go:47`, `internal/controller/agent_guard.go:98-132`). So the upload is issued by a **synthetic agent**: a curl-driven identity `w36-probe` that enrolls (`POST /api/v1/agent-enrollments` → `POST /api/v1/agents/enroll`), claims a run of a job no real agent can match (`agentSelector: [kind:w36probe]`, `test/edgecase/workloads/w36-probe.yaml`, verified through the real `dsl.Parse`, `w3-6/fixcheck.txt`), finishes it through `POST /api/v1/agents/{id}/runs/{runId}/finish`, and then uploads. **What is synthetic is who holds the credential, not what code runs** — the `PUT` lands on the same exported handler, passes the same guard and reaches the same `objStore.Put` as an agent's, and **no SQL and no product change is used to construct any state anywhere in this entry** — every row and object reported here was produced through documented HTTP routes, or, in Part C and labelled as an instrument, with `mc`; the `psql` `SELECT`s that appear throughout are observation only. The realistic production shape it stands in for is the ≤5 s window named above: a run terminalised out from under a live agent (cancel, stuck-run reap, failover) whose `uploadArtifact` step then still `PUT`s and is still accepted. **That shape was not attempted — untested, not unreachable — while the acceptance of a post-terminal upload was measured**: gate G6 confirmed it live before any race (`w3-6/gate-g6-synthagent.txt`). **G6 was two uploads, not one, and they must not be read as a single measurement:** the first (`gate-blob`) returned **204** while writing **0 B** — the MSYS path trap the runbook records as note 1 — and it is the upload the `GET /runs/{id}/artifacts` → `[{"name":"gate-blob"}]` listing was taken against; the **4.0 KiB** object is the post-fix re-upload (`gate-blob2`). What G6 establishes either way, and all this entry uses it for, is that a `PUT` to a terminal-but-existing run is accepted and listed.
  - **The window is exactly as wide as the payload, and it was measured before it was raced.** 32 MiB fed at `curl --limit-rate 1M` with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` and no `Content-Length` — the agent's own shape (`internal/agent/client.go:349-371`; the campaign plan's `:352-360` is wrong, and W3-2 filed the same correction) — produced a controller-side `duration_ms` of **32374** on a single `PUT` (`w3-6/partA-width.txt`). `handleArtifactUpload` does `size := r.ContentLength; if size < 0 { size = -1 }` and streams the body straight into `Put` (`api_artifacts.go:75-79`), so the whole of that 32.374 s sits between the run-existence check at `:55` and the completion of the `Put` at `:79`, with no lock, lease or re-check in between.
  - **Part A, hit on the FIRST attempt of a cap of 6.** Run `d764f49f`, `Succeeded` and claimed by `w36-probe` at `04:45:28.212Z`. The upload started at `04:45:28.245Z` (client stamp). **The DELETE was fired on a positive in-flight signal, not on a sleep**: `mc ls --incomplete` showed a multipart upload under `artifacts/d764f49f-…/` at `04:45:28.822Z`, which means the controller was already inside `objStore.Put`. `DELETE /api/v1/runs/d764f49f-…` was issued at `04:45:28.842Z` and returned **204** in 13 ms (`w3-6/partA-race.txt`). The upload then ran to completion and returned **204** with `upload_size=33558547` at `04:46:00.649Z`. **That figure and the stored object's 33554432 B are reconciled here rather than left side by side:** `upload_size` is curl's `%{size_upload}`, which counts **on-the-wire** bytes including chunked-transfer framing, so the 4,115-byte excess is framing and **not** a short write or a padded body — the same reconciliation the checkpoint records as a rig fact at `:2117` (vi).
  - **The controller-side bracket, from the access logs, and it is a bracket for these two specific requests** (`w3-6/partA-bracket.txt`):

        controller3 04:46:00.585826339Z PUT    /api/v1/runs/d764f49f-…/artifacts/orphan-blob status=204 duration_ms=32286
        controller1 04:45:28.891196520Z DELETE /api/v1/runs/d764f49f-…                       status=204 duration_ms=10

    `accessLogMiddleware` stamps on completion, so the `PUT` handler **began at `04:45:28.2998Z`** (*derived*: `04:46:00.5858` − 32.286 s), which cross-checks against the client's own `04:45:28.245Z` start. The DELETE therefore landed **591 ms after the `PUT` handler started** — i.e. after `GetRun` at `api_artifacts.go:55` — and **31.7 s before the `Put` at `:79` completed**. Note also that the two requests were served by **different replicas**; there is no cross-replica coordination on this path and none is attempted.
  - **The result: the run is gone and a 32 MiB artifact of it is not.** `runs` rows for `d764f49f`: **0**. `logs`: 0. `run_log_archives`: 0. `step_reports`: 0. `mc ls --recursive garage/unified-cd-logs/artifacts/d764f49f-…/` → `orphan-blob.tar.gz`, **32MiB** (`w3-6/partB-surfaces.txt`, `w3-6/census.txt`). **The uploader was told it succeeded** — HTTP 204 — and there is no surface on which it could have learned otherwise.
  - **AND THE ASYMMETRY, PRODUCED LIVE ON ONE RUN IN ONE WINDOW, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS MORE THAN A RACE REPORT.** `deleteRunEverywhere` (`internal/controller/run_retention.go:131-173`) does five object-side things with the DB delete **fourth**: record-based archive delete (`:133-141`), deterministic archive-key delete (`:145-147`), `List("artifacts/"+runID+"/")` then delete each (`:148-156`), `st.DeleteRun` (`:158-160`), and then **the deterministic archive key again, best-effort** (`:167-170`). Its own comment names why the fifth exists (`:120-130`): race "(b)" is "The archiver's Put + CreateLogArchive completes *after* our GetLogArchive returned nil but *before* our st.DeleteRun runs", and "**We close (b) here** … once more after DeleteRun succeeds, to catch an archiver that wrote the object during our own deletion window." **There is no equivalent second pass over `artifacts/<runID>/`; step 3 runs once, and `obj.List` snapshots the key set before its loop.** Part C tested that difference directly. On a single terminal run, `artifacts/<id>/` was padded with **10,000** tiny objects so step 3's serial delete loop took measurable time (calibration: 400 pads → a **0.493 s** DELETE, `w3-6/partC-calibrate.txt:9`; 10,000 pads → **12.739 s**, `w3-6/partC-asymmetry.txt:12` — **the two figures live in different captures**, and an earlier version of this sentence cited `partC-calibrate.txt` for both). The DELETE was fired at `04:49:11.518Z`; inside a **0.623 s bracket** running `04:49:14.547Z – 04:49:15.170Z`, ~3.0–3.7 s into that 12.7 s window, **two** objects were planted out of band with `mc` — `runs/<id>/logs.ndjson` (the key step 5 re-sweeps) and `artifacts/<id>/late-blob.tar.gz`. The DELETE returned **204** at `04:49:24.320Z`. Result (`w3-6/partC-asymmetry.txt`):

        runs/2ac4c212-…/       [count=0]                    <- the late log archive was DELETED (step 5)
        artifacts/2ac4c212-…/  late-blob.tar.gz  14B        <- the late artifact SURVIVED

    **Both plants landed inside that same 0.623 s bracket** — and the bracket is all that was measured: `t_plant_before=04:49:14.547` / `t_plant_after=04:49:15.170` straddles **both** `mc cp` calls *plus* the `docker compose exec` startup they share (one `sh -c`), so the interval *between* the two plants is not known, only bounded by 0.623 s. What the bracket does establish is what the finding needs: both plants are inside the one DELETE's window, so **the same execution of the same function caught one and missed the other**. Nothing else could have removed the log archive: steps 1 and 2 ran before the plant (the run had **no** `run_log_archives` row, so step 1 was a no-op), and only step 5 runs after it. **One check the capture does not contain, recorded rather than glossed:** no `mc ls runs/<id>/` was taken *between* the plant and the DELETE's return, so `[count=0]` afterwards is consistent with "step 5 deleted it" **and**, in principle, with "the plant never wrote it". That it wrote is an **inference** — the same bracketed `mc` invocation planted `late-blob.tar.gz`, which is demonstrably present at 14 B afterwards — and this scenario's own MSYS trap (a silently empty upload that still returned 204, runbook note 1) is exactly why the inference is labelled instead of assumed. A single positive existence check between plant and DELETE-return would close it; a re-run should take one. **This is a controlled demonstration and the `mc` plant is labelled as an instrument** — what it stands in for is an archiver `Put` (which W3-2 produced live in the same window shape) and a late `handleArtifactUpload` `Put` (which Part A produced live).
  - **The positive control, recorded as a pass rather than assumed.** Part C's calibration run `332624bb` had 400 artifact objects and **no** racing write: the DELETE removed all 400 and left the prefix empty (`w3-6/partC-calibrate.txt`). So the documented promise holds in steady state and fails only through the window — which is what makes this a race finding and not a "retention does not delete artifacts" finding.
  - **Nothing anywhere records that an object was left behind.** Grepping all three controllers **across the two windows in which an orphan was actually created** — Part A's race bracket and Part C's DELETE — returns **zero** `"level":"WARN"` and **zero** `"level":"ERROR"` lines (`w3-6/partA-bracket.txt`, `w3-6/partC-asymmetry.txt`). **The scope word is deliberate:** those two captures are headed "in the window" and "in the last 6 minutes" and between them do **not** span the whole `04:36:29Z – 04:57:27Z` **session** — session meaning stack-up to teardown-complete (`w3-6/teardown.txt`, `04:57:16.881Z` → `04:57:27.724Z`), which is the wider of the two spans this entry names and is deliberately the one used here; the Repro line's `04:36:29Z – 04:49:24Z` is the narrower **measurement window**, ending with Part C's `DELETE` — so no session-wide silence is claimed. What is load-bearing is covered: nothing was recorded at the moments the orphans were created. The audit log records `run.delete` → **204** at `04:45:28.890056+00` (`w3-6/partB-surfaces.txt`) — a successful deletion, which is true of the row and false of the run's data. The one write that creates the orphan, the artifact `PUT`, has **no audit row at all**: `SELECT count(*) FROM audit_logs WHERE path LIKE '%/artifacts%'` → **0**, because the artifact routes are registered in `registerAgentIdentityRoutes` (`internal/controller/server.go:260`) rather than inside the `/api/v1` group that carries `auditLogMiddleware` (`:355-357`).
  - **Nothing will ever collect it, and the enumeration is exhaustive rather than illustrative.** `ListExpiredRuns` selects `FROM runs` (`internal/store/postgres.go:1494-1502`) and the row is gone, so the sweeper can never nominate the id again — *code-read, and note the sweeper was **disabled** on this rig by design* (all three controllers logged `run retention disabled (keep forever)`, gate G5, `w3-6/gate.txt`), so "it was never collected" is an argument, not a measured negative over a live sweeper. `grep -rn "\.List(" --include=*.go internal/ cmd/` (tests excluded) returns **7** hits, of which 2 are Kubernetes pod lists and **five** are object-store lists: `internal/cache/cache.go:182` (`caches/`), `:223` (`jobPrefix`), `internal/controller/api_artifacts.go:127` (`artifacts/<runID>/`), `internal/controller/log_trim.go:76` (`runLogArchiveKey(id)`, a single key), `internal/controller/run_retention.go:148` (`artifacts/<runID>/`). **Not one enumerates the bucket, the `artifacts/` prefix as a whole, or anything not keyed by a run id or job name the caller already has** (`w3-6/partB-reachability.txt`, which also records that a first, narrower grep for `\.List(ctx` **missed** `api_artifacts.go:127` and was corrected inside the capture rather than quietly re-run). **Artifacts have no DB record of any kind**, so there is nothing to reconcile a bucket against even in principle. **Do not read "never" as a measured window** — the rig was torn down with `-v`; the claim is that no code path exists that would have collected it.
  - **What this scenario did NOT establish, stated explicitly.** Part D (the sweeper path) was **skipped**: the retention interval is hardcoded `time.Hour` at the call site (`cmd/controller/main.go:426`) with no flag and no env override, and `time.NewTicker` does not fire immediately, so even with `UNIFIED_RUN_RETENTION_DAYS=1` and a backdated `updated_at` the first tick is an hour away and a controller restart re-arms it at t+1h rather than firing at once. **So the sweeper was never run**: that `runRunRetentionOnce` reaches `deleteRunEverywhere`, that `ListExpiredRuns`' predicate selects what it appears to, and that the leader-election and backoff wrapper behave as read are all **code-read only** here.
- **Expected:** **contradicted, not silent — and the docs survey that establishes it was run in full with hit counts printed** (`w3-6/docs-greps.txt`, **283** lines, counts **64 / 169 / 30 / 5** for the four surveys, untruncated per the W3-3 method rule). Two `docs/` sentences promise the deleted-artifact behaviour unconditionally (`docs/configuration.md:84`, `docs/operations.md:47`, both quoted above), and per `FINDINGS.md:478-479` a statement in `docs/` is a contract limb. **Three scoping honesties the entry rests on rather than glosses.** (i) **Both passages are about the retention *sweeper*, and the live demonstration used the *manual* DELETE.** The bridge is a one-line code read, not a speculation: `handleDeleteRun` calls `deleteRunEverywhere(r.Context(), s.store, s.objStore, id)` at `internal/controller/api_runs.go:433` and `runRunRetentionOnce` calls it with the same four arguments at `run_retention.go:80` — it is the same function, and Part C's asymmetry is *inside* it. **That bridge is code-read and the sweeper limb is therefore unmeasured**, per the bullet above. (ii) **The manual route's own documentation is silent, not contradicted** — `docs/cli.md:367-373` says only "Deletes a run that has reached a terminal state" and says nothing about objects. (iii) **The obvious counter-reading is that the docs describe steady state and not a race.** It is answered by the code itself: `run_retention.go:111-130` treats exactly this race as in scope for the log-archive key and closes it, so "races are out of scope" is not the design's own position — the design's position is that this window matters, applied to one of the two key spaces the function deletes. **This branch had not previously ruled on either passage** (`grep -rn "configuration.md:84" test/edgecase/` and `"operations.md:47"` → no hits, `w3-6/docs-context.txt`), so the Task-3 reclassification trap does not apply. **The guard's own comment is deliberately NOT treated as the contract**, and the reasoning is recorded because it extends the campaign's rule: `handleArtifactUpload`'s comment at `api_artifacts.go:57-61` names this exact hazard — "A late upload for a deleted run would create an orphaned object nothing ever cleans up (deleteRunEverywhere already ran its prefix delete). Terminal-but-existing runs are still accepted: their objects stay referenced and are removed with the run." — and `FINDINGS.md:479` disqualifies only "an unexported helper's own doc comment". `handleArtifactUpload` is exported and routed, so the letter of the rule does not disqualify it; **the spirit does**, because an inline comment inside a function body is not a *published* promise (not an API field, not a schema column, not a statement in `docs/`). It is evidence of intent, and as evidence it is worth more than a contract would be: **the second sentence of that comment is false in exactly the case the first sentence warns about** — a terminal-but-existing run's objects are *not* always "removed with the run".
- **Severity reasoning, stated because the window is narrow and this entry does not hide it:** the sweeper deletes runs that are days old, which are almost never being uploaded to, so the sweeper-side window is rare in practice; the manual-DELETE window needs an agent uploading to an already-terminal run, which itself needs a cancel, a reap or a failover first. Nothing is lost, no run is mis-stated, and no live run is affected.
