What is left is an object that no code path can reclaim and that outlives the deletion of the run it belongs to — plus, per the observation below, remains downloadable. **minor.** The one consideration that would raise it, and which this entry deliberately does **not** claim to have established: `docs/configuration.md:84` calls run deletion "irreversible", and an operator using retention as a data-deletion control would be relying on a promise this window breaks. Whether anyone does is outside what was measured.
- **Notes:** the cheapest fix is **a fourth line in the same style as the fifth step**: after `st.DeleteRun` succeeds, re-`List` and delete `artifacts/<runID>/` best-effort with a `slog.Warn` on failure, exactly as `run_retention.go:161-170` already does for the log-archive key. It does not close the race — a `Put` completing after *that* still orphans — but it collapses the artifact window to the same width the log-archive key already has and makes the two key spaces consistent, which is the substance of this entry. Two further improvements are independent of it: (a) **a `slog.Warn` when a `PUT` completes for a run that no longer exists** would make the orphan detectable at all — `handleArtifactUpload` could re-check `GetRun` after `Put` and at minimum log the leaked key; today the failure is completely silent on every surface including the audit log; (b) **an orphan reconciler, or any object→run cross-check, does not exist and cannot be written cheaply** while artifacts have no DB record — the only way to find a leaked artifact today is to diff the bucket against `runs`, which nothing ships. **Cross-references, not re-filings.** **W3-2 (`FINDINGS.md:1760`) is the sibling and must not be merged away**: it produced the same *class* — an unreachable orphan — by a different production path (a compensating `obj.Delete` failing after `CreateLogArchive` failed, `internal/controller/archiver.go:111-114`), on the *log-archive* key space, and its own entry says W3-6 owns the class and that the two "should be one finding about 'no orphan-object reconciler exists' with two producers". **This is that finding for the *race-window* producers, and the two are named here: W3-2's failed compensation and W3-6's artifact-prefix TOCTOU. The completeness claim is scoped to those two deliberately, because a third producer of the same class exists and needs no window at all:** `DELETE /api/v1/jobs/{name}` deletes the `jobs` row and nothing else, cascading away every `runs` row of the job without ever reaching `deleteRunEverywhere` (which has exactly two callers, `api_runs.go:433` and `run_retention.go:80`), so **every** artifact and **every** log archive of **every** run of a deleted job survives unconditionally. It surfaced in this scenario's own docs survey (`w3-6/docs-greps.txt:80`, `docs/cli.md:164`) and is **filed as its own entry, the last in this file**, rather than folded in here, for three reasons: its mechanism is not a race, so nothing in this entry's window-based severity argument applies to it; it is strictly worse on every axis and would be buried at **minor**; and, unlike everything above, it was **not executed**. Folding an unmeasured, windowless, higher-severity producer into a measured race entry would have made this entry's severity claim, not just its completeness claim, wrong. W3-2's Part C (`FINDINGS.md:1793`) supplies the clean-failure control that this orphan is a delta from (a blocked `PUT` leaves no object and no incomplete multipart upload). **W0-1 and W3-2's startup entries are unrelated** — nothing here restarts a controller.

## W3-6 — observation: five of seven run-scoped API surfaces answer **200** for a run that `GET /runs/{id}` answers **404** for, and two of them serve the deleted run's data — the orphaned 32 MiB artifact is listable *and* downloadable in full with a server credential and listable with any agent credential (agent **download** is code-read from the shared route group, `server.go:497`), while the `PUT` that created it is the one run-scoped write in the API with no audit row at all
- **Invariant:** none violated, and the reasoning is a deliberate consistency call rather than a close reading. **I7 (state display consistency)** — `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:54`, verbatim: "**State display consistency** — run status, approval status, and audit rows never contradict each other or reality" — is the invariant this comes closest to, and it is **not** filed as violated. Nothing here states an untruth: the run really is gone and the artifact really is there; two routes with different preconditions answer different questions rather than the same question differently. **W3-2 already met this shape and declined to file it** — `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}/logs` returning 200 for a deleted run (`FINDINGS.md:1760`) — and consistency with that call is why this is an observation. **The delta from W3-2's case is recorded rather than smoothed over, because it is the reason this is filed at all:** W3-2's 200 carried an *empty* body, so nothing of the deleted run was served; here the 200s carry **the deleted run's actual data**, 32 MiB of it. **I1 held** (`:48`). **I4** (`:51`) held — see the violation above for the full quotation and for the inherited W3-2 ruling that an orphan is not an unreadable archive.
- **Severity:** minor (observation)
- **Repro:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-6-retention-vs-upload.md` Part B, against the orphan Part A produced (run `d764f49f`, deleted `04:45:28.890Z`, object `artifacts/d764f49f-…/orphan-blob.tar.gz` at 32 MiB). Stack and date as in the entry above. Evidence: `w3-6/partB-surfaces.txt`, `w3-6/partB-reachability.txt`, `w3-6/census.txt`.
- **Observed:**
  - **Seven run-scoped surfaces, each measured with its status *and* its body** (`w3-6/partB-surfaces.txt`), for a run with 0 `runs`, 0 `logs`, 0 `run_log_archives` and 0 `step_reports` rows:

        GET /runs/{id}                       404        52 bytes   run not found: d764f49f-…
        GET /runs/{id}/artifacts             200        25 bytes   [{"name":"orphan-blob"}]
        GET /runs/{id}/artifacts/orphan-blob 200  33554432 bytes   (the artifact, in full)
        GET /runs/{id}/logs                  200         3 bytes   []
        GET /runs/{id}/logs/stats            200        34 bytes   {"count":0,"maxSeq":0,"minSeq":0}
        GET /runs/{id}/steps                 200         3 bytes   []
        GET /runs/{id}/yaml                  404        36 bytes   get run spec: no rows in result set

    **Two of seven say the run is gone; five answer 200; two of those five hand back its data.** The `/logs` 200 is W3-2's already-recorded shape and is cited, not re-derived; `/logs/stats` and `/steps` are new instances of it, recorded here so the surface count is complete rather than sampled.
  - **The mechanism is that the artifact routes have no run-existence precondition, which settles a question the campaign plan left open.** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-30-edge-case-campaign-w3.md:144` lists "whether `handleArtifactList`'s route group enforces run existence" as **not established**. It does not: the group is `s.r.Route("/api/v1/runs/{runID}/artifacts", …)` at `internal/controller/server.go:496-499`, carrying only `s.agentOrServerAuth`, and `handleArtifactList` (`internal/controller/api_artifacts.go:120-147`) answers purely from `s.objStore.List(prefix)`, reconstructing names from keys. `handleArtifactDownload` (`:88-116`) likewise `Get`s the key directly. **Contrast the upload path on the same prefix**, which does check (`GetRun` at `:55`) and additionally enforces ownership: the write side of the artifact prefix is guarded and the read side is not.
  - **Any agent credential can read it, not only an admin token.** The same list request with the synthetic agent's `uca_` bearer returned **200** with the same body (`w3-6/partB-surfaces.txt`). The route group carries no `requireMinRole`, so the reachable principal set is "any valid server token or any valid agent credential" — including agents that never owned the run. That is a pre-existing property of the route and **not** something this scenario produced; what this scenario adds is that the property now applies to a run the system believes it deleted.
  - **The audit trail records the deletion and not the write that survived it.** `run.delete` → 204 is present at `04:45:28.890056+00`; `SELECT count(*) FROM audit_logs WHERE path LIKE '%/artifacts%'` → **0** over the session's 13 rows, because the artifact routes are registered by `registerAgentIdentityRoutes` (`internal/controller/server.go:260`, `:263-282`) rather than inside the `/api/v1` group that installs `auditLogMiddleware` (`:355-357`). So the audit log's whole account of run `d764f49f` is: triggered, deleted, 204 — complete, and complete in a way that omits the only event that mattered.
- **Expected:** as-designed on every limb; no code path here does anything other than what it plainly says. **The docs are silent, not contradicted:** the full survey (`w3-6/docs-greps.txt`, **283** lines, counts **64 / 169 / 30 / 5** printed with the output, untruncated) contains no statement about what a run-scoped route should answer for a deleted run and no promise about artifact-read authorization; `docs/audit.md:31` documents `run.delete` → `DELETE /api/v1/runs/{id}` and says nothing about artifact writes being audited or not.
- **Notes:** the operational point is that **the surface that would tell an operator an orphan exists is the one surface that still works**, and nothing points them at it: `GET /runs/{id}/artifacts` will happily list the artifacts of a run you cannot otherwise see — but only if you already know the id, and the id is exactly what a deleted run no longer has anywhere to be enumerated from. Three cheap improvements, in order of value: (i) give the artifact route group the same `GetRun` precondition the upload path already has, so all seven surfaces agree — this also turns the orphan from "served" into "inert", which is the difference the severity argument in the entry above turns on; (ii) audit artifact writes, which today are the only run-scoped writes with no audit row; (iii) if (i) is judged too strict for a download path that may legitimately outlive a run, make that intent explicit in `docs/` rather than leaving it as an absence. **Cross-references, not re-filings.** **W3-2 (`FINDINGS.md:1760`)** owns the `/logs`-returns-200 observation and this entry cites rather than repeats it; the orphan itself is the violation entry immediately above and is not double-filed here.

## W3-6 — spun out of this scenario's docs survey and **NOT EXECUTED (code-read only)**: `DELETE /api/v1/jobs/{name}` deletes the `jobs` row and nothing else, so every artifact and every log archive of every run of the job survives the delete **unconditionally, with no race and no window**, unreachable by the same argument the entry above establishes — while `docs/cli.md:164` says the command "Deletes a job and all its run history (steps, logs, artifacts)"
- **Invariant:** **documented contract**, contradicted. `docs/cli.md:164` (§`jobs delete`): "Deletes a job and all its run history (steps, logs, artifacts)." The parenthesis is the promise and it names artifacts explicitly; per `FINDINGS.md:478-479` a statement in `docs/` is a contract limb. On the invariant table nothing is violated, and the reasoning is the same one the entry above inherits from W3-2: **I4 (log/artifact integrity)** (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:51`) is **NOT** contradicted — an object with no record is an orphan, not an unreadable archive. **NOT I1/I2/I3/I5/I7**: no run status is involved, nothing executes twice, no lock, nothing injected, and no surface states an untruth (the job really is gone).
- **Severity:** **major** — argued against this file's own bands (`:6-8`), and argued *up* from the **minor** of the entry it was spun out of rather than inherited from it. `:8` gives minor as "diagnosability, **docs gap**, cosmetic": this is not a docs gap, because the doc describes the intended behaviour correctly and the code does not implement it, on **every** invocation rather than through a window. `:7` gives major as "incorrect visible behavior, unbounded recovery": the operator runs a documented cleanup command, is told it succeeded, and the object store's contents are wrong afterwards — and the leak is unbounded in the only sense available to it, since the run ids that were the sole handle on those objects are destroyed by the same statement, so no code path and no operator procedure can enumerate what leaked. It is strictly worse than the race above on every axis: **no window** (100% of invocations, not a sub-second overlap), **both key spaces** (`artifacts/<runID>/` *and* `runs/<runID>/logs.ndjson`, not one), **every run of the job** rather than one, and it has an **automated caller** — the AppSource reconciler's prune path (`internal/controller/appsource_apply.go:120`, documented at `docs/resources.md:827`, "delete jobs removed from the repo") deletes jobs in bulk with no operator in the loop. **`:6` (critical) is arguable and is deliberately NOT claimed**, for two reasons stated so the operator can overrule them: nothing is lost or corrupted — the failure direction is *retention*, not deletion — and no authorization boundary moves, since reading a leaked object still needs either bucket credentials or a run id that no longer exists in any database. What would raise it is that `docs/configuration.md:84` calls run deletion "irreversible", so an operator using `jobs delete` as a data-purge control keeps everything they believe they purged, and per the observation above those objects stay **served** to any agent or server credential that knows an id. **Whether to escalate on that is the operator's call, and it should be measured before it is escalated** — see Repro.
- **Repro:** **NONE. This entry was not executed**, and that is the single most important thing about it. It is a code read at this branch's HEAD, done while re-checking the entry above, and it is filed rather than dropped because leaving it unfiled would have made that entry's "the two producers are named here" claim false. Read: `grep -rn "func .*DeleteJob" --include=*.go internal/` → exactly two implementations; `grep -rn "deleteRunEverywhere" --include=*.go internal/ cmd/` (tests excluded) → exactly two call sites; `grep -n "REFERENCES public.jobs" internal/store/migrations/001_init.up.sql`. **A measurement is cheap and should precede any fix**: trigger a job, let a run archive its logs and upload an artifact, `DELETE /api/v1/jobs/{name}`, then `mc ls --recursive` both prefixes. Any rig with an object store does it in minutes — this scenario's own rig (`test/ha` + `bigbody`) had already been torn down with `-v` when the read was made.
- **Observed (code, not measurement — every line below is a read):**
  - **`handleDeleteJob` does one thing.** `internal/controller/api_jobs.go:220-231`: `guardManagedResource`, then `s.store.DeleteJob(r.Context(), name)`, then `204`. **There is no `objStore` reference in the function and no call that could reach one.**
  - **`DeleteJob` is one statement.** `internal/store/postgres.go:2168-2171`: `DELETE FROM jobs WHERE name = $1`. That is the whole implementation; it is the only one in the tree besides the interface declaration at `internal/store/store.go:182`.
  - **The run rows go away by cascade, taking every handle on the objects with them.** `internal/store/migrations/001_init.up.sql:620`: `runs_job_name_fkey ... REFERENCES public.jobs(name) ON DELETE CASCADE`; and `runs` in turn cascades to `logs` (`:572`), `run_log_archives` (`:604`), `step_reports` (`:644`), `run_approvals` (`:596`), `run_outputs` (`:612`), `step_outputs` (`:636`). **`run_log_archives` is deleted without its object ever being deleted** — the row is the only record that the object exists.
  - **`deleteRunEverywhere` is never reached.** It has exactly two callers: `internal/controller/api_runs.go:433` (the manual run DELETE) and `internal/controller/run_retention.go:80` (the sweeper). Neither is on the job-delete path, and `internal/controller/` has exactly one artifact-prefix deleter anywhere — `run_retention.go:148` — which is inside it.
  - **Nothing else will ever collect the objects, by the entry above's own enumeration.** `ListExpiredRuns` selects `FROM runs` (`internal/store/postgres.go:1494-1502`) and the rows are gone; the five object-store `List` call sites in the tree are `cache.go:182`, `cache.go:223`, `api_artifacts.go:127`, `log_trim.go:76` and `run_retention.go:148`, **not one of which enumerates the bucket or the `artifacts/` prefix as a whole** (`w3-6/partB-reachability.txt`). Artifacts have no DB record at all, so there is nothing left to reconcile a bucket against even in principle.
  - **The one thing this shares with the entry above's `handleArtifactUpload` comment is the belief.** `api_jobs.go:219`'s own doc comment says "Associated Run history is also cascade-deleted" — true of the database and, like `api_artifacts.go:57-61`'s second sentence, silently false of the object store.
- **Expected:** **contradicted, not silent.** `docs/cli.md:164` promises the artifacts; the CLI's `jobs delete` is the documented front end of this route (`docs/audit.md:28` maps `job.delete` → `DELETE /api/v1/jobs/{name}`). The survey behind this is the same one the entry above prints in full (`w3-6/docs-greps.txt`, 283 lines, counts **64 / 169 / 30 / 5**, untruncated); `docs/cli.md:164` is line 80 of that capture and was printed there before this entry existed. **Two honesties.** (i) **No `docs/` sentence anywhere promises the *converse*** — nothing says a job delete keeps objects, so this is not a documented trade-off. (ii) **This branch had not previously ruled on `docs/cli.md:164`** — the entry above's `w3-6/docs-context.txt` check covered `configuration.md:84` and `operations.md:47` only, and no wave has cited `cli.md:164` — **and that second half is UNCAPTURED, unlike the first, which is why it is labelled rather than left in the same breath.** `w3-6/docs-context.txt:2-7` holds the greps for `configuration.md:84` and `operations.md:47` and their "(no hits)"; it holds **no grep for `cli.md:164` at all**. The claim was re-derived at review — `grep -rn "cli\.md:164" test/edgecase/` returns only this entry, its sibling's Notes and the checkpoint — and is **true**; it simply rests on an unarchived terminal read.
- **Notes:** the fix is the same shape as the entry above's and cheaper: **have `handleDeleteJob` enumerate the job's runs before deleting the row and call `deleteRunEverywhere` for each** (`ListRunsByJob` already exists and is indexed, `internal/store/migrations/008_run_indexes.up.sql:10`), or, if a synchronous unbounded loop in a request handler is unacceptable, **write the run ids to a work table the retention sweeper drains** — which is the only variant that also fixes the AppSource prune path, since that one has no request to be slow in. Either way the artifact-side re-sweep proposed in the entry above should land first, because both fixes route through the same function. **Cross-references, not re-filings.** The class is the one W3-2 (`FINDINGS.md:1760`) named and the entry above owns: "no orphan-object reconciler exists". **This is its third producer and the only one that needs no race**; the other two are the artifact-prefix TOCTOU (the entry above) and W3-2's failed compensating delete. The observation above supplies what makes the leak *reachable* rather than merely present: the artifact route group has no run-existence precondition and no `requireMinRole`, so a leaked object stays listable and downloadable to anyone holding any agent or server credential and an id.

### W3-5: log lines are accepted with a `204`, discarded by the archive seal, and the loss is invisible to the sender and to every surface of the run

- **Scenario:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-5-seal-vs-flush.md`. Executed on branch `plan/edge-case-w3`, **`05:40:18Z – 05:53:02Z`** on 2026-07-31, on the plain `test/ha` stack + Garage + `mc`, no overlay, no interposer, torn down with `down -v` (`w3-5/teardown.txt`). The developer stack (`docker compose ls`, project `unified-cd`) was running and untouched at both ends (`w3-5/gate.txt`, `w3-5/teardown.txt`).
- **Invariant:** **I4**, quoted verbatim from `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:51`:

  > "**Log/artifact integrity** — a Succeeded run's log line count matches what the workload emitted; no duplicates, no reordering; archives stay readable"

  **Clause by clause, because the fit is not clean and pretending otherwise would be dishonest.** *"no duplicates, no reordering"* — **not touched**; W3-4 owns duplication and nothing here reorders. *"archives stay readable"* — **not touched, and deliberately not stretched**; every archive here is readable and internally self-consistent, it is simply short. The clause in play is the first — and it is a **three-way conjunct** (`Succeeded` **and** what the **workload** emitted **and** a short count) that **neither arm satisfies in full**: Part B has a real workload emitting real lines but the run is `Cancelled`; Part A has a `Succeeded` run whose lines were emitted by an instrument, not a workload. **This entry does not stretch I4's text to close that gap. It is filed at the scope both arms actually demonstrate, which is stronger than I4's literal case and strictly contains it: _a run terminalised by the controller while the agent still holds a log backlog permanently and silently loses that backlog._** **The code bridge is real but is not offered as if it closed the literal gap**: the guard reads `run_log_archives` and **nothing else** — it never looks at `runs.status` (`internal/store/postgres.go:918-936`, the `WHERE NOT EXISTS` is on the archive row alone) — so *if* a `Succeeded` run held a post-seal backlog its lines would be dropped by the identical INSERT. That shows the drop is **status-blind**; it does **not** show that `Succeeded` and a live workload backlog can co-occur. **Per Correction 1 in the Notes, they may not be able to**: a `Succeeded` status requires the agent to reach `FinishRun` (`internal/agent/orchestrator.go:787-788`), which is strictly after every agent-side flush point (`:706`, `:727`, `internal/agent/backend_host.go:382-386`), and with the sidecar flush winning its race essentially always, every remaining producer of a post-seal backlog terminalises the run `Failed` or `Cancelled`. **The severity is unchanged at major on the restated scope** — the loss is permanent, unbounded and invisible on every surface — and this file's own rule that "an invariant must be contradicted by its own text (not by its spirit)" (`:1509`) is respected by restating the finding rather than by amending I4. **A later wave that wants the literal I4 limb must demonstrate a reachable `Succeeded` producer; this scenario cannot, and says so rather than implying otherwise.**
- **Severity:** **major**.
- **Observed:**
  - **The mechanism, live and exact.** The controller accepts a bulk log write for a sealed run, returns **`204 No Content` with a zero-byte body**, stores nothing, and records the fact in exactly one place: a controller-side `slog.Warn`. `AppendLog`'s `INSERT … WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM run_log_archives WHERE run_id = $1::uuid) RETURNING seq` matches no rows, `pgx.ErrNoRows → return 0, nil` (`internal/store/postgres.go:918-936`, guard at `:922`, sentinel at `:927-928`), and both handlers translate `seq == 0` into a warning and a 204 (`internal/controller/api_agent.go:564-570` single; `:719-738` bulk).
  - **Part B — the natural race, HIT ON ATTEMPT 1 OF A CAP OF 3, with nothing synthetic in it.** Run `c158dcd4` (`edge-longrun`, 300 × `tick N` at 1/s) on **agent2** (`w3-5/partB-attempt1.txt`, `w3-5/partB-evidence.txt`, `w3-5/partB-accounting.txt`):

    | instant | event | source |
    |---|---|---|
    | `05:46:55.060Z` | agent logs `running` for the run | agent2 container log |
    | `05:47:07.066Z` | **last log line ever stored**, `tick 12` (`logs.ts`) | `partB-accounting.txt` |
    | `05:47:07.243Z` | `inject.sh partition agent2` | `partB-attempt1.txt` |
    | `05:47:11.9 / :16.5 / :21.1Z` | **arm probe-confirmed**: line count 12, 12, 12 | `partB-attempt1.txt` |
    | `05:47:22.723Z` | `POST /runs/{id}/cancel` → **204**; `runs.updated_at = 05:47:22.7657Z`, status `Cancelled` **immediately** | `partB-attempt1.txt` |
    | `05:47:49.606Z` | **the real archiver seals it** — `run_log_archives` row, `line_count=12`, `max_seq=67` | `partB-attempt1.txt` |
    | `05:48:22.768Z` | `inject.sh heal agent2` | `partB-attempt1.txt` |
    | `05:48:23.085Z – 05:48:26.083Z` | **9 bulk requests**, `dropped` totalling **78**, spread across **all three replicas**. **The 9 and the 78 are measured, but not by the same capture and the citation is split accordingly:** the **9** is `partB-evidence.txt:14` ("count of WARN lines: 9"), whose own summing step **failed** — `bash: bc: command not found`, `:15-16` — so the **78** comes from `partB-accounting.txt:20` (`requests=9 dropped_sum=78`) and is hand-summable from the nine warning lines at `census.txt:34-42` (62 + 8×2). **"all `204`" is *code-derived*, not captured** — `handleAgentLogBulk` writes `w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)` unconditionally after the warning (`api_agent.go:736-738`), and no capture records a status code for these nine. **Part A is where a post-seal `204` is measured on the wire.** | `partB-evidence.txt`, `partB-accounting.txt` |
    | `05:48:25.080Z` | agent logs `received cancellation signal from master; interrupting run` | agent2 container log |

  - **The accounting closes exactly, and that is the entry's strongest number.** Last stored line `tick 12` at `05:47:07.066Z`; the agent stopped emitting when it took the cancellation signal at `05:48:25.080Z` — a span of **78.014 s** at one tick per second, so **78 further ticks** (*derived*). The controller counted **78** dropped lines (*measured*, sum of the nine `dropped` fields, `partB-evidence.txt`). **12 stored + 78 discarded = 90 lines offered, and every one of the 78 reached the controller and was thrown away with a success ack.** The batch shape is visible too: eight batches of **2** (the 2 s auto-flush cadence, `internal/agent/runner.go:211`, and the stored rows show the same pairing) plus one of **62**.
  - **The sender's experience of losing 87 % of the run's output is one INFO line.** agent2's container log **strictly after the heal instant** (`05:48:22.768Z`) contains **exactly one line in total**: the `INFO` cancellation notice above. **Zero `ERROR`, zero `WARN`, zero mention of a log push, a bulk request, a drop or a seal** (`partB-evidence.txt`, filtered on the timestamp and the counts printed). **The window is bounded and stated**: it runs from the heal instant to the capture's own timestamp header `05:49:24.418Z` — **61.650 s** — so the claim is "one line in that minute", not "one line ever". Before the heal the same log is full of partition errors — those are expected and are not this claim; the claim is scoped to the post-heal window and the capture is too. **One capture hazard, recorded so a re-reader does not take the wrong block:** `partB-evidence.txt` contains an **earlier, wrongly filtered** post-heal block *above* the one this bullet uses — `:25-30`, "post-heal agent2 lines: **202**, ERROR: **176**, WARN: **20**" — which is in fact the whole log and not the post-heal window, and which the capture then supersedes with the block it labels "correctly filtered" (`:48-58`, lines **1**, ERROR **0**, WARN **0**). **This bullet rests on the second block; the first is not evidence for anything and must not be cited as a contradiction of it.**
  - **Why the sender cannot know, in code.** A 204 is not an error to the agent client (`Client.do` errors only on `>= 400`, `internal/agent/client.go:107-113`), so `flushLocked` takes the success branch and the batch is never queued into `p.pending` (`runner.go:390-392`) — and the buffer was already `Reset()` before the send (`:371`). **The `[N log line(s) dropped: controller unreachable]` marker therefore cannot ever fire for this channel**: `droppedLines` is incremented *only* by `appendPendingLocked`'s cap eviction (`runner.go:432`), which requires a queued batch, which requires an error. Confirmed live — `SELECT count(*) FROM logs WHERE run_id=… AND line LIKE '%dropped%'` → **0** on every run in the session (`partB-evidence.txt`, `partA-postseal.txt`).
  - **Part A — the same INSERT against a `Succeeded` run, sealed by the real archiver.** Run `cd7c25a0` (`edge-w35-probe`, claimed by a curl-driven synthetic identity — **an instrument, and it is named as one every time it carries a claim below**). 20 lines pushed **pre-seal** landed normally (204, `size_upload=2973`, `logs` = 20 — `partA-preseal.txt`), which is what makes the post-seal zero mean something. `finish` → 204 at `05:43:16.978Z`, `runs.status = Succeeded`. **The archiver sealed it 2.615 s later** — ***derived*, and the basis is stated because the sentence just quoted a different clock**: the interval is measured against `runs.updated_at 05:43:16.9427Z` (`partA-seal.txt:5`), **not** against the client-side `finish` return at `05:43:16.978Z`, from which the same seal is 2.580 s — `archived_at 05:43:19.558Z`, `line_count=20`, and the archiver's own `slog.Info` on **controller1**: `"archived Run logs" runId=cd7c25a0-… key=runs/cd7c25a0-…/logs.ndjson bytes=2011` (`partA-seal.txt`). **No arm of this scenario wrote a `run_log_archives` row by hand**, which matters because `test/edgecase/README.md` § "Sidecars are NOT available on this rig" advises exactly that; the seal here is the product's.
  - **Part A's post-seal push, with the response captured in full.** 10 lines at `05:44:09.458Z`: **`HTTP/1.1 204 No Content`**, `size_upload=1493`, `size_download=0`, **response body 0 bytes**, and the only headers returned are `Server`/`Date`/`Connection`/`Referrer-Policy`/`X-Content-Type-Options`/`X-Frame-Options` — **there is no field anywhere in the response that could carry a drop count** (`partA-postseal.txt`, headers pasted there verbatim). Controller3 warned at `05:44:09.5022Z` with `dropped=10`. Afterwards: `logs` count **20** (unchanged), `post-%` lines **0**, `GET /runs/{id}/logs/stats` → `{"count":20,"maxSeq":50,"minSeq":31}`, `run_log_archives` row byte-identical. **Four product surfaces agree on a story that is short by ten lines.**
  - **Both drop paths were exercised, not one.** The single-line route `POST /api/v1/agents/{id}/logs` at `05:44:32.899Z` → **204**, `size_upload=154`, line not stored, controller1 logged the **singular** `"dropping log line for sealed run"` at `05:44:32.9540Z` (`partA-single.txt`). **Note the asymmetry between the two warnings themselves:** the bulk form carries `"dropped":N`; the singular form carries **no count field at all**, so N single-line drops are N indistinguishable log lines.
  - **The archiving replica is not the warning replica, and there is no coordination.** **controller1 sealed Part A's run** — the archiver's own `slog.Info` (`"archived Run logs" runId=cd7c25a0-…`) is in `partA-seal.txt`, and the same line for the gate run `3d32270a` is in `gate-g4-archive.txt`. **No capture attributes Part B's seal to any replica**: it is evidenced by the `run_log_archives` row alone (`archived_at 05:47:49.606056+00`, `line_count=12`, `max_seq=67`), and the archiver `slog.Info` for that run was never grepped — so no claim is made about which replica wrote it. Only that it was the **product's** archiver and not a hand-written row (no arm of this scenario inserted one). The drops were warned by controller3 (Part A bulk), controller1 (Part A single), and all three replicas within Part B's 3 s burst. An operator grepping one container's log sees a fraction of the loss.
  - **Session-wide silence elsewhere.** **Zero** controller `ERROR` lines across the whole session, all three replicas (`census.txt`, count printed). The audit log's only word on the Part B run is a single `run.cancel` row; there is **no audit row of any kind** for a dropped log line (`census.txt`, `audit_logs` grouped by action: `agent.enrollment.create 4`, `agent.enrollment.exchange 4`, `job.apply 3`, `run.cancel 1`, `run.trigger 4`).
- **Expected:** **the drop itself is DOCUMENTED AND SANCTIONED, and this entry leads with that rather than burying it.** `docs/operations.md:51`: "Log lines arriving after archival are discarded with a controller warning (`dropping log line for sealed run`) to keep the archive consistent; storing them would make the run untrimmable and, after trim, invisible." `docs/troubleshooting.md:851-868` gives it a whole section, calls occasional occurrences "expected noise" (`:863`), and names as a common cause exactly what Part B produced: "An agent retrying after a network partition (the run was finalized by the stuck-run reaper meanwhile)" (`:865`). **So this is NOT filed on the contract limb** — the policy is a published, deliberate, well-reasoned trade-off, and the seal has a real justification (`postgres.go:911-917`: late lines make the run untrimmable and, after trim, invisible). It is filed on **I4**, which the campaign's rule makes sufficient on its own (`FINDINGS.md:479`).
  - **What the docs do not say, which is where the finding lives.** The survey was run in full and untruncated with hit counts printed (`w3-5/partE-docs.txt`): `archiv|seal` → **35**, `log line|dropped|lost log|log loss|silently` → **49**, `204|no content|acknowledg|ack\b` → **84**, `at-least-once|at least once|exactly once|guarantee` → **1**. **Filtering the 84 down to hits that concern a log append or an agent leaves ZERO** (`partE-docs.txt`, the filtered grep and its count are in the capture). Nothing in `docs/` says the sender is told it succeeded, that its buffer is cleared, that it will never retry, or that the run's own log carries no record.
  - **The contrast is with the project's own documented loud-loss convention for the sibling channel**, which `docs/troubleshooting.md:895-898` states as a design principle: "Rather than let that gap in the run's log pass silently, the agent counts every discarded line and, on the next flush that successfully reaches the controller, emits this one synthetic line reporting exactly how many lines were lost." That channel gets a marker in the run's own log **and** its own troubleshooting section explaining that "the count in the marker is the only remaining record" (`:918-919`). **This channel gets neither, and its sender does not even know to look.** That is a stronger statement than the W1-2 major it sits beside: there the agent knew and stayed quiet; here the agent does not know.
  - **Already-ruled check, run before filing** (`partE-docs.txt`). `docs/operations.md:51` and `docs/troubleshooting.md:889-899` are both already cited by `w3-4-bulk-append-duplication.md:205-216` and `FINDINGS.md:1561`, which read `:889-899` **narrowly** — as authorising retry and saying nothing about what the controller already committed. **That reading is binding and this entry is consistent with it**: it cites `:889-899` only for the convention it states, not as a promise about delivery. **`docs/troubleshooting.md:851-868` — the sealed-run section itself — had not been cited by any prior wave**; the grep output is in the capture.
- **Notes:**
  - **THE PLAN'S PREMISE FOR THIS SCENARIO IS WRONG ON TWO COUNTS, AND THE SECOND IS NEW.** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-30-edge-case-campaign-w3.md:107-115` asserts that "for any run with a sidecar, log lines are flushed strictly after the run is terminal, by construction, on every run", and calls the sidecar's seal-race exposure "**structural, every run**". (i) **Sidecars are unavailable on this rig** — Task 3's two independent mechanisms (`native: true` ⇒ `pod == nil`, and no Docker socket), recorded in `test/edgecase/README.md`; `test/ha/agent.Dockerfile`, which both the plan and the task brief name, **does not exist** (the image is built from `docker/agent.Dockerfile`). (ii) **Even where sidecars do exist, the window is not structural.** The ordering is real — `defer b.CloseScopes(...)` at `internal/agent/orchestrator.go:209` runs after `FinishRun` at `:787-788` — but `CloseScopes` follows `FinishRun` by **microseconds to milliseconds**, while the archiver can only seal on its next **30 s** tick after that same `FinishRun` commits (`cmd/controller/main.go:400`, hardcoded, no flag, no env). **The sidecar flush therefore wins the race essentially always**; it loses only if the tick happens to land inside that sub-millisecond gap. The plan's "every run" is an over-read of a real ordering. **The realistic producer of this loss is not the sidecar ordering at all — it is delayed delivery** (partition, reaper, restart), which is what `docs/troubleshooting.md:865-866` says and what Part B produced naturally on the first attempt. **A later wave with a sidecar-capable rig should not expect the structural shape.**
  - **CORRECTION 2 — the brief's sharpest framing is wrong, and the accurate version is sharper, but the consequence runs the *other* way from what an earlier draft of this entry said.** The plan (`:108`) and the task brief say sidecar *status* was given a `rejectTerminal=false` accommodation for the post-`FinishRun` window while sidecar *logs* "get none". **The load-bearing fact is confirmed at HEAD:** `handleAgentLogAppend` (`api_agent.go:551`), `handleAgentLogBulk` (`:709`) and `handleAgentSidecarStatus` (`:760`) all pass `rejectTerminal=false`, so a late log line is **not** stopped by the terminal guard — it is stopped ~30 s later by a **different and strictly later gate**, the seal. **But there is no inconsistency to file, because neither log endpoint is an F2 endpoint.** F2 is **four named handlers** (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-15-hardening-f1-f7-design.md:14-19`): `handleAgentSetStepOutputs`, `handleAgentSetRunOutputs`, `handleAgentSidecarStatus`, `handleAgentCreateApproval` — and the other three all pass `true` (`api_agent.go:647`, `:673`, `internal/controller/api_approvals.go:77`). So `api_agent.go:744`'s comment "unlike the other F2 endpoints" is **correct at HEAD, not stale**. **The real asymmetry is one of scope, not of inconsistency: the log endpoints were never inside the terminal gate's scope at all, because the F2 hardening never enumerated them.** The F2 review reasoned explicitly about what arrives in this window and carved out the display-only channel with a written rationale (`api_agent.go:741-752`: "both agents stop their sidecar pumps via a deferred CloseScopes that runs AFTER FinishRun, so the final reportStatus(..., \"exited\", exitCode) call is *expected* to arrive once the run is already terminal", allowed because "UpsertSidecarStatus is a display-only upsert keyed by (run, index), so a late/duplicate write here is harmless") — and **never considered the data channel, which dies 30 s later at an unreviewed gate.** Two further span corrections: that comment is `:741-752`, not the plan's `:744-748`; and the bulk block is `:719-738` with the `slog.Warn` at **`:736`** (the plan's `:732` is right, but it is the `droppedRun = req.RunID` assignment, not the warning).
  - **The fix shape, with the counter-argument to each, because "just accept the lines" is not available.** (a) **Tell the sender.** The 204 is deliberate — `api_agent.go:565-567`: "204 keeps unmodified agents from retry-storming — same philosophy as FinishRun's alreadyFinalized response" — but `handleAgentFinishRun` solves the identical problem *with a body*: `200` plus `{"alreadyFinalized":true}`, chosen precisely so an unmodified agent does not error while a caller that looks can observe the no-op (`api_agent.go:573-582`). **The same trick applied here costs nothing and would let a future agent count what it lost.** (b) **Record it in the run.** The archiver owns the archive; appending a synthetic `[N log line(s) dropped: run already archived]` line to the *archive object* (or a counter column on `run_log_archives`) would put the loss where the operator reads the log, matching the documented convention for the sibling channel. Counter-argument: it mutates a sealed artifact, which is the thing the seal exists to prevent — a counter column avoids that. (c) **At minimum, attribute the bulk warning per run** (see the observation below). **What is NOT a fix:** storing the lines, which `postgres.go:911-917` explains would make the run untrimmable and, after trim, invisible.
  - **Boundary with W1, by citation and not by re-measurement.** The **W1-2 major** and **W1-6** measured loss where the agent's bytes **never reached the controller** — the step-end `Flush`'s fixed 5 s budget (`internal/agent/runner.go:343`). This entry is the opposite half of the wire: the bytes arrive, are accepted, and are discarded server-side. **Part B lost NOTHING to the W1 channel and the captures prove the separation**: all 78 undelivered lines are accounted for controller-side, including the post-cancel tail (the last drop request lands at `05:48:26.083Z`, **after** the agent took the cancellation signal at `05:48:25.080Z`, i.e. the step-end `Flush` itself went through the 204 path). The two defects share the `LogPusher` and nothing else. Also distinct from **F9** (loss at the 1 MiB `p.pending` byte cap, never approached here — 78 lines of `tick N`) and from the **W1-6 quadratic-amplification** observation (that is about wasted request volume with no loss by its own mechanism; this is loss with no waste).
  - **Cross-reference, not a re-filing.** `FINDINGS.md:1499` (the W2 checkpoint) told W3-5 to start from the measured asymmetry that `handleAgentStepReport` refuses to write to a terminal run while `logs/bulk` accepts. **That asymmetry is confirmed and it is now shown to be only half the story**: `logs/bulk` accepts post-*terminal* and then rejects post-*seal*, ~30 s later, with the same 204 either way. A sender cannot distinguish "stored" from "silently dropped" at any point on that timeline.
  - **What this scenario did NOT establish, stated explicitly.** (i) **No arm reproduces the plan's structural sidecar race** — it cannot be produced on this rig at all, and per the correction above it is not structural anyway. (ii) **The literal `Succeeded`-run-with-a-real-workload case was never produced — and per Correction 1 it may be *unreachable in production*, not merely unproduced here.** No agent-side flush point exists later than `FinishRun` (`orchestrator.go:706`, `:727`, `backend_host.go:382-386` all precede `:787-788`), and once the sidecar flush is granted its race essentially always, every remaining producer of a post-seal backlog terminalises the run `Failed` or `Cancelled`. The argument that the drop is status-blind is a **code read** (`postgres.go:918-936` consults no run status), not a measurement, and it does not show the two halves can co-occur — which is exactly why this entry is filed at the restated scope given under **Invariant** above rather than on I4's literal wording. (iii) **Part A's line set was emitted by an instrument**, and every claim resting on it says so. (iv) **The rig's intermittent-500 allowance was never spent** — no step was retried for a 500 — but **no capture counts 500s across the session**, so this is an absence of trouble, not a measurement, and it appears nowhere as evidence.
  - **A Windows/MSYS note that is the mirror image of W3-6's.** One post-seal attempt at `05:43:40.738Z` is **void, not a result**: `curl -D/-o` with MSYS-form output paths failed to open them, exit 23, `http_code=000`, `size_upload=0` — **the request never left the client**. Unlike W3-6's note 1 (where an MSYS `@` path uploaded an empty body and still returned 204), this failure mode is **loud**. Both are avoided the same way: Windows-form paths for every `curl` file argument, and check `%{size_upload}`, never the status alone. The void attempt and its re-run are both in `partA-postseal.txt`.

### W3-5 (observation): a bulk log-append spanning two sealed runs counts every drop and attributes them all to whichever run happens to be last in the body

- **Scenario:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-5-seal-vs-flush.md` Part C. Same session and stack as the entry above.
- **Invariant:** **I4** is the closest fit (`:51`, quoted in the entry above) and **is not contradicted by this on its own** — the loss it mis-attributes is the entry above's loss, already filed there. This entry is about the *record* of that loss, so **I7** (`:54`, "run status, approval status, and audit rows never contradict each other or reality") is the nearer invariant in spirit; it is cited as closest fit only, because a `slog` warning is not a run status, an approval status or an audit row. **Recorded as an observation because it is as designed and today unreachable by any agent, not because any invariant was found to be violated.**
- **Severity:** **minor (observation)**.
- **Observed:** `handleAgentLogBulk` counts every dropped line into one `dropped` total but overwrites `droppedRun` on **every** drop (`internal/controller/api_agent.go:730-733`) and logs only the survivor (`:736`). Produced live with two sealed synthetic runs owned by the same identity (`w3-5/partC-setup.txt`, `partC-mixed.txt`, `partC-order.txt`):
  - **C2** — one body of 4 lines, 2 for run `cd7c25a0` then 2 for run `edb91160`, `POST`ed to **`cd7c25a0`'s** path at `05:45:39.231Z` → **204**, `size_upload=596`. controller1 logged one warning at `05:45:39.2751Z`: `"dropping log lines for sealed run" run=edb91160-… dropped=4`. **A grep of all three replicas restricted to the `T05:45:39` second — a window that covers only this request — finds `cd7c25a0` in ZERO warning lines** (`partC-order.txt`, the tight re-grep and its three zeros are in the capture; an earlier 90 s grep in `partC-mixed.txt` matched it once, but that hit is the *single-line* drop from Part A at `05:44:32Z` and is not this request).
  - **C4 — the same four lines with the order reversed** (`edb91160` first, `cd7c25a0` last), same path, `05:46:09.379Z` → 204: controller1 now warns `run=cd7c25a0-… dropped=4` at `05:46:09.4293Z`. **The same physical loss is attributed to a different run purely by position in the body.** No `mix*` or `rev*` line is stored anywhere (`SELECT count(*) … LIKE 'mix%'` and `'rev%'` → 0, both captures).
  - **Incidentally confirmed and worth recording:** the `{runId}` path parameter of `POST /api/v1/agents/{agentId}/runs/{runId}/steps/{stepIndex}/logs/bulk` is **decorative for this handler** — it reads `chi.URLParam(r, "agentId")` only (`api_agent.go:703`) and takes every run id from the body. Both C2 and C4 were posted to `cd7c25a0`'s path and were guarded and applied entirely per body element.
- **Expected:** **silent, not contradicted.** The docs survey behind the entry above (`w3-5/partE-docs.txt`, counts 35 / 49 / 84 / 1, untruncated) contains nothing about how a drop is attributed, and `docs/troubleshooting.md:851-868` — the section that tells an operator what to do about these warnings, including "If you see a sustained stream of these warnings for the same run, the agent may be stuck" (`:868`) — is the passage this most directly degrades, since "the same run" is exactly the signal that becomes unreliable. That is a weakened diagnostic, not a broken promise.
- **Notes:** **This is an instrument result and is bounded accordingly.** A real agent's `LogPusher` is per run and per step (`internal/agent/runner.go:233-235`), holds its own `runID` field (`:234`) and passes it to every send, so **no agent in the tree today can emit a mixed batch**; Part C's body was hand-built. **That is a full enumeration, not two examples**: `grep -rn "AppendLogBulk" --include=*.go internal/ cmd/` (excluding tests) returns **nine** call sites and every one supplies a single run id — `internal/agent/runner.go:311`, `:362`, `:390`, `:421` (all `p.runID`), `internal/agent/orchestrator.go:178`, `:220`, `:310` (all `c.RunID`), `internal/agent/agent.go:652` and `internal/k8sagent/agent.go:394` (both the function's own `runID` parameter). The gap is therefore **latent** — reachable by any client of the documented API, and live the moment any sender puts more than one run in a body. **The plausible future sender is an *agent-level* batcher across concurrent pushers**, which is credible because W1-6's amplification is per-agent across 16 slots. **That is NOT what W1-6 recommended, and an earlier draft of this note said it was.** W1-6's fix (`FINDINGS.md:474`, carried forward as "the coalesce-the-backlog fix" at `:496` and `:1491`) is "coalesce all pending batches into a single `AppendLogBulk` call" — **batches, not runs** — and `p.pending` belongs to one per-`(run, step)` pusher whose every request carries `p.runID`, so implementing it **as written** yields a single-run body and **cannot** produce a mixed batch. The conflict is with the agent-level generalisation only, and the caution is now recorded at both ends (`:474`, `:496`, `:1491`) so a triager arriving from the amplification item sees it. The fix here is one line — accumulate `map[string]int` instead of a scalar, or move the warning inside the loop — and it is the cheapest of the three fixes listed in the entry above.

**W3-5 corroboration of W2-2 (`:587`) — cross-referenced, not re-filed, per this branch's convention (`:798`).** Part B's cancelled run `c158dcd4` was left with `step 0 | Running | exit=NULL | ended=NULL` at `05:51:03Z`, **3 m 40.4 s** after the cancel (*derived*: the census stamp `05:51:03.198Z`, `w3-5/census.txt:2`, minus `runs.updated_at 05:47:22.7657Z`, `w3-5/partB-attempt1.txt:19-21`; **an earlier version of this line said "3 m 38 s", inherited verbatim from the W3-5 draft and short by 2.4 s**), against the same census's control `Succeeded | step 0 | Succeeded | exit=0`. W2-2 already owns this defect, is **not** marked FIXED, and states it for "every plainly-cancelled run"; its third control (`:599`) is the **same fixture, same route, same observation** (`edge-longrun` cancelled, run `Cancelled`, step `0:tick=Running/end=NULL`) held for **14 m 46 s**, and its Notes (`:610`) already considered and **rejected** the narrow `handleCancelRun` → `MarkRunStepsInterrupted` fix in favour of terminalising steps inside `FinishRun`'s own transaction ("Prefer the funnel"). **The only thing new here is a fourth reproduction, on a controller built from this branch's HEAD** — i.e. *with* PR #113 / `2c018fe` present — confirming that `MarkRunStepsInterrupted` still has **exactly one caller in the tree** (`internal/controller/stuckrun_reaper.go:81`; the only other non-test hits are the store implementation `internal/store/postgres.go:819` and the interface declaration `internal/store/store.go:228`). No severity, no new entry.

### W3-1 (observation): a cache restore torn mid-stream leaves the workspace half-populated, the step is reported `Succeeded`, the next step builds on the debris, and the deferred save then overwrites the good cache entry with it — permanently, with no fault still present

- **Scenario:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-1-cache-delete-vs-restore.md` Part A'. Executed on branch `plan/edge-case-w3`, **`06:44:24Z – 07:03:0xZ`** on 2026-07-31, on `test/ha` + Garage + `mc` + the **S3 interposer** overlay (`compose/s3proxy.override.yaml`), torn down with `down -v` (`w3-1/teardown.txt`). The developer stack (`docker compose ls`, project `unified-cd`) was running and untouched at both ends (`w3-1/gate.txt`, `w3-1/teardown.txt`). **Ten runs, all `Succeeded`, zero non-terminal at teardown — I1 held, with a census** (`w3-1/census.txt`). **Zero controller `ERROR` lines across all three replicas from boot to the census** (`census.txt`, count printed). **The scope word is narrowed deliberately:** `census.txt` is stamped `07:01:18.759Z` while the session ran on to `07:02:54.124Z` (`teardown.txt:1`, `:50`), so the final ~95 s — which held only the interposer arm-clear and `down -v` — are **outside** it. The capture's own heading says "the WHOLE session"; it is the census stamp, not the heading, that bounds every zero taken from that file (see also `:2024`).
- **Invariant:** **I4** is the invariant this scenario was chartered against, quoted verbatim from `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:51`:

  > "**Log/artifact integrity** — a Succeeded run's log line count matches what the workload emitted; no duplicates, no reordering; archives stay readable"

  **and the honest answer is that I4 is NOT contradicted. This entry says so in its Invariant line rather than burying it**, per this branch's rule that an invariant must be contradicted by its own text and not its spirit (`FINDINGS.md:1509`). Clause by clause: *"a Succeeded run's log line count matches what the workload emitted"* — **not touched**; every line the fixture emitted was stored, on every run. *"no duplicates, no reordering"* — **not touched** (W3-4 owns duplication). *"archives stay readable"* — **not touched, and deliberately not stretched**: the cache archive left behind is a perfectly well-formed, readable 1966149-byte tar+zstd; it is simply the *wrong contents*. I4's subject is "Log/**artifact** integrity", and a cache entry is neither a log nor an artifact (artifacts are the `artifacts/<runID>/` prefix, with their own handlers and their own findings above). **W3-5's narrow reading of the same clause is the binding precedent and this entry stays consistent with it.** **I2 was considered and is not reached**: the later step observes half-restored inputs and acts on them, which is the substance of this entry, but I2 is an *at-most-once* bound and a step that runs exactly once on bad input does not violate it. **I7 was considered and is not reached either, and the check was run rather than assumed**: on a torn extract `cache.Restore` returns `(false, err)` (`internal/cache/cache.go:146`) so the agent logs `cache restore error`, **not** `cache hit` — no surface lied. On the *subsequent* poisoned runs it does log `cache hit`, and that statement is *true*: there was a hit; the entry is simply corrupt. **The entry therefore rests on the mechanism and on what the docs do not say, not on a contradicted invariant.**

  **AND THE CAMPAIGN'S INVARIANT SET HAS NO CACHE-INTEGRITY CLAUSE AT ALL. That is a coverage gap, not an absence of defect, and it is flagged here for the W3 checkpoint alongside W3-3's secret-store gap (`:1621`).** I4's subject is "Log/**artifact** integrity" by its own text; the other six are about run terminality (I1), at-most-once execution (I2), lock leaks (I3), bounded recovery (I5), zombie windows (I6) and status/audit consistency (I7). **Nothing in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-edge-case-testing-design.md:48-54` covers the integrity of a cache entry** — which is precisely W3-3's shape, where the missing clause was secret-store integrity. **W3-3 escaped the gap because it had a published-contract limb** (`docs/secrets.md:420`); **this entry has none** — the Expected section below rules the docs *silent*, not contradicted, and that survey was re-derived independently in review — so here the gap is load-bearing.

  **CLASSIFICATION, DECIDED EXPLICITLY RATHER THAN INHERITED FROM AN ABSENT TITLE MARKER: filed as an OBSERVATION, at unchanged `major` severity.** An earlier version carried no marker, which under `:481` ("entries with neither marker are violations") made the wave's tally count it as a violation while the entry's own two limbs disclaimed both of the grounds `:478` gives for one. **The body was never wrong; the tally the checkpoint would have inherited was.** Three reasons for relabelling rather than writing a sentence to defend the violation label: **(i)** `:478`'s violation test is substantive and disjunctive — an invariant **or** a documented contract — and this entry fails both by its own explicit rulings, so calling it a violation would be exactly the stretch `:1509` forbids, merely in the opposite direction from the four stretches that rule was written for. **(ii)** This branch's operative practice already puts "no invariant contradicted **and** docs silent" in the observation bucket, in as many words: W3-5's mixed-batch entry (`:1927`) — "*Recorded as an observation because it is as designed and today unreachable by any agent, not because any invariant was found to be violated*" — and W3-2's `:1757` and `:1810`. **The decisive case is inside this very scenario:** the two W3-1 observations below (`:2004`, `:2015`) are observations on exactly this reasoning, and the remaining W3-1 entry (`:1986`) is a violation *only* because it found a contract limb — leaving this one unmarked would have one scenario classifying its four entries on two different rules. **(iii)** Reclassification has precedent here — W2-2 went minor violation → observation on a documentation-gap argument (`:650`, `:1479`), and `:1716` states the governing test: W2-2's rule applies when the doc gap **is** the whole finding, and it does here, because unlike W3-2's crash-loop entry there is no behavioural invariant limb underneath to carry it. **This is the first `major`-severity observation in this file, and that is deliberate, not an oversight.** Severity and classification are orthogonal axes: the bands at `:6-8` measure impact, while the violation/observation split records whether a **codified** promise was broken. **What the label costs the reader is stated so nobody has to reconstruct it — an observation is easier to skip and this one should not be skipped. The missing invariant clause is the only reason it is filed this way, and adding a cache-integrity clause would make it a violation with no new evidence.**
- **Severity:** **major (observation) — argued against this file's own bands (`:6-8`) rather than defaulted, and argued *down* from critical on precedent consistency.** The `critical` band is "data loss / silent corruption / security", and on its text the "silent corruption" disjunct is arguably met: this is silent corruption of persistent state that survives the fault. **What holds it at the top of `major` is calibration against the rest of this file — the argument W3-4 made for itself at `:1562`, and the one this entry now uses in place of a weaker regenerability reading.** This file has filed **zero** criticals across W0-W3, and three entries on this branch are permanent, silent losses of *actual product data* filed **major**: **W1-2** (`:179` — a step's unflushed log tail dropped forever, no marker), **W3-4** (`:1521`, severity at `:1523` — a `Succeeded` run's stored log permanently duplicated and reordered, 2002 emitted lines stored and served as 8493) and **W3-5** (`:1878`, severity at `:1886` — a controller-terminalised run's entire log backlog accepted with a `204` and discarded). **Corruption of a regenerable cache cannot outrank those at a higher band without re-opening all three**, and that is a file-wide recalibration rather than a call this entry gets to make alone. **The withdrawn argument, named so it is not re-derived:** an earlier draft argued the severity down *from regenerability* — that the object is a cache and not a system of record. That does not survive this entry's own evidence, because **regenerability is the thing the defect defeats**: nothing in the product ever regenerates the entry (each poisoned run hits it and re-saves the poison), and clearing it takes a key change or an out-of-band delete. What does still hold, and is folded into the calibration above rather than offered as its own reason, is that **no product data** (runs, logs, artifacts, secrets, audit) is touched — the census shows ten clean `Succeeded` runs and zero `ERROR` lines while this was happening. **A reader who weighs the fifth Expected bullet below — the docs' own canonical cache example is exactly the pattern this breaks — may reasonably read it as critical, and that reading is not unreasonable.**
- **Observed:**
  - **The mechanism, live, first attempt.** Run `45a8c8bc` (`edge-cache-torn`, agent2, `w3-1/partA-torn.txt`). The restore's GET was throttled to 262144 B/s through the interposer so the 16909275-byte archive was a ~64 s stream — **and that ~64 s is IMPORTED FROM A DIFFERENT REQUEST, which `:1984` (iii) establishes but this sentence did not carry**: the severed GET has **no interposer access-log line at all** (nginx's fast shutdown discards in-flight requests before logging them), so the figure is attempt 1's `rt=64.359` for the same object at the same armed rate (`w3-1/partA-attempt1-objects.txt:15`), not a measurement of this stream. The agent's workspace `deps/` was sampled at a **~2.11 s** cadence — **not "once per second", as an earlier version of this bullet said**: the capture's own stamps are `06:59:04.129 → :06.243 → :08.365`, i.e. 2.114 s and 2.122 s apart, and `partA-attempt1.txt:4-39` holds the same ~2.1 s spacing across its whole sample run (`docker compose exec` costs ~200-400 ms, which is why 1 Hz is only the rig's *nominal* floor) — and the stream was severed at the third sample, on **two consecutive rising counts** — 6 → 14 → 22 entries — not on a sleep. **The load-bearing claim is the two rising counts, and it is unaffected by the cadence:**

    | instant | event | source |
    |---|---|---|
    | `06:59:02.375Z` | `wipe` removes `deps/`; `deps-present=no` | run log, `partA-torn.txt` |
    | `06:59:04.129 / :06.243 / :08.365Z` | extract in flight: **6 → 14 → 22** entries on disk | `partA-torn.txt` |
    | `06:59:08.387Z` | **sever**: `docker compose restart -t 1 s3proxy` (SIGTERM = nginx fast shutdown = in-flight connections closed) | `partA-torn.txt` |
    | `06:59:09.863310565Z` | agent2 logs `WARN "cache restore error" step=restore_deps error="extract: unexpected EOF"` | agent2 container log, `partA-torn.txt` |
    | `06:59:09.873Z` | **the very next step starts and reads the debris**: `STATE entries=30 complete=no bytes=1950208` | run log |
    | `06:59:11.193694371Z` | agent2 logs `INFO "cache saved" key=edge-cache-torn-v1` | agent2 container log |
    | `06:59:11.782Z` | run terminal = **`Succeeded`** | `partA-torn.txt` |

  - **The step did not fail, and that is the load-bearing correction to this wave's plan.** `step_reports` for run `45a8c8bc`: `0|wipe|Succeeded|0`, **`1|restore_deps|Succeeded|0`**, `2|inspect_deps|Succeeded|0`. The plan's facts block (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-30-edge-case-campaign-w3.md:61`) predicted "step fails → `markFailed` (`orchestrator.go:390`)". It does not. The cache path is lenient by **explicit written policy** — `internal/agent/orchestrator.go:969-971`: "Cache stays warn+skip on error (lenient policy): a restore/save problem should not fail the step, unlike artifact upload/download" — and `:972-973` logs the warning without returning, so `executeCacheStep` falls through to `ReportStep(..., "Succeeded")` at `:1004-1006`. `markFailed` is reachable only from the three error returns at `:923`, `:927` and `:960` (key template, path template, rejected path), none of which is a restore failure.
  - **The workspace was left partially populated, and the partiality is exact, not impressionistic.** 30 of 257 entries; 1950208 of 16777241 bytes; `zz-COMPLETE` (the sentinel that sorts last in the tar) **absent**; and **`f0030` truncated mid-write at 49664 bytes against a uniform 65536** — `extract`'s `io.Copy` (`cache.go:305`) was interrupted *inside* a file, not between files. **Where the 65536 comparator comes from is stated rather than assumed, because it is not a recorded fixture property:** `w3-1/fixture-parse.txt` records only the DSL parse (job, three steps, the `cache:` block) and **no file sizes at all**; the 65536 is read off `w3-1/partA-workspace.txt:11` (`65536 … deps/f0029`) — i.e. off the **poisoned** workspace on the *other* agent described two sentences below. The inference is sound (the fixture writes uniform files and `f0029` is the last untruncated one), but it is **debris used as the yardstick for debris**, and no capture in this scenario measures the pristine fixture. **Those four numbers are `inspect_deps`' own output, measured by the next step on the machine that tore it** (`partA-torn.txt`). **The direct on-agent corroboration is real but is of the *poisoned* restore, not of this one, and the capture says which:** `w3-1/partA-workspace.txt` was taken at `07:01:41.107Z` on **agent1**, in `/working0/edge-cache-torn/deps` after the last poisoned run `6f7aab7a` — 30 files, `f0029 = 65536`, `f0030 = 49664`, no `zz-COMPLETE`. **Same debris shape, different agent, a later run.** agent2's own torn workspace was never sampled directly by `ls`; that the two agree file-for-file is the poisoning bullet's point below, not a second measurement of this one.
  - **The next step read the debris and acted on it.** `inspect_deps` printed `CACHE-PRESENT complete=no` / `FIRST=f0001 LAST=f0030` / `SHORT-FILE deps/f0030 49664` and, because `deps/` was non-empty, **did not regenerate**. That is the whole point of the fixture's `if [ "$n" -eq 0 ]` guard, and it is not a contrivance — see the docs bullet below.
  - **THE DEFERRED SAVE THEN OVERWROTE THE GOOD CACHE ENTRY WITH THE DEBRIS, AND THE DAMAGE IS PERMANENT.** `orchestrator.go:984-1001` queues the save as a post-hook that archives whatever is on disk at the end of the run, under the same key, unconditionally. Measured at the object store (`partA-torn.txt` — **which carries the *after* values only; the *before* `"size":16909275` is not in that capture** and is cited from `w3-1/partC-expired-meta.txt:38` and `w3-1/gate-g5-key.txt:7`): `…03wr97BXtIVjub_36j2JVjtDszd6PUiA4U5vmgdzcZY.tar.zst` went from **16 MiB to 1.9 MiB**, and its `.meta` from `"size":16909275` to **`"size":1966149`** with a fresh `"expiresAt":"2026-08-01T06:59:11.191362412Z"`. **Then, with every interposer arm cleared and the clear confirmed** (`inject.sh s3-show` → `(unarmed)`, `w3-1/partA-poison.txt`), two further completely unfaulted runs — `ae96d3c4` at `06:59:47` and `6f7aab7a` at `06:59:52`, both on **agent1**, i.e. not even the agent that tore it. **`ae96d3c4` logged `INFO "cache hit"`** — captured verbatim, agent1, `06:59:47.038289352Z` (`partA-poison.txt:35`). **`6f7aab7a`'s hit line was never captured**: the capture holds that run's step output and nothing from the agent's container log, so **no evidence here says it logged a hit, and an earlier version of this bullet said "both logged" it.** What carries `6f7aab7a` instead is its own `STATE entries=30 complete=no bytes=1950208` and `SHORT-FILE deps/f0030 49664` (`partA-poison.txt:41`, `:44`) and its `Succeeded` census row (`census.txt:12`) — which is the substance the bullet needs either way. **Both restored 30 files with `f0030` at 49664 bytes and no `zz-COMPLETE`, and both reported `Succeeded`.** **The corruption crossed agents, survived the fault being removed, and is self-perpetuating** (each poisoned run re-saves the poison).
  - **The operator-visible surface for all of this is ONE line in one agent container's log.** `WARN "cache restore error"` on agent2. The run is `Succeeded`; every step is `Succeeded`; there is no run log line about it (the agent's own `slog` does not go into the run's `logs`); nothing in Postgres records it; the controller logged **zero** `ERROR` lines all session; and on the two subsequent poisoned runs there is not even a `WARN` — the agent correctly logs `cache hit`.
- **Expected:** **the leniency is DOCUMENTED AND SANCTIONED, and this entry leads with that rather than burying it.** `docs/jobs.md:1399`: "Restore is best-effort (a miss or error never fails the step); save is deferred until the run's main stages complete." `docs/kubernetes-integration.md:446`: "**Cache** is best-effort: a `cache:` step restores at step time if a matching key exists, but a miss or restore error never fails the step. The matching save is deferred until the end of the run … and is also best-effort — a save error is logged but never fails the run." **So "the step did not fail" is not the defect and must not be filed as one.** What is undocumented is everything downstream of it: that a restore *error* (as opposed to a miss) leaves debris on disk, that the next step sees it, and that the save then writes the debris back over the entry.
  - **The full docs survey, untruncated, hit counts printed** (`w3-1/partE-docs-survey.txt`, the W3-3 method rule): `cache` → **97**, `ttl|expir` → **33**, `cleanup|garbage|sweep|reclaim|orphan` → **87**, `partial|corrupt|atomic|all-or-nothing|torn` → **11**. **Not one of the 11 concerns a cache restore.** On this limb the docs are **silent, not contradicted**: nothing says a restore is all-or-nothing, nothing says the workspace is cleaned up after a failed restore, and nothing says a failed restore suppresses the save.
  - **`docs/jobs.md:1397` was read in full before being weighed and is NOT cited as contradicted.** "On hit, the cached directory is restored before the step runs. On miss, the directory is saved after the run completes." It describes the hit and the miss cases; a torn restore is neither. An omission is not a contradiction — `FINDINGS.md:1688` is this branch's binding precedent on exactly that move, and this entry follows it.
  - **THE SHARPEST THING IN THE DOCS IS NOT A PROMISE — IT IS THE EXAMPLE.** `docs/jobs.md:1386-1390`, inside the canonical `cache:` example the whole Cache section is built around, is a `download-deps` step whose entire body is `if [ ! -d vendor ]; then go mod vendor; fi`. **A torn restore leaves `vendor/` present-but-incomplete, so `[ ! -d vendor ]` is false, `go mod vendor` is skipped, and `go build ./...` — the next step of that same example — runs against a truncated dependency tree.** The fixture's `if [ "$n" -eq 0 ]` guard is a transcription of this idiom, not an invention. **This is not a contract limb — an example is not a promise** — but it is why the severity argument above is close.
  - **Already-ruled check, run before filing** (`w3-1/partE-already-ruled.txt`). `docs/jobs.md:1397`, `:1399`, `:1403`, `:1405`, `:1407` and `docs/kubernetes-integration.md:440`, `:446` are cited **nowhere** on this branch outside this scenario's own runbook. Per the W3-5 lesson the *finding* was greped too, not just the doc text: `grep -in cache` over `FINDINGS.md` as it stood before this scenario returns **21** lines. **The count and the conclusion below are both right; an earlier version's three-way characterisation of those 21 was not, and it is corrected here rather than quietly restated.** Broken down in full: **twelve are unrelated word matches** — W1-6's credential-cache family (`:364`, `:369`, `:397`, `:460`, `:463`, `:494`), pgx's `stmtcache` log form (`:531`, `:1378`, `:1514`), a reaper's cached SELECT answer (`:808`), `agentRunGuard`'s `claimedBy` memo (`:1175`), and W0's forward-looking "ready for W3's cache/restore-race scenarios" (`:157`). **Four are cache-adjacent but rule on something else** — `docs/operations.md:14` and `docs/configuration.md:452` quoted inside W3-2's artifact entries (`:1757`, `:1810`), W3-2's `unified-cd-cache/caches/` **control** probe (`:1799`), and W3-2's naming of the cache cleaner as one of the two goroutines the object store gates (`:1734`). **Five are the real prior art, and not one of them rules on cache behaviour** — W2-1's "goroutine never started" null result (`:581-582`), the W2 checkpoint's instructions to W3-1 (`:1497`), and W3-6's `List`-call-site enumeration, stated twice (`:1836`, `:1873`). *(`:1503` was cited alongside `:1497` in the earlier version; it matches `DeleteExpired`, not `cache`, and is not one of the 21.)* **One absolute is also corrected:** "zero entry headings mentioning cache" is false to a literal grep — `:364` is a `##` heading and contains "cached-**token**". The statement that survives is the one the entry actually needs: **zero entries are *about* a cache, and no prior entry on this branch rules on `internal/cache/` at all** — `:1836`/`:1873` cite `cache.go:182`/`:223` only as two of five object-store `List` call sites inside a run-retention finding.
- **Notes:**
  - **THE SCENARIO'S CHARTERED MECHANISM DOES NOT EXIST, AND THE BACKEND SAYS SO. This is the wave's clearest "the plan's window is unreachable" result and it is not softened here.** W3-1 was chartered as a TTL-expiry-vs-restore race; the plan corrected it to a delete-vs-restore race and listed "**what S3 does to an in-flight GET whose key is deleted is NOT established** — measure it" (`plan:61`, `:139`). **Measured, and the answer is that it does nothing.** Stated as a **fact about the backend**, one version, one configuration, and deliberately not generalised to S3, AWS or MinIO:

    > **Garage v2.3.0 (`dxflrs/garage:v2.3.0`, `--single-node --default-bucket`): an object `DELETE` issued while a `GET` of the same key is streaming does NOT terminate the in-flight response. The reader received the complete object.**

    Two independent measurements. **(i) Pure backend — no unified-cd and no proxy in the path** (`w3-1/partB-backend.txt`): `mc cp --limit-download 256KiB` direct to `garage:3900`, a 63 s / 260.04 KiB/s transfer of a 16909279-byte object; `mc rm` of the same key at `06:54:50.551Z`, **8.3 s in**, returned success and reported a `versionId`; an `mc stat` issued **32 ms later** returned "Object does not exist", so the delete was immediately effective **for new requests**; the in-flight `cp` nevertheless exited **0** with **16909279 bytes**, the full object. **(ii) Through the product** (`w3-1/partA-attempt1.txt`, `partA-attempt1-objects.txt`): run `ef7631ab` on agent2, `mc rm` at `06:52:06.285Z` with **20 of 257** entries extracted; the interposer's own access line for that GET reads `rt=64.359 … bytes=16909275`, completing at `06:53:05.497Z` — **every byte delivered** — the agent logged `cache hit`, and `inspect_deps` reported `entries=257 complete=yes SHORT-FILES=0`. **So the delete-vs-restore race is real in the code and unreachable against this backend**, and Part A' produced the torn extract by severing the connection instead. **The `extract` failure path is identical either way** (`cache.go:286`/`:305` → `:146`), but this entry says plainly which cause it used.
  - **A second, free result from (ii), worth its own sentence:** the deferred save at `06:53:06` **re-created the object the delete had just removed**, with a fresh `expiresAt`. So a cache-cleanup delete that lands during a run of the job that owns the entry is silently undone by that run's own save — and because the save runs on a **hit** as well as a miss (`orchestrator.go:984-1001` is unconditional), an entry in continuous use has its TTL refreshed on every single run and can never expire. Both halves are code-read plus this one measured instance.
  - **Three cheapest fixes, with the counter-argument to each, because "fail the step" is not available** (the docs sanction the leniency and removing it is a breaking behaviour change). **(a) Do not save after a failed restore** — one boolean threaded from `CacheRestore` into the post-hook closure; it stops the poisoning entirely and changes nothing else. *Counter-argument:* it also suppresses the legitimate case where the step *repaired* the directory itself, which is exactly what the docs' `if [ ! -d vendor ]` example does on a miss — so it must key on the *error* path only, never the miss path. **(b) Extract to a temp dir and rename over the target**, making a torn restore atomically a miss. *Counter-argument:* a second full copy on the same filesystem (or a non-atomic cross-mount rename), and it changes the semantics of restoring *into* a directory that already has content. **(c) At minimum, delete what was extracted when `extract` fails** — cheapest, strictly better than today. *Counter-argument:* it silently converts a partial restore into a miss, so it needs a louder record than today's `WARN` — and that record should reach the **run's own log**, not only the agent's container log, which is the diagnosability half of this entry and where an operator would actually look. **(a) closes the defect; (c) could ship today.**
  - **What this entry did NOT establish, stated explicitly.** (i) **No arm produced a torn restore from a delete** — see the backend fact above; the torn extract was produced by severing the connection at the interposer and every claim about it says so.

    **The sever is therefore a STAND-IN, and `:1516`'s discipline is owed in full rather than in the two-thirds an earlier version paid.** It is **not** a reproduction of any specific production incident, and nothing here is a frequency claim. **The ordinary faults that reach the identical state are enumerated from `extract`'s own error returns** (`internal/cache/cache.go:280-311`), every one of which returns *after* files are already on disk and none of which cleans up: **(1) the GET body ending early** — an object-store, proxy or LB restart/crash, a TCP reset, a read or idle timeout, a partition mid-stream: the class this arm injected (nginx fast shutdown), surfacing at `tar next` (`:286`) or `io.Copy` (`:305`) as `unexpected EOF`; **(2) a write-side failure on the agent host** — `os.Create` (`:301`) or `io.Copy` (`:305`) failing on ENOSPC, EDQUOT, or a read-only/unmounted workspace, which reaches the same returns with a partial tree already written; **(3) a stored archive that is itself truncated or corrupt**, failing at `tar next` — and note this scenario *manufactures* that input, so a poisoned entry is a producer of the same fault for the next agent that reads it; **(4) a path-escape rejection** (`:290`) part-way through an archive. **How often any of these happens in production is NOT measured** — not by this scenario and nowhere in this campaign. What *is* measured is that the state is reachable, that one ordinary transport fault reaches it, and what the product does once it is reached. The arm's effect is evidenced from inside (the **~2.11 s** workspace samples — see the corrected cadence at `:1952`, which an earlier version of both bullets called per-second — and the agent's own `WARN`, not a wall clock), and the armed window is bounded and stated: every interposer arm was cleared and the clear confirmed (`s3-show` → `(unarmed)`) before the two poison-confirmation runs, so everything after `06:59:11` is uninjected. (ii) **`DeleteExpired` never ran**: the cleanup ticker's first fire is at t+24 h (separate entry below) and this session was ~16 minutes; `grep -ci "cache cleanup"` over all three controllers' complete logs returns **0** (`w3-1/census.txt`). **That is an absence over a window this runbook ended, not a "never".** (iii) The interposer has **no access-log line for the severed GET** — nginx's fast shutdown discards in-flight requests before logging them — so that request is bracketed by the agent's `WARN` and the workspace samples, **not** by a proxy capture, and nothing here is presented as a bracket that is not one.

### W3-1: a cache payload whose `.meta` is missing is invisible to both `.meta`-iterating consumers, has no expiry any code can read, and is **still served** on every exact-key restore — contradicting the docs' storage-bound promise

- **Scenario:** `test/edgecase/scenarios/w3-1-cache-delete-vs-restore.md` Part C1. Same session and stack as the entry above.
- **Invariant:** **none is contradicted.** I4 does not reach a cache entry (argued clause by clause in the entry above, not repeated). **This entry is filed on the documented-contract limb**, which the campaign's rule admits on its own (`FINDINGS.md:479`: "an exported API field, a schema column, or a statement in `docs/`").
